The console's newer write landed in the same memory, so the older content is gone there too. It comes back filled from its storage where `tex_init_fill` can, else zero. Half: layouts are padded, and an image another one only clips at its edge kept its content.
  - **Clean:** an image whose content is still its storage's (a packaged texture, or a shared one filled from its storage) that the GPU never wrote and the texture pool by ID never read, after `DM_VK_IMAGE_EVICT_AGE` presents without a lookup (default 600). It comes back uploaded or filled as it was made. The pool by ID is left out because a view dropped from its set reads 0 until the sync after a draw reads its ID again.
  - `DM_VK_IMAGE_EVICT=0` keeps every image as before. The memory line every 300 frames gives the images alive and what went (`[vk] image memory: ...`); `DM_VK_IMAGE_EVICT_LOG=1` adds a line at each scan.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_img_evict.py` builds `img_evict.h` and checks both rules and the sweep over a pool's images. On the Fold (Adreno 840, Android 16), Ocarina of Time with raw global memory and the upscaler replacement on, Saria's scene from the same save, 2026-11-11:
  - Without: 2,340 images, 2,229 MB at frame 2,700; GL mtrack 4.35 GB, 11.05 GB of PSS.
  - With: image memory 1.36-1.6 GB through the scene, 1.95 GB for about 5 s at a camera cut until the next scan; GL mtrack 3.5-3.8 GB, 10.0-10.5 GB of PSS. By frame 2,700, 974 overwritten images (1,133 MB) and 322 clean ones (79 MB) went, and 241 came back. The scene runs at the same 16-18 fps, and its shots look as before (Saria, Link and Navi lit, no black or missing textures).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Switch 2).
- **Open:** the Fold still ends the game in the scene, at 10.4-10.9 GB: what is left (raw global memory's 1.18 GiB of placed pools, a 290 MB 12288x12288 D16 depth target drawn every frame, the game's 4.4 GB in compressed swap) leaves no room, and a camera cut holds both shots' targets (+0.5 GB) until the next scan.

### A texture ID with no texture kept its cached view

- **Symptom:** a command buffer fails with a GPU page fault on the same frames in every run, in a compute or draw command buffer whose shaders sample through the texture pool. Ocarina of Time with `DM_NVN_RT_RECLAIM=1` (PR #1588): f3,677 and f4,851, compute shader 9e6b00b050e30801, each run.
- **Cause:** `tex_by_id` (`nvn_mtl.m`) keeps an ID's cached view when the slow path finds the same texture behind it: `cur == v || v.parentTexture == cur`. When the ID has no texture now, `treg_tex` returns nil as `cur`. That happens for a texture that went and was not made again, a texture that could not be made, or a reclaimed render target. A cached view that is the base texture itself has no parent either, so nil == nil kept it and marked it valid again. The pool tables' fast path (`tex_fast_rid`) then wrote its resource ID after the host had released the texture and dropped its residency. With the reclaim it happened about 60 times in a 5,000-frame run.
- **Fix:** general, in `tex_by_id`: the cached view is kept only when `cur` is non-nil. An ID with no texture drops its view, and its slot takes the typed zero texture an empty slot of that type gets.
- **Check:** Ocarina of Time on an M2 Pro 16 GB with `DM_NVN_RT_RECLAIM=1`, to f5,241 and f7,750. The f3,677 and f4,851 faults are gone, with no trimmed texture handed back (checked by marking every texture the reclaim's trim released). Pace is the same (7,750 frames in 202.5 s against 7,731 in 202.75 s), and reclaim counts are alike. A fault at f786 has another cause and is there without the reclaim too. The picture there was not compared: a dead ID now reads the typed zero texture instead of the released one.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Switch 2, `0400486024DEC000`, v131072).

### Live resolution variants share physical image storage

- **Symptom:** GPU memory grows as resolution changes or rooms load, although almost every retained texture still has a live NVN descriptor; reclaiming unused sharing-table entries cannot recover it.
- **Cause:** an NVN texture object describes storage in a pool. Several live descriptions at the same pool offset can describe the same physical memory at different resolutions, but `(pool, offset, description)` sharing allocated a separate private Metal texture for every size. The live-object guard in `alias_take_over` correctly kept those descriptors, and consequently kept all their independent allocations.
- **Fix:** `image_texture` in `nvn_mtl.m` gives a declared resolution family an exact-sized placement heap. Eligibility is GPU-only, mapped, non-sparse storage, `COMPRESSIBLE` without `DISPLAY`, `VIDEO_DECODE`, `LINEAR`, `SPARSE` or `LINEAR_RENDER_TARGET` (with or without `IMAGE`: a depth target carries only `COMPRESSIBLE`), single-level, single-sample 2D images. Sharing stays within one NVN pool, offset and format. Live descriptions are recorded at initialization so the first used resolution can allocate the largest declared requirement; a single-description address retains its ordinary allocation. No power-of-two capacity or extra pixel-format-view usage is imposed. A fresh heap is zeroed through its largest description; creating a later alias never queues a zero fill over another alias's content. Metal 4 residency names the heap, and encoder boundaries include resource-alias visibility while these heaps exist. Each texture holds its heap owner; normal sharing-table eviction and deferred texture release retire the allocation after its last texture/view goes, without a pool-lifetime heap cache.
- **Limits:** this handles initialized resolution families, not arbitrary overlapping offsets, cross-format inheritance, CPU-visible or sparse storage. If a larger description is declared later and cannot fit an existing heap, it gets a new exact allocation; old heaps follow their existing textures' lifetimes. Inherited texels are not translated between incompatible texture layouts, and the family's textures are not compressed ([the entry below](#a-resolution-family-member-reads-another-sizes-compressed-blocks-as-garbage)). No game-specific profile switch is used.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0100DCA0064A6000 check_t_image_alias` runs under Metal validation with direct and deferred encoding: 128 live descriptions retain their individual dimensions while sharing one allocation; small-first fresh storage, literal pixels across resolution changes, descriptor creation after a writer, indirect Metal 4 argument-buffer access, native integer-image atomics, depth/stencil clears and stencil views, pool/offset/format separation, excluded CPU-visible/sparse/linear images, a depth family with `COMPRESSIBLE` alone and repeated destruction are checked. Integer-image matching restores the `ShaderAtomic` usage that Metal omits from its queried usage flags.
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md#texture-memory-2026-10-27), investigated from [#257](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/257) and [#100](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/100). The game's note records the live-allocation census and Mac comparison; other games and long iOS sessions remain unvalidated. [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): its dynamic-resolution depth buffer, Depth32Float with `COMPRESSIBLE` alone in a virtual pool, had 15–16 live sizes of 1,882–1,920 pixels at one offset, 14.5 MB each, outside the family until the flags rule took any compressible image; in Kokiri Forest on an M3 Pro (18 GB, measured before main's Ocarina fixes) Depth32Float textures went from 95 (458 MB) to 60 (191 MB) plus 21 family heaps (82 MB), and GPU allocation from 7,710 to 7,520 MB.

### A resolution family member reads another size's compressed blocks as garbage

- **Symptom:** after the game renders at another resolution for a while (a pause menu, a resolution switch) and comes back, a render target it loads instead of clearing holds non-finite or random pixels, in a screen-shaped region that opaque draws wipe away and transparent ones keep; bloom spreads them into a strobing glow. A non-finite scan (`DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`) counts about a million of them in the first pass back.
- **Cause:** [a resolution family](#live-resolution-variants-share-physical-image-storage) places every size in one Metal heap. Metal compresses a private texture losslessly by default (`allowGPUOptimizedContents`), and each texture reads the shared bytes through its own compression layout. When one size reads what another size wrote, it decodes the other's compressed blocks as garbage. On the console the same bytes are texels of the same format in other places, which stay finite.
- **Fix:** `image_texture` in `nvn_mtl.m` creates every family member uncompressed, and so the largest description that zeroes a new heap: inherited bytes read back as the other size's texels, misplaced, as on the console. Storage with one description keeps its ordinary, compressed allocation. `DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=0` turns compression back on (an A/B of its cost).
- **Cost:** Ocarina of Time makes 215 family heaps in the Kokiri Forest (202 of them Depth32F). On its `kokiri-deck` route on an M4 Pro, one host with the switch on and off, the median GPU time a frame over the runs that stayed on the route was 22.07 ms compressed (4 runs, 21.94–22.23) and 22.38 ms uncompressed (5 runs, 21.73–22.73): at most about 0.3 ms, within the spread of the runs.
- **Check:** `check_t_image_alias` (above) clears a 1440x810 RGBA16F render target through the host's clear path and reads the 1600x900 size of its family back: every texel is the clear colour or zero. With compression (`DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=0`) 6,144 of 1,440,000 texels were neither on an M4 Pro.
- **Found on:** [ASTRAL CHAIN](../../dm/games/01007300020FA000/README.md) (1.0.1): the 1600x900 HDR targets (RG11B10Float) share their storage with the pause menu's 1440x810 targets; the first pass back from the menu loaded 1,115,523 of 1,440,000 non-finite pixels, which the game never clears (issue [#1935](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1935), PR [#1936](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1936)).

### Render targets that move with the dynamic resolution get new Metal memory each time

- **Symptom:** while a game's dynamic resolution moves, the residency set's `added` count in the `residency set up to frame` lines stays at hundreds of MB per 300 frames from the shared-texture site, and frames stutter. On a 16 GB Mac, wired memory and swap follow, with p95 frame times near 100 ms. Ocarina of Time after 04419bc2, which freed its dynamic resolution from its floor (GitHub issue #1831).
- **Cause:** the engine keeps its render targets in a GPU-only pool and gives them new offsets and sizes as its resolution changes. Each `(pool, offset, description)` is a Metal texture of its own (the sharing table), and the storage takeover (`alias_take_over`) lets as many go. On an M6, 3,468 textures were made in about 1,000 frames of Ocarina's village: 97% were in its 305 MB virtual pool, at 1,965 offsets and 289 sizes, and they added 450-575 MB to the residency set every 300 frames. The live total stayed bounded at about 2.1 GB: it is churn, not a leak. Metal does not lay a texture out as NVN does, so these targets cannot share the pool's memory as on the console. A placement heap that mirrors the pool (PR #1453) and a reclaim of targets dead by overwrite (PR #1588) both corrupted the picture: the game keeps live targets in the part of the full-size scene target that its current resolution leaves unused.
- **Fix:** general, `rh_texture` in `nvn_mtl.m`. Such a target (a `COMPRESSIBLE` texture in a `CPU_NO_ACCESS` pool, private storage, not display, video, linear or sparse, and not a resolution family's heap, see the entry above) is made in a placement heap of its own. When its texture is freed, which happens after the submissions that used it complete (`defer_drain`), the heap stays resident in a free list. The next target that fits, up to an eighth larger, is placed in it. Metal's allocation sizes come in steps, so neighbouring resolutions share a size (#1583's `rt_sizes`: 855 resolutions at 2048x1152 fall into 21). The new texture is filled as a new allocation is (`tex_init_fill` or zeros), so nothing it reads depends on the reuse. Its memory was the old texture's only up to that texture's completed last use, so no alias flush is added. Heaps that wait 3 s, and the oldest past 256 MB waiting, leave the residency set when a submission begins (`rh_trim`). The memory line counts the heaps made, reused, freed and waiting. `DM_NVN_RT_RECYCLE=0` turns it off, and `ab` alternates it with the `DM_AB` phases. This does not lower the steady total of the live targets, which stays open.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_rt_recycle`: placement at the target's own size, the heap kept resident while it waits, reuse at a nearby size with zeroed contents, a new heap for a size the waiting ones cannot hold, CPU-visible pools and textures without `COMPRESSIBLE` kept apart, the trim, and the switch off. It runs with direct and deferred encoding under Metal validation. In the game: Ocarina of Time on an M6 (16 GB), the village walk with dynamic resolution on and `DM_NVN_RT_RECYCLE=ab DM_AB=300`, one run. Over the phases while its resolution moved, the shared-texture site added 455, 457 and 521 MB per 300 frames off, and 317, 37 and 27 MB on. Frame p90 was 33.3 and 49.8 ms off, 16.8 and 17.6 ms on. Phases after the resolution settled measured the same either way. Screenshots of the village were clean, with no GPU errors or red lines. Measured with the shaders of chain f9f01e4f9994, which were on disk.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#memory) (Switch 2, `0400486024DEC000`, v131072), [#1831](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1831).

### Render-target views that change the format

- **Symptom:** the whole picture came out darker and more saturated on the Mac and the iPhone: comparing with a Switch screenshot, every channel at every level equalled the Switch value decoded from sRGB once more (0.55 → 0.26, 0.75 → 0.52).
- **Cause:** Mario's last pass writes the `RGBA8` swapchain texture through an `RGBA8_SRGB` view (`TextureViewSetFormat` 0x38 on a 0x25 texture), so the GPU encodes the linear shader output to sRGB on write. The host used to attach the base texture, which stored the linear values unencoded.
- **Fix:** `attach` now attaches a Metal view of the view's format, so the pipelines get that format too; the view is kept until the submission completes. A format change that is not an sRGB / linear pair needs `MTLTextureUsagePixelFormatView` on the base texture; without it the host draws in the base format and reports `render target view format … cannot be attached`.
- **Present step:** the matching rule: the Switch scans out the stored bytes, so an sRGB-typed swapchain texture (`RGBA8_SRGB` / `BGRA8_SRGB`) is sampled through a non-sRGB view (`present_raw_src`); sampling it directly would decode it once more on screen only, which a `DM_NVN_SHOT` capture (raw bytes) does not show. Mario's swapchain is `RGBA8`, so this path is for other games (`t_present_crop` covers it).
- **Check:** `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` prints these views as `V <pass> c<i> view format …`; Mario uses only this one (1 of 80 passes in a course frame).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Textures of an sRGB / linear pair over the same storage

- **Symptom:** a movie (or another picture the game converts each frame) does not show: the screen keeps the last 3D frame while the decoder delivers every picture. A pass dump shows a pass writing one serial and the composite sampling another serial of the same size, the two Metal formats `RGBA8Unorm` (70) and `RGBA8Unorm_sRGB` (71).
- **Cause:** the game makes two NVN texture objects over the same pool and offset with the same description but for the format, `RGBA8` (0x25) and `RGBA8_SRGB` (0x38): it renders into the linear one and samples the sRGB one, which on NVN share their bytes. The storage-sharing table is keyed by pool, offset and the whole description ([Transient render targets shared by storage](#transient-render-targets-shared-by-storage)), so each got its own Metal texture and the sampled one never changed.
- **Fix:** `tex_mtl_` (`nvn_mtl.m`): a texture that misses the table, but whose description differs from a shared texture's only by the other format of an sRGB / linear pair (`g_pfv_srgb`, the pairs Metal views without `PixelFormatView` usage), is a Metal view of that texture in its own format (2D and 2D array, single-sample). Other format changes keep their own texture. A usage upgrade (`tex_upgrade`) asked through the view upgrades its parent and makes the view again on it; a view whose parent left the sharing table (upgraded through its own serial, taken over, evicted) is dropped at its next lookup and made again on what holds the storage now (log line `stale sRGB / linear view dropped`).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_srgb_alias` (Metal validation): the sRGB texture is a view of the linear one, pixels written through the linear texture read back through the sRGB one, an `R32F` texture over the same storage keeps its own; after the linear texture is upgraded the view reads the upgraded storage, and an upgrade through the view upgrades the parent.
- **Found on:** Mario Kart World (`0400C3F00006E000`; its profile is a separate pull request): the Grand Prix's cup opening movie (2560x1440: the YUV pass writes ser 31381 `RGBA8`, the composite samples ser 31382 `RGBA8_SRGB`, both at offset 0 of a 15 MB pool).

### Render-target use added on demand

- **Symptom:** a clear of, or a format-converting copy into, a texture made without render-target use is declined.
- **Cause:** a whole-level clear and `copy_t2t_draw` (which renders into the destination, [Copies a blit cannot do, drawn](#copies-a-blit-cannot-do-drawn)) need a texture with render-target use.
- **Fix:** the texture is upgraded to render-target use first, as format views are upgraded (`tex_upgrade`; [Integer views for images a shader declares uint or int](#integer-views-for-images-a-shader-declares-uint-or-int)).
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (the clear of a 32x80 packaged texture); [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md) (a format-converting copy of a 128x128 RGBA8 render target into a packaged RG8 texture, mirrored in Y, as the boat arrives in Rogueport, about frame 10,950: `copy_t2t_draw` declined a destination made without render-target use).

### A texture upgrade while a draw binds its arguments ends the pass

- **Symptom:** `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x794` in `-[AGXG16GFamilyRenderContext_mtlnext drawIndexedPrimitives:…]+0x50`, called from `draw_`, right after the log line `texture upgraded to allow format views` (Metal API validation reports nothing first).
- **Cause:** `draw_` takes its render encoder from `rp_begin_ex`, then binds the program's arguments (`encode_args`). Binding a texture view of another format calls `tex_by_id`, which upgrades a texture made without `PixelFormatView` (`tex_upgrade`); the upgrade copies the old texture through `blit()`, which ends the render pass (`rp_end`). The draw then encoded on that ended encoder: the driver reads the context it released at end of encoding (`ldr x21, [x0, ivar]`, then `[x21 + 0x794]`).
- **Fix:** after `encode_args`, a draw whose pass ended (`g_enc != en`) is encoded again from the start in a new pass; the texture is already upgraded, so the second binding does not end it (a second end would be logged red and the draw skipped).
- **Found on:** [Need for Speed: Hot Pursuit Remastered](../../dm/games/010029B0118E8000/README.md) (a 4096x1024 texture sampled through a view of another format at its first loading screen).

### Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow

- **Symptom:** `render target is a compressed-format texture …`; the pass's draws did nothing (the attachment was left out), or the pipeline-descriptor validation aborts the process.
- **Cause:** a GPU block encoder writes a BC texture through an uncompressed integer view (one texel per block). Metal can neither attach a compressed format nor make an uncompressed view of one.
- **Fix:** such a pass draws into an uncompressed shadow of blocks × blocks in the view's format: the level's (or layer's, or depth plane's) blocks are copied into it through a buffer before the pass and back after it (`bcrt_attach` / `bcrt_flush` in `nvn_mtl.m`; the method comes from PR #11). The shadows stay resident and are reused, and they are kept out of the load-skip and store-elision bookkeeping. `attach`, the pipeline prediction and the sampling views go by the NVN format, so BC textures are never attached or viewed raw. The encoder's output must be declared `uint` ([Integer fragment outputs declared by hand](shaders.md#integer-fragment-outputs-declared-by-hand)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): agl's BC4 block encoder (`8e2cc2671cae6020`) writes a 32×32 RGTC1 (BC4) texture through an `RGBA16UI` view (one texel per 8-byte block); its twin for a 2D source (`93aa5e22a8202409`) failed in an iPhone run; GitHub issue #4 (reported from an iPhone 14 Pro / A16). [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): agl's BC4 block encoder (`0544282c162305db`, the same shader Mario Wonder ships) encodes a 32×32×32 BC4 3D texture one depth plane per pass through an `RGBA16UI` view; a raw dump at frame 1500 of the new-game route: the texture was all zeros before, now all 32 planes hold encoded blocks.
- **Note:** the A16 has no BC formats; the host used to store BC textures decompressed on such GPUs (about 3.9 GB instead of 2.8 GB for BotW, over iOS's limit on a 6 GB iPhone), and that path has since been removed: only GPUs with BC are supported ([GPUs without BC textures are refused at start](#gpus-without-bc-textures-are-refused-at-start)).

### GPUs without BC textures are refused at start

- **Symptom:** on an iPhone before the A17 Pro, the game runs until its first BC texture, then `failed assertion 'Texture Descriptor Validation' … MTLTextureDescriptor has invalid pixelFormat (134).` (or another number from 130 to 153, Metal's BC formats; 134 is `BC3_RGBA`) and `CRASH sig=6`, the backtrace through `tex_mtl`, `tex_by_id` and `encode_args`.
- **Cause:** BC textures are created and uploaded as BC, with no decompression path ([Compressed textures as render targets](#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow), note), and the A16 and older iPhone GPUs have no BC formats (`MTLDevice.supportsBCTextureCompression` false). The format mapping is right; the device cannot make the texture.
- **Fix:** the device is checked before the game starts. The iOS app checks it when Play is pressed (`front_ios_device_check` in `dm/ios/Sources/main.m`, called by `startGame` in `front_ios.m`, or at once in an app without a launcher) and does not start the host on such a GPU: the screen says the device is not supported and names the GPU and the devices that are (iPhone 15 Pro or newer, M1 iPads or newer), and the log has a `[RED] boot:` line. The check makes the process's first Metal device, and Metal reads `MTL_HUD_ENABLED` then, so it runs after the launcher has applied its settings; at launch (`boot_env`, before the launcher), it kept the launcher's **Performance HUD** switch from working ([GitHub issue #840](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/840)). The host's early loading, which makes no device, still starts at launch on such a GPU and is left unused. `mtl_init` (`nvn_mtl.m`) stops every other route the same way, with a `FATAL … has no BC texture formats` line, for the NVN, Vulkan and GL layers alike; the classic-only path of a device without Metal 4 (a virtual Mac's shader capture) is left as it was.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY X/X-2 HD Remaster](../../dm/games/0100BC300CB48000/README.md): [GitHub issue #704](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/704), an iPhone 15 (A16) with another region's copy (`0100B3600AE9A000`): the launcher ran (no BC textures), FFX aborted at its first BC3 texture.

### A depth texture drawn through a colour view

- **Symptom:** the boot aborts in Metal's pipeline validation: `MTLPixelFormatDepth16Unorm is not color renderable`.
- **Cause:** a depth texture drawn through a colour view (a 1024×1024 D16 texture through an R16 view); Metal has no colour view of a depth texture.
- **Fix:** such a pass draws into a colour shadow of the same pixel size, copied in before and back after the pass through a buffer, as a compressed render target is ([Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow](#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow); `shrt_block`; D16 and D32F, whose Metal pixel is the NVN pixel; not D24S8).
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md).

### The same texture attached at two colour slots

- **Symptom:** a G-buffer target holds only scattered squares (a different order for each 32×32 tile).
- **Cause:** on Maxwell the ROP writes only masked channels, so two slots holding the same texture with one at write mask 0 do not interfere. On Apple's TBDR each attachment has its own tile memory and the whole tile is stored at the end of the pass, so the copy stored later (loaded, never written) overwrote the other one's writes.
- **Fix:** a slot whose texture, level and layer repeat an earlier slot is left unattached (`rt_dup_slot`), also in pipeline prediction; a draw that really writes such a slot is reported once (`color slot … holds the same texture as an earlier slot`). Records hold copies of texture objects (`tex_snap`), so slots are compared by serial, not by pointer.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the G-buffer passes attach the albedo and another buffer twice each (c1 = c2, c3 = c4), the duplicate slots with write mask 0; the albedo held only scattered squares.

### Passes with no render target

- **Symptom:** `no render target could get an MTLTexture` (the pass skipped).
- **Cause:** a fragment shader that only stores to a storage buffer draws into a viewport with no colour or depth target set.
- **Fix:** the host opens an attachment-less pass at the viewport's size (`bare_dims` in `nvn_mtl.m`, from PR #11).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): an agl fragment shader draws one triangle into a 1×1 viewport (it samples a 64×64 cube map, and the game reads the result back from the buffer). Compared at the same frames of the new-game route, the cave's rock and stone blocks now get ambient light they lacked, and Zelda's bloom halo in the descent cutscene is gone.

### Layered passes for vertex shaders that write the layer

- **Symptom:** a black world: everything rendered through a 3D LUT built by layered draws comes out black (every plane went to plane 0); `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` shows no `L` line.
- **Cause:** a Metal vertex shader selects the attachment layer per primitive only when the render pass has `renderTargetArrayLength`.
- **Fix:** a draw whose vertex shader writes the layer opens a layered pass (`g_rp_layered` in `draw`, `g_pass.layered` in `rp_begin_ex`): the pass gets every layer the attachments have from their attached one on (3D textures: depth planes), and it is split when the next draw's shader differs. `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` prints `L … layered pass: n layers`.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md): the 3D scene came out black once the opening reached Lumiose. The game builds its 32×32×32 colour grading LUT with `b29f08619f379a83`, one instance per depth plane, each writing its plane through `render_target_array_index`, and tone-maps the frame through that LUT.

### Passes into other layers of one texture level run as one layered pass

- **Symptom:** a frame-time spike every few frames (the Metal HUD's frame-time line jumps by several ms at a regular interval) while the scene stands still; `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` over consecutive frames shows the slow frame with about 130 more render passes, each one draw into a 64x64 to 8x8 face of a cube or array level, and the next pass starting late.
- **Cause:** on Apple GPUs a render pass costs about 35–40 µs whatever it draws (M2 Max, with or without barriers: a Metal 4 microbenchmark of 132 one-draw 64x64 cube-face passes took 5.4 ms, 4.2 ms without barriers). An environment cube map filtered face by face, one pass per face and level, pays that cost once per face.
- **Fix:** the draw that opens a pass looks ahead (`sm_scan`, `sm_plan` in `nvn_mtl.m`): when its only target is one layer of an array or cube texture level and the following targets switch to other layers of the same level with nothing but state, uniform updates and draws between them (no game barrier, clear, copy or dispatch), the pass is opened over those layers (`renderTargetArrayLength`) and `OP_SET_RT` switches layers inside it. Each draw reaches its layer through a vertex amplification view mapping (count 1, `renderTargetArrayIndexOffset`); a pipeline built with the default `maxVertexAmplificationCount` of 1 ignores the mapping (every draw landed in the first layer), so these draws use pipelines built with 2 (`sm_amp_get`, keyed `AMP_MARK`), compiled in the background on first use while the passes stay apart. A draw that samples the attachment or a clear ends the merged pass. `DM_NVN_SLICE_MERGE=0` turns it off; `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` prints `L … slice-merged pass` and `M … goes on in layer n`, and the residency set line counts merged passes and layer switches.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): every tenth frame filters the environment cube map in 132 passes (22 levels of 6 faces); 284 → 173 render passes in that frame, frame p95 13.2 → 11.1 ms at 900p on an M2 Max.
- **Check:** `DM_NVN_TEXDUMP=layers=100:path` dumps the first such frame with and without the switch: the 173 targets matched (105 identical, the rest within 1% RMSE, the scene's rain).
- **Resolved in review of #213:** the first draw now gets the attachment-read check before planning a merged pass. Every later check refuses unknown bindless/dynamic reads (`TEXU`, `TEXD`, `TEXA`, image atomics and their reflection flags), and considers textures sharing the attachment's storage. Slot-only reads cannot prove those draws independent. `t_perf_order` checks all six array layers' pixels and five actual continuations after pipeline warm-up, plus the first-draw and unknown-read refusals. It runs with both encoding modes and merging disabled.

### Compute dispatches between the draws of a pass run ahead of it

- **Symptom:** a GPU-bound frame with many passes on the same targets, one after the other, each preceded by a compute encoder of one dispatch (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`: `compute / copy after pass … dispatches 1` between render passes of the same size); about 4 ms of GPU idle a frame inside command buffers in Metal System Trace.
- **Cause:** Metal cannot dispatch compute inside a render pass, so every dispatch between two draws ends the pass; on Apple GPUs each extra pass and encoder costs its fixed 35–40 µs plus the store and reload of every attachment.
- **Fix:** `cs_hoist_try` (`nvn_mtl.m`, `DM_NVN_CS_HOIST`, on by default, `0` off): when a draw opens a pass, the ops up to the first one that would end the pass anyway are scanned, and each dispatch met on the way runs before the pass when moving it ahead of the draws before it changes nothing they read (no storage buffer they read, no colour attachment of the pass sampled, no image written, the draws write no memory). A dispatch that samples the pass's depth moves only while no draw before it in the pass writes depth, so it sees the same depth. `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` prints `H … dispatch … moved ahead of the pass`; the residency set line counts moved dispatches and why scans stopped.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the NintendoWare VFX GPU particles run one dispatch per emitter between the effect draws of the G-buffer and of the half-resolution passes; 28 → 8 compute encoders and 149 → 132 render passes a frame, frame p50 9.9 → 9.0 ms at 900p on an M2 Max. Its particle collision samples the G-buffer depth, and no draw before those dispatches writes depth (counted: 0 stops).
- **Check:** the cube-filter frame's 173 targets dumped with and without (`DM_NVN_TEXDUMP=layers=100`): every 3D target within 0.7% RMSE; only the 2D overlay of the final picture differed (a loading banner's animation, timed by the wall clock).
- **Resolved in review of #213:** a nonzero game barrier stops the scan. A sample of depth-stencil storage cannot move ahead of either a depth or stencil write. Unknown indexed-indirect ranges and bindless/dynamic draw texture reads refuse hoisting; fixed-slot sampled textures contribute their storage ranges (including linear and buffer-backed textures). Sparse virtual addresses, unknown sizes and overflowing ranges do not prove independence. Compute uniform/storage accesses overlapping attachment storage also refuse hoisting; sampled texture storage is compared by extent, including different offsets into one linear buffer. Texture registration uses its actual unfair lock. These are general dependency checks, without profile rules.
- **Regression:** `t_perf_order` drives the production replay with an independent dispatch (moved exactly once, GPU writes 73), an explicit barrier (not moved), and the scanner with a stencil-only view after a stencil-writing draw. It also checks indirect index ranges, sampled buffer storage, unknown texture reads and aliased attachments. The initial PR failed 13 harness assertions; restoring only its depth-only write guard makes the stencil case fail too. The fixed harness passes under Metal validation in both encoding modes and with optimizations disabled. [BotW gameplay](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#final-synchronization-and-pass-validation-2026-10-26-current) exercises both optimizations on/off; it retains pass savings but does not demonstrate a timing gain in the tested 60 fps workload. [Metroid Dread gameplay](../../dm/games/010093801237C000/README.md#final-gameplay-validation-2026-10-26-current) broadens coverage without activating either optimization.

### Clears of attachments larger than the render pass

- **Symptom:** a scene drawn only in a small square in the top-left corner of a black screen; speckled and blocky patches; a full-screen overlay showing one tile; the texture dump right after the scene pass shows the picture only in the corner, and `DM_NVN_HILITE` on the scene's shaders lights only the corner.
- **Cause:** a Metal render pass covers only the smallest of its attachments, and so does a clear in its load action. A clear folded into a pass whose other attachment is smaller cleared only that corner of the larger target, so later draws failed the depth test against the previous frames' depth everywhere else. NVN clears the whole target.
- **Fix:** `rp_begin_ex_` (`nvn_mtl.m`) clears an attachment larger than the pass in a pass of its own first and loads it in the real one (`C … cleared in a pass of its own` in a pass dump).
- **Found on:** [Bayonetta 2](../../dm/games/01007960049A0000/README.md): every frame starts with a pass whose colour target is 32x32 (then 256x256 ones) beside the 1280x720 depth buffer, and the game clears the depth there; the story's opening narration showed its scene in a 32x32 or 256x256 square of a black screen. [Bayonetta](../../dm/games/010076F0049A2000/README.md) needed nothing more.
- **Not checked:** the fix in other games beyond the unit tests.

### Clears of layered render targets in a pass that is not layered

- **Symptom:** a point light (a lantern, a lighter) lights only one side of the room with straight, sharp edges, and everything else around it is dark as if in shadow; a dump of the shadow cube (DM_CTL `texraw`, every face) shows face 0 drawn and faces 1-5 all zero; more generally, layers above the first of an array, cube or 3D target keep their old or zero content after a clear.
- **Cause:** NVN clears every layer of the render target view that is bound (the whole texture without a layer range). A clear folded into a Metal pass's load action clears only the layers the pass attaches, and a pass that is not layered (`renderTargetArrayLength` unset) attaches one. When the clear lands in such a pass (the vertex capture pass of a geometry shader draw, a lazy clear landed on its own), the other layers kept zero depth from creation, every fragment the later layered pass drew there failed the depth test, and the comparison sampler read them as fully shadowed.
- **Fix:** `rp_begin_ex_` (`nvn_mtl.m`, `att_layers`) clears a cleared attachment whose view covers more layers than a pass that is not layered in a pass of its own, with `renderTargetArrayLength` set to the view's layers, and loads it in the real one, as it does for attachments larger than the pass ([Clears of attachments larger than the render pass](#clears-of-attachments-larger-than-the-render-pass)). A view limited to some layers clears only those. The pass dump prints `C … cleared in a pass of its own (… N layers …)`. `t_clear_layers` runs the production replay on a cube depth target and a 2D array colour target, with and without layer ranges.
- **Found on:** Little Nightmares (`01002FC00412C000`, Unreal Engine 4, a provisional profile): its point lights' one-pass cube shadows (geometry shader `ffc21034b958840b`, which emits each triangle into the six faces) clear the 512x512 cube in the pass that captures the vertex shader's output; only the +X face was cleared, so the lantern's and the lighter's light passed only through that face.
- **Check (2026-11-01):** before rebasing, the full Metal graphics suite and [Breath of the Wild's full Mac route](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#layered-clear-validation-2026-11-01) passed. These local checks used an M4 Pro, macOS 27.2 and Xcode 27.2; both regression revisions used the documented `DM_ALLOW_OTHER_METAL=1` override because the installed Metal compiler was `32023.922` while the then-current lock pinned `32023.921`. After rebasing onto the Xcode 27.2 pin and #730, `t_clear_layers` and the [image-store integration regression](#image-stores-into-an-attachment-of-their-own-pass) pass. [Tears of the Kingdom's runtime comparison](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#mac-routes) remains incomplete; its foreground-controlled repeat and Pokemon Scarlet's remaining checks were stopped at the maintainer's request. The other games and iOS remain unchecked here.

### Lazy clears folded into the next pass

- **Fix:** lazy clears (`DM_NVN_LAZY_CLEAR`) fold clear-only passes into the pass that follows.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the four clear-only passes before the G-buffer fold into its first pass (about 30 MB less loaded and stored a frame at 1600x900).

### A channel mask that covers every channel is a whole clear

- **Symptom:** `texture clear case not implemented … mask 0x1`.
- **Cause:** an R8 render target cleared with channel mask 0x1.
- **Fix:** a mask that covers every channel the format has is a whole clear (`mtl_pf_chmask`).
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY TACTICS - The Ivalice Chronicles](../../dm/games/010038B015560000/README.md).

### Texture clears with partial channel masks or regions

- **Symptom:** `texture clear case not implemented … mask 0x8` while entering a rendered scene.
- **Cause:** `ClearTexture` only handled whole-level clears whose mask covered every channel. Persona 5 Royal clears only alpha on a 1920x1080 RGBA8 target; a load-action clear cannot preserve its RGB channels.
- **Fix:** partial colour texture clears use the existing clear-triangle shaders in a load/store pass, with the requested channel mask and rectangle. The viewport comes from the selected mip, the region selects the slices or depth planes, and the current draw scissor does not affect the operation. Whole-level whole-channel clears keep the load-action path. `t_clear_tex` checks pixel and channel preservation, mip/layer selection, integer values, an empty mask and the whole-clear path under Metal validation.
- **Found on:** [Persona 5 Royal](../../dm/games/01005CA01580E000/README.md), the opening casino.

### Scissored colour clears

- **Cause:** NVN `ClearColor` honours the scissor. Clears are normally folded into the next pass's load action, which only works for a full-target clear; Mario clears sub-rectangles of small targets to zero (pyramid levels packed into 480×272, 256×256 and 64×36 targets).
- **Fix:** a zero clear outside a pass with a partial scissor is now a copy from the shared zero buffer into that rectangle of the attachment's level / slice (`clear_rect_zero`).
- **Check:** `DM_NVN_CLEARLOG=1` logs clears that are still skipped (mid-pass, partial channel mask or non-zero colour with a partial scissor).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Clears inside a pass, with a partial mask or a partial scissor

- **Symptom:** red lines for unimplemented mid-pass, partial-mask and partial-scissor clears (the clears were dropped); `depth-stencil clear with a scissor or partial stencil mask, cleared whole` (logged once).
- **Cause:** NVN clears obey the scissor and the clear's own channel or stencil write mask, and can come between draws, while a Metal load action clears a whole attachment at the start of a pass.
- **Fix:** such clears are drawn as one triangle over the pass in the open render encoder (`clear_color_tri` / `clear_ds_tri` in `nvn_mtl.m`, adapted from MacNativeGames, MIT). The pipeline has the pass's formats, the clear's mask as the write mask of its slot and no writes to the other slots. Depth is `ALWAYS` (and written for a depth clear), stencil `REPLACE` through the clear's write mask, the scissor is clamped as for draws, and visibility counting is off for the triangle. The value comes from the frame arena through the triangle's own argument tables, and the draws' tables are put back after it. A clear outside a pass opens the pass as a draw would. Clears a load action can express keep it. Two cases are still cleared whole, logged once: a depth / stencil clear with a scissor outside a pass, and a partial stencil write mask.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): it issues the first whole-cleared case (a depth clear scissored to 8×8 of a 64×64 target), and so does Wonder ([Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md)). With `render_cond` ([Conditional rendering evaluated at replay](#conditional-rendering-evaluated-at-replay)) TOTK's scissored colour clears are all skipped; without it they are 50×50 tiles of a 500×400 target cleared to opaque black, about five a frame. [Metroid Dread](../../dm/games/010093801237C000/README.md) logged the red line once while loading on its smoke route.

### A depth clear over the whole target with a partial stencil mask

- **Symptom:** a pass loads a whole depth/stencil target every frame only to clear it: Donkey Kong Bananza clears a 2880x1620 Depth32F-S8 target with stencil mask `0x1`, before any pass is open, and loads 22.8 MB a frame for it.
- **Cause:** any stencil mask other than 0 or 0xff takes `clear_ds_tri`, for depth too, and the triangle's pass loads both planes.
- **Fix:** with no pass open and a depth clear that covers the whole target, the depth half is the next pass's load action (`OP_CLEAR_DS` in `nvn_mtl.m`), and only the stencil is drawn. A clear that is partial in either sense, or made with a pass open, keeps the triangle for both.
- **Check:** `t_clear_ds`: the depth and stencil planes after a masked stencil clear with a full depth clear, outside and inside a pass.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) ([issue #1138](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1138)): on an M2 MacBook Air the pass went from 346 to 284 µs of GPU time and its load from 22.8 MB to 4.6 MB a frame.

### Depth16 load-clears match fragment writes

- **Symptom:** a tile grid in Ultrahand and Recall highlights; an `EQUAL` depth test rejects fragments writing the same depth as a cleared Depth16 target.
- **Cause:** Apple GPUs truncate a `Depth16Unorm` load-clear but round fragment depth. A clear of 0.5 stores 32767 while a fragment writing 0.5 stores 32768.
- **Fix:** `nvn_depth16_clear_value` selects the nearest D16 value in double precision and adds a quarter-unit margin inside both its truncation and rounding bins, surviving Metal's conversion of the descriptor to float. Both attachment load-clears (`rp_begin_ex_`) and whole-texture clears (`clear_tex`) use it only for `Depth16Unorm`. Other formats and clears drawn as fragments retain their existing conversion. `t_depth16_clear` compares both production command paths with fragment-written reference depths, including half-bin boundaries and adjacent floats.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md), reported in MacNativeGames commit `3d90e41` and ported in PR #199. The conversion difference is reproduced here with a synthetic Mac GPU test; the game's highlight effect and the iPhone have not been rechecked.

### Integer colour clears

- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferClearColori` / `ClearColorui` clear an integer target.
- **Fix:** recorded as a float clear; both replay paths convert the value to the attachment's integer type.
- **Found on:** [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md) (an integer target cleared once Mario stands on Rogueport's docks, about frame 12,300 of a new file).

### Same-pass feedback read through a snapshot

- **Symptom:** solid magenta blocks, exactly (255, 0, 255), hard-edged and aligned to the 16-pixel grid (16×16, sometimes two stacked): Super Mario 3D World and Super Mario Odyssey showed them only on the iPhone; Captain Toad and Origami King on a Mac, Apple M6, Captain Toad in about half the frames of the opening course, moving with the falling leaves. On an RG11B10F target (no alpha) the blocks are pure black or neon green, (0, 252, 0), and flicker, sometimes only part of a tile: Super Mario Bros. Wonder on an Apple M6 and an M1, most visible on the speech bubbles of dialogue; or non-finite pixels that bloom spreads into green blobs (NieR:Automata on the Mac). A rendered cutscene alternates brightness every game frame, with blocky bands of light and shadow switching on and off: each lighting-zone bit flips every frame (Xenoblade Chronicles 3, a read-only image load).
- **Cause:** a draw writes an attachment while sampling it (agl's post-effect program writes the RGB10A2 image as an attachment while sampling it; Origami King's anti-aliasing and NieR's FXAA likewise). Writing and sampling one attachment within a render pass is undefined in Metal: the M4 Pro happens to return the old content, while the A20 Pro and the Apple M6 now and then return whole 16×16 blocks as (1, 0, 1), probably blocks of the compressed render target caught while another tile stores them. On the Switch the read returns the content from before the draw.
- **Fix:** `fb_prepare` in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game. A draw whose fragment stage samples the level and layer of a colour attachment of its pass, and that writes that attachment (its channel mask and the shader's fragment outputs) or follows a draw of the same Metal pass that wrote it, samples a snapshot instead: the host ends the pass, copies the attachment's level and layer into a snapshot texture in the replay stream and binds it for that draw. NVN makes rendering visible to texture reads only through `nvnCommandBufferBarrier`, so a snapshot taken since the game's last barrier (and in the same submission) holds everything the game may expect to read: later such draws reuse it without ending the pass. A pass that read the attachment directly ends before a draw that writes it, so the earlier reads cannot see these writes in other tiles. A draw that samples an attachment nothing in its pass writes reads it directly, and a sampled view that spans more levels than the attachment's one (mip-chain passes) is left as it is. A draw that samples the attachment at its own pixel reads the tile instead ([Same-pass feedback read from the tile](#same-pass-feedback-read-from-the-tile)). The binding scan covers combined slots 0–127 and separate texture slots 128–255 (`NTEX`), using the argument encoder's handle lookup; an earlier bound of 128 silently skipped separate bindings. Before this rule, each game's profile listed its shaders in a `feedback_snap` key (six profiles), and a shader not yet seen to break stayed unprotected. `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` turns the snapshots off, `all` takes a fresh one for every draw that samples an attachment of its pass.
- **A clear still waiting:** a clear with no draw after it waits to be the load action of the next pass on its targets, so when the draw that takes a snapshot is the first of its pass, the attachment still holds what it had before the clear. The copy lands that clear first (`rp_flush`, not only the end of an open pass), for the colour snapshots and the depth copies ([Depth sampled in the pass that writes it](#depth-sampled-in-the-pass-that-writes-it)): before, a game that clears a target, sets a barrier and then draws into it while sampling it read the content from before its clear. Found by reading issue [#1932](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1932)'s patch, not seen in a game; `pending_clear_case` and `real_depth_snap_case` with a clear waiting check it (they failed before).
- **Image loads:** the same snapshot serves a read-only fragment image when its resolved single-level 2D view overlaps a colour attachment. Lookup and argument encoding use the same image view. Images that reinterpret the registered pixel class, writable images and atomic bindings keep their original bindings. Only sampled texture slots qualify for tile reads.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback` exercises the production snapshot and argument encoder at slots 0, 128 and 255 (GPU readback must retain a red snapshot after the original attachment is overwritten green, then read the green original on the next draw), and the rule: a draw that writes nothing reads an unwritten attachment directly, a writing draw takes a snapshot, the next one reuses it without ending the pass, a writing draw after a direct read ends the pass, a game barrier makes the next one take a fresh snapshot. 69 checks pass with Metal API validation on the Apple M6. On that Mac without a list (2026-10-30): Origami King's castle route went from blocks of up to 273 magenta pixels in its screenshots with `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` to 0–8 single pixels; Wonder's opening course from blocks in 4 of 97 screenshots to 0 of 160; NieR's opening flight from 662,086 non-finite pixels in 19 `DM_NVN_MAGSCAN` frames to none. Each snapshotted only the shaders its profile had listed.
- **Found on:**
  - [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md#found-on-this-game): the lighting shader reads its zone mask through an image slot.
  - [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md): `DM_NVN_MAGSCAN` on the A20 Pro found the blocks first in the 1920×1080 RGB10A2 composite (`ser=30`) in World 1-1, in the pass whose shader `66a71a3081b91b9a` renders into that texture while sampling it. They kept appearing with the sparkle particle `5e4de7561c670fee` dropped, so that earlier suspect (picked by position) is not the cause. The profile listed `66a71a3081b91b9a` and `30e3ccb0bd304ead` (which samples a G-buffer target of its own pass on the title screen); in 1-1 that is one 8 MB copy per frame. `3e8567da37eb28a1` renders level 1 of a texture while its view covers levels 0–4 (mip-chain passes) and is left as it is. The source said the fix was in place and waiting for a device run when written; the README lists the game as tested and playable on the iPhone.
  - [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its FXAA (`fd2c47b73f0e7e24`, `filter09.pso`) samples the RG11B10F scene target it renders into through a **separate** texture bind, and `fb_prepare` took only combined slots (`b->reg >= 128` was skipped), so the key had no effect. On the Mac some of its reads came back NaN in red and blue; the bloom spread them into green blobs and the eye adaptation darkened the frame (`DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`: 50,000+ non-finite pixels a frame over the opening's ocean, 0 with the snapshot). Separate texture binds now take the snapshot too.
  - [Captain Toad: Treasure Tracker](../../dm/games/01009BF0072D4000/README.md): agl's post-effect program 987 (`54f58a7cab608766`, the same program as 3D World's `66a71a3081b91b9a` in another build); `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=all` cleared every frame and named this one shader, which the profile then listed.
  - [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md#rendering): 16×16 magenta blocks on an iPhone 17 Pro (1.4.1), reported with `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP` runs that named the same agl post-effect program (`5345f3231650f0b7`) on the 1280×720 RGB10A2 image ([seedds/nxnative#544](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/544)). Odyssey draws it so only in handheld mode (the iPhone's): docked, it renders at 1600×900 and the post-effect reads another target. The report proposed a sixth list; the host now finds such draws itself, and on the Mac with `DM_OPMODE=0` the rule snapshots this shader from frame 617, where the iPhone's log had named it.
  - [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md#rendering): black and green blocks on the Mac (Apple M6; an M1 in [seedds/nxnative#377](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/377)) from its two feedback draws `a7e5c6fd501cf21f` and `22a61cb1ec732aef` on the RG11B10F scene, the same keys in v0 and 1.2.1, which had shown nothing on the iPhone. Screenshots with a flicker detector: v0's opening course 6 of 160 frames with blocks before, 0 of 150 with both listed; 1.2.1's Badge House dialogue 23 of 158 before, 0 of 158 after.
  - [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the cost case (below): over a thousand draws a frame sample a G-buffer target of their own pass, about 460 of them writing it.
  - [Paper Mario: The Origami King](../../dm/games/0100A3900C3E2000/README.md#rendering): magenta 16×16 blocks on the Mac (Apple M6) from its anti-aliasing pass `8dc20835c07cd7ff` (`shaders/aa.bfsha`) on the 1920×1080 RGB10A2 scene, the only shader `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=all` snapshotted; about one block a frame at Peach's castle before, none with it listed.
- **Note:** the cost. Breath of the Wild's G-buffer decals write and sample their own targets (`4d3d5a4408c2e8d9`, `753d0169ae3ce4f0` and others on the 1600×900 G-buffer), about 460 such draws a frame outdoors on the Great Plateau; a fresh snapshot for each (`DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=all`) takes the Mac's GPU time from about 4 to 28 ms a frame (frame time 16.6 → 36.6 ms before). Between the game's barriers they need about ten snapshots a frame, and about 50 draws reuse each: GPU time p50 3.5–4.1 ms with `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` and 3.7–4.5 ms with the rule over four runs each (2026-10-30, Apple M6, the outdoor save looking around, frames 4,800–6,900; the frame time stays at its 30 fps budget). The decals had shown no blocks on the iPhone; the snapshot makes them read what the Switch guarantees rather than what the tile happened to hold. `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=all` with a screenshot detector shows whether blocks come from such a draw at all, and a pass dump (`N … same-pass feedback sampling` lines after the draw's `K` keys) names the shaders.

### Same-pass feedback read from the tile

- **Symptom:** a scene full of deferred decals runs far below its target where the same scene with `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` holds it, and the log's `same-pass feedback sampling: snapshot #…` count climbs by hundreds a frame. Tears of the Kingdom's first shrine on an iPad Pro 11 (M5): GPU p50 58.8 ms, about 15 fps, about 284 snapshots a frame ([seedds/nxnative#737](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/737)); Breath of the Wild on the Great Plateau, camera toward the Temple of Time, on an iPad Pro 13 (M4): 60 → about 42 fps, about 45 one-draw passes more a frame on the 1920×1080 G-buffer. Splatoon Raiders in inked stages on a MacBook Air (M2): GPU p50 22–26 ms against 11–14 ms in the hub, a one-draw pass and a whole-map snapshot of its 6400×6400 ink map (80 MB) for each ink stamp ([seedds/nxnative#1063](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1063)). The `[nvn] same-pass feedback up to frame …` line (every 300 frames) gives the snapshots, reuses and tile reads per frame.
- **Cause:** [the snapshot rule](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot) is right but each snapshot costs a pass end, full-size copies and a pass reopened with every attachment loaded. The games issue a barrier (`0x12`, `ORDER_FRAGMENTS | INVALIDATE_TEXTURE`) before each decal that reads the G-buffer it writes, and such a barrier makes rendering visible to texture reads, so every decal after one needs a fresh snapshot. The turnaround between passes dominates (about 250 µs a pass on the M4 iPad, against 7–10 µs of work), so the cost is far higher on iPads than on a Mac. The decals sample the G-buffer at their own pixel: their vertex shader writes the clip → screen varying `((x + w) / 2, (w − y) / 2, ·, w)` of `gl_Position`, and the fragment shader samples at its `.xy / .w`. Splatoon Raiders' ink stamps (`6a5b76a60670e513`) set a barrier before each stamp too, and sample the map at `gl_FragCoord.xy` over the render scale times `c[6].xy`, which an inline uniform update sets to 1/6400 just before.
- **Fix:** in the chain and the host, for every game. `dmsh_fetch.py` rewrites a sample at `in.m_X.xy / in.m_X.w` in the readable-tile version (`<key>_bf`) as it does a sample at a plain interpolated output, and marks it projective; `screen_varyings` proves on a vertex shader's source that output X is the screen varying in every entry point (final assignments at the function's top level, one return, variables assigned once, constant coefficients). It also rewrites a sample at `(gl_FragCoord.x [/ render scale] * U, gl_FragCoord.y [/ render scale] * V)`, U and V uniform components, and records them (`frag_pos`); the host's render scale is always 1. `fpos_proof` takes such a sample when U and V, as the draw reads them, are 1 / the target's width and height (within 1/256 of a texel at the far edge): `gl_FragCoord` is the pixel centre whatever the viewport, and a lower-left window origin stores the image bottom-up, so its rows and `gl_FragCoord.y` move together. `ubo_float` gives the value of an inline update queued earlier in the submission instead of refusing it (`pend_read`). `fb_tile_plan` in `nvn_mtl.m` then makes a draw whose every sample of an attachment of its open pass is such a sample (for a projective sample the viewport over the whole target with no swizzle, upper-left window origin and no tessellation or geometry stage; the sampled view the attachment's own level, layer and format with no channel mapping beyond 0, 0, 1 for the channels its format lacks (`sw_tile`: Raiders' RG8 map is sampled through red, green, zero, one), no alpha test, logic operation or multisampling, TBDR optimizations off) read the attachment from the tile (`[[color(k)]]`) through its `_bf` function: no snapshot, no pass end. On Apple's GPUs the tile holds every earlier fragment of the pass in order, which is what NVN guarantees such a draw after a barrier, and one of the values it leaves undefined without one. The pipeline is built in the background on first use; until it is there the draw takes its snapshot, so no frame waits for the compiler. `DM_NVN_FB_TILE=0` turns the tile reads off. The same proofs now also let the readable-tile pass merge of the TBDR optimizations (`DM_NVN_TBDR=1`, `fz_p2_draw`) take projective and pixel-position samples. A tile read of an R, RG or RG11B10 attachment gives 0, 0, 1 in the channels the format lacks, as a sample does (M4 Pro, every R and RG float and normalized format); `t_feedback` checks it for RG8.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback` runs `test_dmsh_fetch.py --fixture` (Breath of the Wild's decal pair, cut down) and then `t_feedback` on it: a snapshot while the tile pipeline builds, then after a barrier none, the pass still open, the GPU reading green that an earlier draw of the pass wrote and red elsewhere, and a snapshot again with a viewport smaller than the target. Then Raiders' stamp, cut down, on an RG8 attachment: with the scale 1/8 in memory a snapshot and no tile pipeline, with an inline update of 1/4 queued the proof holds, and after a barrier the GPU reads green where an earlier draw wrote it, red elsewhere, with blue 0 and alpha 1; and the channel mappings `sw_tile` takes and refuses (152 checks with Metal API validation, Apple M4 Pro). `test_dmsh_fetch.py` checks the spellings the rewrite takes, the ones it must refuse, and that the variants compile for the Mac and the iPhone; `t_refl` checks the `frag_pos` fields. A frame captured looking at the Temple of Time (Breath of the Wild, chain `7ea22518e071`) replays bit-identical to the live frame with tile reads, and within 10 pixels by one in one byte with snapshots.
- **Found on:**
  - [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#rendering): the shrine slowdown of #737 (`7d1b1cac266f08f2`, `5733c5bf03d78005`, `9a5e3335514e4cbb` on the 1600×896 G-buffer, vertex shader `bb19fab2f950e587`).
  - [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the decals `4d3d5a4408c2e8d9`, `753d0169ae3ce4f0` and others (vertex shader `095cd6ad97fd779a`); about 320 draws a frame read the tile outdoors.
  - [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md#rendering): the ink stamps `6a5b76a60670e513` on the 6400×6400 RG8 ink map, reported with a first patch in #1063 by David Padron-Harris.
- **Note:** on a Mac (Apple M4 Pro) Breath of the Wild's outdoor save with the camera turning (frames 3,000–7,000, two runs each): snapshots 28.6 → 4.5 a frame, attachment traffic 666 / 596 → 354 / 344 MB load / store a frame, render passes p50 146 → 141, GPU span (first start to last end) p50 12.15 → 11.84 ms, GPU time p50 8.87 → 8.78 ms; the Mac's pass turnaround is small, so the gain there is small. The iPad cases are not measured here. The snapshots left a frame are draws whose samples are not projective (`b2f5ae9ef79011b9` on attachment 5, `7636e1d2542c221a`). Splatoon Raiders on the Mac (M4 Pro), the same scripted route to the first beach twice, frames 7,500–14,000: `DM_NVN_FB_TILE=0` 4.2 snapshots a frame and GPU p50 12.78 ms (windows of 300 frames, median), tile reads 0.6 snapshots, about 111 tile reads a frame (the stamps `6a5b76a60670e513` and `649e8d19b45094e7`) and GPU p50 9.50 ms; screenshots at the same moments differ only in animated water, smoke and the character. Breath of the Wild has 38 such shaders: 5 → 4 snapshots a frame and 71 → 0 draws reusing one, the same 293 tile reads. The reporter's heavier inked stage (22–26 ms of GPU on an M2) is not reproduced here.

### Snapshot textures taken by format and size

- **Symptom:** the `[nvn] residency set up to frame …` line (every 300 frames) shows hundreds to thousands of additions from `fb_snap`'s line in `nvn_mtl.m`, gigabytes of them, and as many removals, with the residency commits' time and the submit thread's kernel time up. Tears of the Kingdom on an iPhone 17 (1.4.2, [seedds/nxnative#931](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/931)): 1,500-1,600 additions of 2.4 MB textures every 300 frames, residency commits 250-600 ms every 300 frames against about 25 in an older build, kernel time on the submit thread 1-2 ms a frame against 0.2.
- **Cause:** the [snapshot](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot) cache has eight entries, and a new key took them in turn. A frame that snapshots attachments of several sizes almost never found an entry whose texture had the size it needed, so it released that texture and made one: Tears of the Kingdom takes 4-5 snapshots a frame of 1600×896 and smaller targets, and every one made a texture.
- **Fix:** `fb_snap` in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game: a new key takes an entry whose texture already has its format and size, preferring one no later draw can still reuse, then an entry no later draw can reuse, then the least recently used. An entry whose snapshot a draw may still reuse until the game's next barrier is taken last, so reuse is unchanged. The cache still holds at most eight textures.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback`: 30 snapshots of four sizes over six frames, each after a barrier, make four textures, and a live snapshot is not taken by another key while a stale entry is free (98 checks).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#found-on-this-game). On the Mac (Apple M4 Pro, 2026-11-03, the regression route, frames 1,800-5,100) against `main` at `12915a31`: textures made 1,274-1,650 (4.4-6.0 GB) → 0-4 every 300 frames, residency commits 1,208-1,390 taking 150-212 ms → 31-65 taking 1.8-5.3 ms, the submit thread's kernel time 0.81-0.85 → 0.29 ms a frame (together with [the readback buffers](nvn-objects.md#readback-buffers-kept-for-reuse)); snapshots, reuses and tile reads a frame the same (13.0, 7.0, 22.0), 59.8 fps in both.

### Bindless shaders taken as depth readers

- **Symptom:** a scene with many bindless decals runs well below its target with the GPU busy, and a pass dump (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`) shows long runs of one-draw passes on the same targets, each loading and storing every attachment. Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's battle maps: 31 consecutive one-draw passes on four 1024×576 targets a frame, 126.5 passes and about 1.4 GB of attachment loads and stores a frame.
- **Cause:** `fb_prepare` marked the pass's depth attachment as read (`g_fb_dread`) for every fragment shader with handles in uniform buffers (`rf.bindless`), since which texture such a handle names is known only at replay. A later draw that writes depth then ended the pass ([the depth half of the snapshot rule](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)). Fortune's Weave's decals are bindless and write depth, so each one ended the pass the one before it opened.
- **Fix:** `fb_texu_reads` in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game. A bindless fragment shader counts as a depth reader only when one of its TEXU handles, read from its uniform buffer as `encode_args` reads it for the draw, names a texture of the depth attachment's serial. Pool handles (`rf.pool`), dynamically indexed tables (`rf.dyn`) and a bindless shader with a TEXA binding still count as readers, as before. Like the colour rule, the test is by serial: a depth texture read through another NVN texture object over the same storage is not seen.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> run`. With `DM_AB=300 DM_NVN_FB_TEXU=ab` (an A/B switch used for the measurement, not part of the change) on the battle map, in-process over 16 phases: passes 126.5 → 93.5 a frame, attachment traffic 603 / 784 → 239 / 420 MB load / store, frame time p50 18.9 → 17.3 ms (MacBook Pro M1 Pro). Screenshots of the same frames with the rule off and on differ only where the fog and effects animate; the decals draw the same.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md#found-on-this-game).


### Pool shaders taken as depth readers

- **Symptom:** a game that reads textures through a pool runs slower than its draws explain, with passes of one draw each on the same targets. A pass dump (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`) shows `SetRenderTargets` going on in the open pass and the pass ending anyway. Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest: two frames of four had 219 passes, 127 of them on the 128x128 reflection probe's cube faces, and frame times of 15, 15, 18, 18.5 ms in a cycle.
- **Cause:** `fb_prepare` marked the depth attachment as read (`g_fb_dread`) for every fragment shader with pool bindings (`rf.pool`), since which texture a pool handle names is known only on the GPU. The next draw that wrote depth then ended the pass ([the depth half of the snapshot rule](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)). What a pool could hold does not narrow this: the game registers its depth textures in the pool every material shader reads ([issue 1385](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1385), [PR 1396](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1396)).
- **Fix:** `pool_ds_read` in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game. The shaders already report which IDs they read ([read tracking](memory.md#shaders-report-which-textures-they-read-through-a-pool)), so each submission's new bits add their depth and stencil textures to a set, and a pool shader counts as a depth reader only for a texture in it. The set is keyed by `tex_alias_key`, what makes objects one Metal texture, so a second object over the same storage counts, and a new object of another size over reused memory does not. It has no size limit: the game makes new shadow maps as its resolution moves, and a first version with 64 entries overflowed after 3 minutes and lost the whole gain. Colour textures over the depth texture's memory do not count: Metal keeps them in textures of their own. `rf.dyn` and TEXA bindings still count as readers.
- **Limit:** the bitmap is read at the next submission, so the very first pass that reads a depth texture through a pool is not ended for it, as read tracking has the same lag. If a later area reads its main depth through a pool, every depth-writing draw after such a shader ends its pass again: the `pool walk` line counts those passes (`passes ended after a pool shader that may read depth`), 0 in Kokiri Forest. [PR 1430](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1430) goes the other way, not counting a draw that writes depth as a reader, which assumes a shader never reads the depth it writes; Ocarina of Time's cave fixtures do ([Depth sampled in the pass that writes it](#depth-sampled-in-the-pass-that-writes-it)).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback` (`pool_depth_case`). On Ocarina's walk: passes a frame 182 → 101 and level across frames, attachments 300 / 325 → 254 / 277 MB load / store, the heavy frame's GPU span 19.2 → 12.6 ms, summed GPU p50 17.5 → 15.5 ms. No depth attachment of the main pass or the probes was ever read through a pool; about 70 shadow maps were. The pictures show no tile pattern.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### Depth sampled in the pass that writes it

- **Symptom:** blocks over the scene where an effect compares against the scene's depth, changing from frame to frame. Short screen-aligned dashes or blocks over rock and wall surfaces, light blue (the sky behind) or black, that stay on the same surfaces as the camera moves; how large and how many depends on the tree and the render size. Per-pass snapshots (`DM_NVN_TEXDUMP` with `DM_NVN_TEXSNAP`) show the surface drawn whole, then a later full-screen pass painting the blocks over it. Or light square blocks, 8×8 pixels, in the sky over distant hills and trees, where a soft-edged effect fades against the scene's depth.
- **Cause:** a draw samples the depth attachment of its own render pass while that pass writes it: the draw writes depth (or stencil) itself, or an earlier draw of the pass did, with no barrier between them. On the console a draw reads the depth stored when it starts. On Apple's GPUs a depth attachment sampled in the pass that writes it reads whole compressed blocks of undefined content, so the shader's comparison against the sample fails or passes block by block, and Metal has no tile read for depth. `fb_prepare` ended the pass only for a depth write after a read (`g_fb_dread`, [the depth half of the snapshot rule](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)), and only in passes with a colour attachment.
- **Fix:** `fb_depth_rule` in `nvn_mtl.m`, called by `fb_prepare`, for every game. The depth attachment follows the colour rule, by what the pass and the draw write:
  - A draw that writes depth or stencil and samples the attachment through a texture slot reads a copy (`fb_depth_snap`): the pass ends, the attachment's level and layer are copied into a snapshot (`fb_snap`, the cache the colour snapshots use) and the draw's binding reads the copy. The copy is reused, without ending the pass, until the game's next barrier, as the colour snapshots are: NVN makes rendering visible to texture reads only through a barrier.
  - A draw that samples it after an earlier draw of its Metal pass wrote depth or stencil (`g_fb_dwrote`), and writes none itself, ends the pass first and reads what was stored, whether bound in a slot or named by a handle in a uniform buffer (`fb_texu_reads`, as in [Bindless shaders taken as depth readers](#bindless-shaders-taken-as-depth-readers)).
  - A draw that samples it first in its pass reads it directly, and a later draw that writes depth ends the pass, as before.

  The rule runs in depth-only passes too. There it takes only reads it can name: a handle table in c2 (TEXA), pool shaders and dynamically indexed tables, which the host cannot resolve to a texture, count as possible readers only in passes with a colour attachment, as before; in shadow passes they would end the pass at every draw. `DM_NVN_FB_DEPTH=0` reads the attachment directly in every case, as before. The log names the first copy (`same-pass depth sampling: copy #1`) and the first pass ended (`same-pass depth sampling: pass end #1`).
- **Limit:** a draw that writes depth and reads it through an image slot, a uniform-buffer handle, a pool or a dynamic table still reads it in the pass: no copy can stand in for a binding the host does not encode itself. [PR 1430](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1430)'s assumption, that a shader never reads the depth it writes ([Pool shaders taken as depth readers](#pool-shaders-taken-as-depth-readers)), does not hold: the Ocarina of Time draws below do.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback`: `depth_snap_case` (a copy for a draw that writes and samples depth, reuse until a barrier, a direct read by a draw that leaves depth alone, the switch off), `depth_after_write_case` (the pass ends before a draw that samples the depth an earlier draw wrote, not before one that samples it first, the switch off) and `real_depth_snap_case` (an actual private Depth32Float attachment, cleared by the production render pass, copied and read back, Depth32Float and Depth32Float_Stencil8, in a colour pass and a depth-only one, and with the clear still waiting for the draw's pass). `regress.py check` on Octopath Traveler II (Apple M4 Pro, 2026-11-10), which takes both paths on its Depth32Float_Stencil8 scene depth (`599866e198259922` and `e2d2ca47a4112324` copy it, about 21 draws reusing each copy; `e0eb1b2bdef248ca` ends about one pass a frame): no new `[RED]` line, fps p50 59.8 and frame p99 18.19 → 18.27 ms, screenshots the same.
- **Found on:**
  - [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), the cave entrance on Death Mountain Trail from a player's save ([PR 1917](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1917)). Right after the cave walls, the full-screen 25a8adc977ff813d samples the scene depth (1536×864 Depth32Float) and writes it with LEQUAL, and five decal draws after it do the same. There are no barriers between those six draws: with a copy for each, 1,000 of them took 1,000 copies, and reused until a barrier about 190, with the same picture and no measurable GPU difference between the two (M4 Pro, two runs each). In play (docked, in a window on the M4 Pro), thin light-blue dashes over the dark rock above the cave arch, gone with the copy. Headless captures of the same spot at 1440x810 and 1536x864 did not show them, so the check there is by eye. Cost of the copy at that spot (headless, docked, against the attachment read directly, two runs each): GPU p50 12.17 against 12.23 ms, frame times the same.
  - The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, the snow in adult Zora's Domain ([issue 1932](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1932)) and on frozen walls, pillars and stairs ([PR 1995](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1995)): square holes or black blocks along the snow's edges on an M3 Max, moving every frame. The snow's pixel shaders (`1d3d97b789b9a6e6`, `06750e798cfec8ef`, `c2352ccb908fa8d0`, `55845639d605e028`, `ee567dea65288f58`) write depth with LEQUAL and sample the scene depth through texture slot 6 to fade their alpha; the depth read came back NaN in whole 8×8 blocks, and a NaN fade is alpha 0. About 145 such draws a frame in Zora's Domain. On the reporter's tree the same copy reused until a barrier took the holes from 108 / 37 blocks (snow / rocks, a tile detector) to 4–8 / 0, and GPU p50 from 25 to 15 ms (passes 244 → 99 a frame, 39 → 60 fps): before it, the depth half of the colour rule gave each of these draws a pass of its own. Not yet run on this repository's main. PR 1995's other design, a pass of its own per draw that draws into a copy of the depth and copies it back, cost a pass and two depth copies a draw.
  - Red Dead Redemption (`01007820196A6000`, profile not in this repository; [PR 1866](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1866)): its cloud fog `rdr2_clouds_fog` (`c0ab3c051753e36d`) fades the clouds against the depth it samples through a uniform-buffer handle, in the pass where the billboards (`rdr2_billboard`), the low-detail terrain and other geometry have just written depth: beige blocks above the ridge behind Armadillo, in both 1.0.0 and 1.0.4. With the pass ended before it (Apple M4, macOS 27.0.1, the route out of the saloon in Armadillo): the blocks gone, about one more pass end a frame, GPU p50 14.3 → 14.7 ms, frame time unchanged at the game's 30 fps.

### Mip-chain pass sampling the level it renders

- **Symptom:** NaN texels in the upper levels of a texture the game fills level by level (each level drawn from the one above), and from there NaN wherever the texture is read: black speckles in whole 2×2 quads, white blotches once bloom spreads them, and a dark frame from the eye adaptation.
- **Cause:** a mip-chain pass renders level n while its fragment shader samples level n − 1 through a view that covers the whole chain (explicit level, linear mip filter). On NVIDIA that reads level n − 1 only. On Metal the fetch can also touch level n, at weight 0 but still fetched, and an attached level read through a texture holds no defined data on Apple's GPUs; 0 × NaN is NaN. The same-pass feedback rule ([above](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)) left such passes as they were.
