- **Fix:** `fb_prepare` in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game: when the sampled view's levels include the attachment's level and start below it, the draw samples a view of the same texture limited to the levels below the attachment (same base, so the shader's level numbers keep their meaning). A pass rendering the view's base level while sampling higher ones, and layer passes, are still left as they are.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#rendering): its environment cube (128×128, 8 levels, R11G11B10F, levels 1–7 drawn by `d1a506c1a94ffdfa` and `89dfaeb53f85920a`) got NaN texels; copied into the sources of the prefiltered cube (`62dc9b15931e0290`) and fed back through reflections and the cube capture every frame, they reached 59,592 NaN pixels of the first gameplay frame's scene (0 with the fix).

### Upsample pass reading the level above the one it renders

- **Symptom:** bloom wide and hard around bright edges: neon letters swell into fat, filled shapes. With `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` they are thin again, but the white blotches of the entry above come back.
- **Cause:** the fix in [Mip-chain pass sampling the level it renders](#mip-chain-pass-sampling-the-level-it-renders) gives a mip-chain pass a view of the levels below its attachment, which assumes the pass downsamples. An upsample pass renders level n and reads level n + 1, chosen by its sampler's LOD clamp (n + 1..n + 1). In a view that ends at level n − 1 Metal clamps that read to level n − 1, a sharper level, and the chain adds it back up to the top. An upsample into the view's base level got no view at all and read the attached level.
- **Fix:** `fb_prepare` in `nvn_mtl.m` (`fb_pinned_level`), for every game: when the slot's sampler (the combined handle's, the shader's `SAMPT` binding of the same slot) has a mip filter and a minimum and maximum LOD that are the same whole number, every fetch reads that level, so the draw samples a view of that level alone, whichever side of the attachment it lies on (not the attachment's own). Other samplers keep the view of the levels below. `t_feedback` (`mip_chain_case`) checks the view each sampler gets.
- **Open:** an upsample that picks its level in the shader (an explicit LOD through a sampler that pins none) still gets the view of the levels below; no game is known to do it.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#rendering), found by the author of PR #1073: its two bloom chains (576×324 R11G11B10F, 5 levels) downsample with `ef18c6eca9b2f991` (sampler LOD n − 1..n − 1) and upsample with `ad1a5991aaad135e` (n + 1..n + 1); a pass dump showed the upsample drawing level 3 through view `0+3` with LOD 4..4. With the rule switched off and on through the Ingot Isle stage intro, the neon letters went fat and filled, then thin, with it.

### Lower-left window origin

- **Symptom:** text a game renders into textures for the title and the menus comes out upside down, while the scene looks right.
- **Cause:** a game that never calls `nvnDeviceSetWindowOriginMode` keeps NVN's default, lower-left (OpenGL), where NDC y = -1 lands on a render target's first memory row. Read as upper-left, every texture the game renders and samples came out flipped and cancelled out, but not the text, and the game copies the frame with `MIRROR_Y`.
- **Fix:** viewport and scissor rectangles, fragment coordinates, copies and texture coordinates address the same memory rows in both conventions, so the host flips the direction inside the viewport (negative viewport height) and flips the window image at present and in raw screenshots (`g_origin_ll` in `nvn_mtl.m`). Facing also follows the [raster flip captured at queue initialization](#queue-initialization-captures-the-raster-origin).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md); the six games before it all switch to upper-left.
- **Note:** the window's Y-direction system register under this origin is open ([Open: the window Y-direction register `SR_Y_DIRECTION`](shaders.md#open-the-window-y-direction-register-sr_y_direction)); the OpenGL layer renders through this origin ([GL orientation through the lower-left origin](#gl-orientation-through-the-lower-left-origin)), and Absolum combines it with a viewport swizzle ([Viewport swizzles applied as a flipped viewport](#viewport-swizzles-applied-as-a-flipped-viewport)).

### Queue initialization captures the raster origin

- **Symptom:** geometry appears inside out and almost black; a fullscreen indirect-lighting triangle is entirely culled even though its vertex data and polygon state match the game.
- **Cause:** NVN emits `SET_WINDOW_ORIGIN.FLIP_Y` into a queue's initial GPU state. `DeviceSetWindowOriginMode` only changes the device field that later viewport construction reads; it does not rewrite an existing queue's raster flip. Deriving Metal winding from the current device origin loses this distinction when the game creates a queue before changing the origin. Reversing winding globally fixes that sequence but breaks games that set their origin first.
- **Fix:** `queue_t.origin_ll` captures the device origin in `nvnQueueInitialize`. Each submission selects that queue's raster origin under the submission lock. Metal winding uses this captured origin; viewport direction and presentation keep using the device origin. The full-coverage proof also accounts for the difference between these origins, and for a negative-Y viewport swizzle.
- **Check:** `t_queue_origin` alternates two queues created with different origins on one device. GPU readback checks CW/CCW triangles, both current device origins, every cull mode, `front_facing`, negative-Y swizzles and the full-coverage proof: 389 checks pass with direct and deferred Metal encoding; restoring the previous winding expression fails 48. The complete Metal suite passes. Mario Wonder's full smoke route passes with the fix.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#rendering), with native SDK initialization evidence and the comparison with Mario Wonder recorded there.

### The residency set committed before a present

- **Symptom:** a pink window with every command buffer ignored while headless runs look right: the first presents fail on the display command buffer with `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, and after a few of those macOS ignores every submission of the process ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)").
- **Cause:** the game presents its window texture in its first frames before drawing into it; the texture was made on that first resolution in the present path and added to the residency set (`res_add`), but only a submission commits the set, so the display command buffer sampled a texture outside the committed set. Headless runs never present, which is why they looked right.
- **Fix:** `present_now` commits the residency set after resolving the presented texture (`res_commit`, free when nothing changed), for every game.
- **Found on:** [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) (13.0.5, `DM_WINDOW=1`).
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild windowed with the change: its title, 60 fps, no GPU error.

### Naming the shaders of a GPU fault

- **Symptom:** `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault` or `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang` on a command buffer, and nothing says which draw or dispatch caused it; `DM_RES_CHECK=1` reports no `missing residency`.
- **Cause:** Metal 4's commit feedback gives only the error and two times, so the log can name a submission but no pass or shader. `MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION=1` can name one, at about a fifth of the frame rate, which is little use for a fault several minutes into a game.
- **Fix:** each command buffer keeps the list of the game shaders it encoded (`cbs_note` in `encode_args`, one comparison per binding once a shader is listed). On an error that command buffer caused, the log has `[RED] game shaders of failed command buffer #N (fence F, segment K): …`, then `[RED] game shaders by failed/ran over N failed of M command buffers: key failed/ran, …`, most often failed first: in how many failed command buffers the shader was, of how many that encoded it. Read the second line of the last report: a shader in most failures with a high ratio is the suspect; one that runs everywhere has a low ratio. A victim of another's fault and a submission refused after one do not count. The first 16 failures are printed. The lists leave out the host's own passes and geometry emulation's kernels, so no shader standing out points there. Nothing here shows a shader is wrong, only that it was present: check it with validation or by reading what it addresses. A validation run must not share a `pso_archive` with normal runs: they cannot use the files it writes.
- **What can fault:** measured on an M4 Pro, with a Metal 4 queue: loads and atomics through a buffer address that is unmapped, freed, not resident or null, from compute or fragment shaders, raise no error (reads give 0 or whatever is mapped there, so an overrun can silently write into another allocation). A fragment shader sampling a texture not in the residency set, or one freed whose resource ID nothing holds, raises `PageFault`; after two of these macOS refused every later submission. A freed texture's resource ID goes to the next texture made, and a stale binding then reads that texture with no error. Other chips may differ.
- **Early release:** `DM_RES_CHECK=1` also reports `[RED] released too early`: an object retired behind an earlier fence than a binding of it. Its `missing residency` line cannot see this, as the object is still in the set at that binding.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) ([issue #1289](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1289)), whose faults recur every minute or so before a fatal one.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_cbs_fault`.

### A present snapshot's copy needs the same residency commit

- **Symptom:** with present snapshots on (`DM_PRESENT_SNAP`, the iOS launcher's **Frame buffering**), a frozen, corrupted picture from the first presents; the first error is already `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorSubmissionsIgnored` ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)") on the display command buffer, then on every upload, segment and submission command buffer, and `[RED] present snapshots: the previous copy into a ring slot did not finish within 2 s`. With snapshots off the same game runs.
- **Cause:** the case of [the entry above](#the-residency-set-committed-before-a-present), on the snapshot path: `snap_take` resolves the presented texture and copies it into a ring slot on the game's queue before `present_now` runs, and only `present_now` committed the residency set. A window texture presented before any submission had used it was outside the committed set when the copy read it. The copy's command buffer has no error handler, so its fault shows only as the ignored command buffers after it.
- **Fix:** `snap_take` commits the residency set after resolving the source (`res_commit`, free when nothing changed), for every game.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST XI S](../../dm/games/01006C300E9F0000/README.md) 1.0.4 on an iPad mini 7 (A17 Pro), which presents three times before its first submission ([GitHub issue #626](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/626)). The reporter's Apple M4 test did not fault without the fix.
- **Not checked:** the iPad with the fix.

### A GPU whose access macOS revoked stops the game

- **Symptom:** after a GPU hang or GPU page faults, every upload, segment, submission and display command buffer comes back `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorSubmissionsIgnored` ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)"). Nothing is drawn again while the game's sound and logic go on, until the game aborts itself (`game called nn::diag abort`, about 70 s later); in full screen, WindowServer froze for 40 s and its watchdog killed it. With the fix the log has `[RED] GPU access revoked: the … command buffer was ignored`, and a Mac app shows an alert and quits.
- **Cause:** macOS revokes a process's GPU access after GPU errors it caused, and from then on runs none of its command buffers: they come back at once with `MTL4CommandQueueErrorAccessRevoked`. The host logged them and went on. Their feedback still came, but the GPU no longer signalled the shared event a game's SyncWait waits on (for up to an hour, `mtl_wait_at`), and each present took a drawable that no command buffer drew. Metal 4 reports a commit's error as `MTL4CommandQueueErrorTimeout` with no text, with the command buffer's own error under `NSUnderlyingErrorKey` and `NSMultipleUnderlyingErrorsKey`, so the top-level code alone never shows it.
- **Fix:** `gpu_lost_check` (`nvn_mtl.m`) looks for `MTL4CommandQueueErrorAccessRevoked` in each command buffer's error and the errors under it; the first one stops the game, for every game:
  - presents stop (`present_now`);
  - the shared event is raised from the CPU to the last fence whose feedback has come back, then again at each later submission's feedback (`gpu_lost_release`), so no SyncWait waits for a signal that will not come, and no command allocator is reset before Metal hands its command buffer back;
  - the front end is told once (`front_gpu_lost`, `dm/host/front.h`). A Mac app leaves full screen, says why in an alert that points to the log and `scripts/bug_report.py`, and quits with exit status 70 (⌘Q's is 0). Without a window, and on the iPhone, the run ends with a FATAL line and an abort.

  A hang or a page fault alone does not stop the game: the GPU recovers from those, and command buffers discarded as innocent victims (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorInnocentVictim`, also a timeout) come back while the game goes on. `DM_TEST_GPU_LOST=<n>` imitates the revoked access from the nth submission on, and `t_gpu_lost` checks the error's shape, the single call, the released waits and the stopped presents.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (v131072) on a Mac ([GitHub issue #1326](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1326)). Its first run hung in full screen and every command buffer after the hang was ignored. Its second run, in a window, recovered from several innocent-victim timeouts, page-faulted at fences 110102 and 110108, and had every command buffer ignored from fence 110114, two frames later.
- **Open:** the hang and the page faults that made macOS revoke the access.
- **Not checked:** a real revoked access with the fix; only the imitation (`t_gpu_lost`).

### A CPU-written window texture resolved where the present is queued

- **Symptom:** an abort at the first present: `[RED] upload batches belong to the submit thread only: up_begin called on thread 'nvn-present-link'`, with `pkg_upload`, `tex_mtl` and `present_now` in the backtrace.
- **Cause:** the game fills its window textures with `nvnTextureWriteTexels` at boot and presents one before drawing into it. A texture whose content comes from its storage (CPU-written, packaged) is uploaded when it is first resolved, and again when its pages have changed since, in an upload batch only the submit thread may open; the first resolution of this one was `present_now` on the present thread. Two cases were left after the first fix: a texture first used by the present, whose first use decides whether it is taken from its storage ([storage the CPU wrote before its first use](nvn-objects.md#block-compressed-storage-the-cpu-wrote-before-first-use), `tex_cpu_content`), so it did not count as one before; and an uploaded one whose pages changed between the queue and the present thread, which the present thread uploaded again.
- **Fix:** `present_enqueue`, on the thread that queues the present, resolves the texture when that is the submit thread, and commits the upload batch (`up_flush`) when it is taken from its storage, so the display command buffer comes after the upload on the queue. Off the submit thread `tex_mtl` hands back an uploaded storage texture as it is, without looking at its pages (the submit thread's next look still finds a write since): a present shows the content as of when the game queued it. For every game.
- **Found on:** [Bastion](../../dm/games/010038600B27E000/README.md) (1.0.0, SDK 5.4.0: both 1920x1080 window textures written by `main+0x9374`). The two later cases in `t_nvn`, whose window textures nothing draws into: they were GPU textures while a sliver of another GPU-written span counted, and came up as storage textures once it did not (c06d1e79).
- **Check:** `t_nvn` (present pacing presents its never-drawn window textures).

### The present upscales through MetalFX

- **Symptom:** a game that renders below the screen's resolution looks soft on a phone, in a large Mac window or on a 4K screen.
- **Cause:** the drawable has the layer's native pixel size (`front_ios.m`, `front_mac.m`), and the present pass (`present_encode_m4`) scaled the presented texture into it bilinearly.
- **Fix:** `DM_PRESENT_UPSCALE=metalfx` (the iOS launcher's **Upscaling**; off by default on every platform, no profile key): where the picture's rectangle on the canvas (`present_viewport`) is larger than the content (`fx_wanted`), the present copies the content to the origin of a texture of its size (MetalFX reads from the origin, so a crop with an offset works too), MetalFX's spatial scaler (`MTL4FXSpatialScaler`, perceptual colour processing: the input is the raw gamma-encoded bytes the present samples) scales it into a texture of the rectangle's size in the present's Metal 4 command buffer, and the present pass draws that 1:1 (`fx_encode`). Letterbox, the lower-left origin and the drawable format stay the present pass's. The copy waits for the queue's earlier work and ends with a producer barrier that the scaler's encoders wait behind. A new size or format makes a new scaler after the presents on the GPU finish, at most once every 30 presents (a window being resized shows bilinear frames meanwhile); a size MetalFX refuses stays bilinear. Whole-window captures and `DM_NVN_SHOT_OUT` screenshots draw the picture the same way (`present_offscreen_m4`), with separate scaler state and resources retained until GPU completion; `DM_NVN_SHOT` and `DM_CTL` "shot" still save the game's own frame. Tests: `t_present_crop` (sizing, offset crops, letterbox, sRGB bytes, lower-left origin, standard fallback, resize throttling and asynchronous whole-window capture).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild — upscaling and measurements](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#upscaling-current).
- **Limit:** the scaler takes the presented frame, including any filtering and HUD drawing the game already performed. Feeding it an earlier scene target would mean replacing a draw of the game's own, and nothing general identifies which draw is a plain upscale. Scaler support is checked at runtime; unsupported devices, sizes and formats use standard scaling.

### The NVN present crops by the vi layer crop

- **Symptom:** in handheld mode the picture fills only two thirds of the screen's width and height, in the bottom-left corner (the top-left with the upper-left origin), the rest black.
- **Cause:** the game keeps its 1920x1080 window textures in handheld mode, draws 1280x720 into them and crops through `vi::SetLayerCrop`, never `nvnWindowSetCrop`. The host recorded the layer crop (`hle_vi.c`) only for the Vulkan present ([Swapchain pre-transform and layer crop](#swapchain-pre-transform-and-layer-crop)); the NVN present read only the window's crop and showed the whole texture.
- **Fix:** the NVN present shows the window's own crop when the game set one, else the vi layer crop (`present_crop` in `hle_nvn.c`); on the Switch both become the crop of the buffer queued to the layer. A layer crop that does not fit the window texture is reported once in red and the whole texture is shown, as before.
- **Check:** `t_present_crop`: the window's crop wins, the layer crop is used without one, one larger than the texture is ignored, and a real `nvnQueuePresentTexture` queues the layer crop (that check fails with the old call). On an iPhone 15 Pro in handheld mode Link's Awakening's picture fills the screen's height.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md#rendering) (1.1.0, handheld mode on the iPhone).

### A crop only picks texels; the picture has the layer's shape

- **Symptom:** a scene turns narrow, with black bars at its sides, while the swapchain or window textures keep their size; circles come out as tall ovals. The log has `vi::SetLayerCrop 0,0 1200x960` and `present upscaling: ... 1200x960 → 1800x1440` in a 2560x1440 window.
- **Cause:** the present used one rectangle for two things: which texels to read and the shape to show them in. On the Switch the compositor stretches a buffer's crop to the layer, so a game can draw an anamorphic picture into a crop of another shape: 1200x960 of a 1920x1080 swapchain docked, 800x640 handheld. The host fitted the crop's own 5:4 into the canvas, and reported 5:4 to the front ends.
- **Fix:** the crop still selects the source texels, for every graphics API, and `present_shape` (`nvn_mtl.m`) gives the shape: the display's 16:9, or a sized layer's (`vi::CreateLayer` with a size, `mtl_present_layer`). A screen taller than 16:9 (`DM_SCREEN_ASPECT`, `mtl_present_screen`) counts only for a texture of its shape, since only builds with screen-shape stubs render it. Both encoders, MetalFX, the screen-sized captures and the shape reported to the front ends (`front_picture_shape`) use it, so a canvas of another shape still gets bars around a 16:9 picture and clicks map onto the picture as drawn. `vi::SetLayerScalingMode` stays ignored: the games seen set mode 2 or none.
- **Check:** `t_present_crop`: a 5:4 crop fills a 16:9 canvas and keeps bars on a 16:10 one on the classic and Metal 4 presents, a sized layer's shape, both `DM_SCREEN_ASPECT` cases and the shape a real present reports.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy 2](../../dm/games/0100FD8022DAA000/README.md#rendering) ([issue #949](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/949)).

### Setting the same targets again continues the pass

- **Symptom:** one-draw passes on one target, hundreds of times a frame, with the same attachments each time (a `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` frame: `load` and `store` of the whole target in every line); a low frame rate that follows the target's size.
- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferSetRenderTargets` ended the host's pass even when it named the targets of the open pass. On the Switch the call costs nothing; on a tile-based GPU each pass stores its attachments, and the next one starts again.
- **Fix:** the call goes on in the open pass when the targets are the same (`rt_same`, `rtc_on` in `nvn_mtl.m`; `DM_NVN_RT_CONTINUE=0` ends every pass as before). What a pass end gave the draws around it is covered: a colour attachment sampled by [the same-pass feedback rule](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot), a game barrier by `barrier_game`. Depth has two rules of its own: a draw that samples the depth attachment after the call ends the pass there, as its memory holds nothing of this pass until then, and a draw writing depth or stencil after a draw of the pass read that attachment ends it too, as the GPU stores the pass tile by tile, so the earlier reads would see the later writes in the tiles already done. A pass with no colour attachment ends as before.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): its ink map (6400x6400 RG8 with a Depth32F-S8) got about 150 one-draw occlusion queries a frame, each after setting the map again: 150 passes of about 1 ms, 42 GB of attachment loads a frame, about 6 fps. Without the depth rules its water came out in grey blocks of tiles: the water reads the scene's depth, then the next draw writes the water's own.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback` (the depth attachment read, then written).

### A render area for passes on very large targets

- **Symptom:** a pass on a very large target costs about the same however little it draws; `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` shows time proportional to the target's area.
- **Cause:** a pass with no render area covers its whole attachments. A synthetic pass on a flat 6400x6400 target cost about 0.1 ms, so the cost depends on what the target holds or how the host creates it; which of the two was not found.
- **Fix:** a draw pass on a target of 16 M pixels or more that loads what it keeps stops at the far corner of its scissor when that is at most half the target (`renderTargetWidth` / `renderTargetHeight`, `g_rp_aw` in `nvn_mtl.m`; `DM_NVN_RP_AREA=0` turns it off). Only the draw path opens such a pass. A draw whose scissor leaves the area ends it, and so does anything else that needs the pass (`rp_begin`).
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): each ink stamp's pass, scissored to 1952x2940 at (0, 3460), about 0.95 → 0.32 ms. Metal keeps what lies outside the area (a 6400x6400 target checked texel by texel on an M4 Pro, with API validation on).
- **Note:** the same stamps sample the map they write, with a game barrier each, so each still takes a whole snapshot of it ([Same-pass feedback read through a snapshot](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)), about 0.76 ms; a copy of the stamp's rectangle takes 0.09 ms, but nothing proves a stamp samples only inside it. **Open.**

### Variable rate shading ignored

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferShadingRateImagePalette`, or `nvnCommandBufferBindShadingRateImage` or `nvnCommandBufferSetShadingRatePaletteMode`, every frame.
- **Cause:** NVN2 lets a game shade tiles of a pass at a coarser rate. Metal's render passes have no per-tile rate: a rasterization rate map resamples the whole target instead, which is another thing.
- **Fix:** `hle_nvn.c` accepts the three calls and records nothing. Shading every pixel is the most any rate asks for, so the picture is the same or sharper, and the only cost is GPU time where the game meant to save it. This drops a feature rather than emulating it, and the choice is a platform limit.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md), every frame; [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md), the palette.

## Blend, depth and stencil

### Ten attachment records for eight colors and depth-stencil

- **Symptom:** angle-dependent character smearing, or vertex-only pipeline failures with `invalid depth/stencil pipeline formats 16843012/260`, although the pass began with valid `260/260` formats.
- **Cause:** the store-decision ledger `g_sd` had nine entries, but eight color targets plus separately tracked depth and stencil need ten. The tenth `sd_att` overran the array. On the observed iPad build it overlapped `g_pass`: `udc_note_draw` wrote the overflow entry's `wrote` and `wwhy` bytes into the high bytes of the cached depth format. A later vertex-only skinning pipeline was rejected, so its dependent draws consumed stale bone matrices. The lookahead's nine-byte result array had the same capacity error.
- **Fix:** one `SD_ATT_MAX` constant sizes the ten ledger entries and both store-lookahead result declarations. Both aspects retain their own write/store decisions; no optimization or shader is disabled.
- **Check:** `t_attachment_capacity` replays eight distinct color targets with combined depth/stencil and dependent vertex-only producer/consumer draws. Literal GPU readbacks verify all 8,192 storage values, all eight color clears, and both depth/stencil clears; it also checks formats remain `260/260` and neither pipeline fails. The test runs through both pipeline compilers, both encoding modes and both TBDR settings. AddressSanitizer reproduces the old 56-byte write past the 504-byte ledger and passes after the fix (renderer/test TU instrumented; NVN linker-section registration helpers kept uninstrumented).
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md), iPad runtime watchpoint and fixed-camera diagnostic comparison. Testing the rebuilt permanent correction on iPad remains open.

### Combined depth-stencil formats stay paired in passes and pipeline keys

- **Symptom:** Metal aborts at pipeline creation: `depthAttachmentPixelFormat (MTLPixelFormatInvalid) and stencilAttachmentPixelFormat (MTLPixelFormatDepth32Float_Stencil8) must match`.
- **Cause:** the renderer treated the two aspects of combined storage as independent attachment formats. `rp_begin_ex(3)` attached only stencil; `pso_predict_rest_` also enumerated a stencil-only `0/260` alternative, even with TBDR lookahead off. Historical prewarm keys retained those formats. macOS accepts that pipeline and pass; the reported iPadOS legacy pipeline validator rejects it. The original log has TBDR off and no attachment capture, so the exact live pass behind its synchronous `pso_get` stack is still unidentified; the speculative producer is established from the source, not inferred from the cutscene.
- **Fix:** a combined texture occupies both physical attachment slots whenever either aspect is needed. The unused plane is loaded and preserved, with its test and writes still disabled through the logical `g_pass.hd/hs` flags. Native depth-only and stencil-only textures occupy only their valid slots; sampling through `X32_Stencil8` is unchanged. `pso_ds_pair` gives live, predicted and parsed prewarm keys the same effective pair, without replacing any nonzero conflicting format. Repeated alternatives coalesce in the pipeline cache. `pso_build_ex` rejects conflicting or non-renderable depth/stencil formats with an error before either compiler path can assert. `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` includes `J` lines with the source NVN/view format, physical Metal formats, logical use, pass mode, pipeline key, cache hit and draw/prediction origin.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0100E95004038000 check_t_pso_depth_stencil`: synthetic shaders, native depth-only/stencil-only, both combined NVN formats, stencil-only/depth-only usage, omission of both planes, attachment-free draws, historical keys, format transitions, cache hits, speculation and invalid descriptors. Literal GPU readbacks check that the unused plane keeps its poisoned value. Both pipeline compilers and both encoding modes run under Metal validation. `t_rt_range` accounts for the retained stencil plane and still checks identical colour/depth/stencil and occlusion results.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md), [issue #702](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/702) (the reporter's log, not independent confirmation). The reporter confirms the descriptor-guard build reaches cutscenes and gameplay on iPad; its subsequent character smear is traced to [the attachment-ledger overflow](#ten-attachment-records-for-eight-colors-and-depth-stencil). Playing and skipping the opening with the complete correction remain separate device checks. Attaching the unused plane can add traffic in opt-in TBDR single-aspect passes; the default full-attachment path does not acquire another attachment.

### No writes to colour outputs the shader does not write

- **Symptom:** undefined values blended into attachments the fragment shader leaves alone.
- **Cause:** Maxwell leaves targets outside the shader's output map untouched; on Metal such an attachment gets undefined values blended in.
- **Fix:** pipelines get a zero write mask and no blending for colour outputs the shader does not write (reflection `colors`, `color_known` in `nvn_refl.m`).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md): volume draws after the G-buffer (`50d813761ca3f1f9` and others) write only outputs 0 and 2, with blending and all channels enabled on targets 1 and 3.
- **Check:** all five games' regressions were unchanged by it.

### No writes to colour components outside the shader's output map

- **Symptom:** undefined values in the components of a target the fragment shader does not write: a NaN or garbage alpha (or G, B) that later passes, such as bloom, spread.
- **Cause:** the SPH's output map (word 18, 4 bits per colour output, R G B A from the low bit) names the components the GPU writes; it leaves the others of a bound target as they were. The translator stores only the mapped components, but Metal writes the whole attachment under its write mask, the unstored components undefined. [The entry above](#no-writes-to-colour-outputs-the-shader-does-not-write) covered only outputs with no component in the map.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` `ps_omap` records a pixel shader's output map in its reflection (`raster.omap`, Maxwell's 0x50-byte SPH and NVN2's 0x80-byte one alike); `pso_desc` ANDs each target's write mask with its output's nibble, and `first_draw_covers` proves a load skippable only when the map covers every channel the target's format stores (`pf_chan_bits`). Libraries of an older chain have no map and keep the channel mask alone. `t_omap_mask` checks both Metal pipeline paths.
- **Found on:** reported by Lichii, a Switch 2 emulator, on [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (their commit `79c1516`): a depth-only visibility draw with target 0 still bound left a band of NaN and red / blue above the horizon, which bloom spread into violet halos. Ocarina's agl_common depth pass maps output 0 RGBA and output 2 R only (map 0x10f, `TranslatorContext.GetDepthRegister`). Not reproduced here (open).

### The alpha test through a library of its own

- **Symptom:** foliage, hair and fences drawn as solid quads (the parts the texture leaves transparent filled with its colour or black), dissolve effects solid instead of breaking up; once per run `SetAlphaRef …: the host has no alpha test`.
- **Cause:** `nvnColorStateSetAlphaTest` and `nvnCommandBufferSetAlphaRef` select Maxwell's fixed-function alpha test: the alpha a pixel shader writes to colour 0 is compared with the reference before blending, and the fragment is dropped when the comparison fails. Metal has none, and the host recorded the function without applying it and only logged the reference.
- **Fix:** every pixel shader whose colour 0 has a float alpha gets an alpha test entry point beside each of its own in its side library `<key>_bx` ([One compile for a pixel shader's side entry points](shaders.md#one-compile-for-a-pixel-shaders-side-entry-points), `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_alpha.py`): `main0_a`, and `main0_u_a` / `main0_m_a` / `main0_d_a` where the shader has them, each running the body, taking the function and the reference from fragment buffer `DMSH_ALPHA_BUFFER` (30) and testing colour 0's alpha, `discard_fragment()` when it fails. `SetAlphaRef` is recorded (`OP_ALPHA_REF`). A pipeline drawn with a function other than `ALWAYS` has `pso_key.atest` set and takes its fragment from that library (`atest_key`, `at_fn`, loaded on first need); its draws bind the function and reference, a copy in the frame arena per value and pass (`at_bind`). The function and the reference are data, so one pipeline serves every function and a reference that changes every draw keeps it; draws without the test keep the base library, whose size and load time do not change. As in OpenGL, there is no test when colour 0 goes to an integer target. `[[early_fragment_tests]]` stays in the variant: such a shader has its depth written before the test, on Maxwell too. Alpha-tested draws count as discarding for the first-draw load elision (`first_draw_covers`). Prewarm rows written before the key field read as alpha test off (`pso_skey_parse`). Not covered: a readable-tile merged draw (`bf_fn`) draws without the test, with a red line naming the shader. `t_alpha.m` replays every function against references 0, 0.3125, 0.5, 1 and NaN over an alpha ramp, and `test_dmsh_alpha.py` checks the entry points and compiles them for the Mac and the iPhone.
The Vulkan replay has no side libraries: `dm/android/spv_alpha.h` rewrites the fragment module once, adding a new entry point whose body calls the shader's original entry point as a function and then compares colour 0's alpha with the function and the reference, discarding the fragment when it fails. The shader's own returns are left alone: rewriting them broke structured control flow. The function is a specialization constant (`SpecId` 0, set by `VkSpecializationInfo`), so one module serves every function; the reference arrives in a four-byte fragment push constant set at each draw (`vkCmdPushConstants`), so it is data and is not part of the pipeline key. Only an Output variable decorated Location 0 is the colour (an Input may share the location), the types the test needs are added when the shader lacks them, and as on Metal there is no test when colour 0 goes to an integer target. A shader whose earlier stage leaves inputs unwritten is transformed first (`spv_drop_inputs`), and one alpha variant is kept per inputs variant; a shader the rewrite does not apply to is remembered as such, so no pipeline retries it. A depth-only pass runs the test too, as Metal does (shadow maps of foliage rely on it): the test reads what the shader writes to colour 0 whether or not the pass keeps it. `spv_alpha.h` is self-contained (no libc), so `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_alpha.py` runs the transform on the PC over synthetic shaders and a game's packed ones, with `spirv-val` checking each result, and the Android CI runs it.
- **Found on:** reported on [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md) (grass and ferns in Firelink Shrine and Darkroot Garden, PR #32), [ASTRAL CHAIN](../../dm/games/01007300020FA000/README.md) (the death effect's tendrils, `EffectOpacityBlood`, solid black) and [Animal Crossing: New Horizons](../../dm/games/01006F8002326000/README.md) (the island map, issue #73, PR #430), each with an implementation of its own; not yet checked in those games with this one.

### Logic operations through a library of its own

- **Symptom:** data a game builds in a target by combining draws comes out wrong; in Xenoblade Chronicles 2 indoor lighting has hard-edged dark boxes (world-space boxes in perspective, darker with the time of day) and lighter translucent rectangles elsewhere; in Skyward Sword HD a brown cast covers the distance and near objects turn into dark silhouettes, coming and going with the camera. A pass dump shows `lo=7` (or any value other than 3) on the `D` lines of the draws that write the target.
- **Cause:** `nvnColorStateSetLogicOp` makes the hardware combine the bits a pixel shader writes with the bits already in the target (CLEAR, AND, …, OR, …, SET) in place of blending; as in OpenGL and Vulkan it acts on integer and normalized targets. Metal has none, and the host recorded the operation without applying it, so every draw wrote with COPY. Xenoblade Chronicles 2 builds a per-pixel list of up to four light probes in an RG32Uint target: each probe's box writes its index into the next free byte with OR, and every other fragment writes 0 and relies on OR to leave the list as it is. With COPY each fragment replaced the list, so inside every probe volume the indirect-lighting pass read the wrong probes or none. Skyward Sword HD clears its RGBA8Unorm frame's alpha to 0, then `Dof_FogToAlpha` draws `(0, 0, 0, fog)` with OR and every channel enabled, to put fog in the alpha for the depth of field: OR with 0 keeps the colours, and COPY blacked them out.
- **Fix:** every pixel shader with colour outputs gets logic operation entry points in its side library `<key>_bx` ([One compile for a pixel shader's side entry points](shaders.md#one-compile-for-a-pixel-shaders-side-entry-points), `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_logic.py`): `main0_l`, `main0_u_l` and `main0_m_l` run the body, read each output's attachment (`[[color(n)]]`, framebuffer fetch) and combine it with the output by the operation and the target's channel masks from fragment buffer `DMSH_LOGIC_BUFFER` (29). A float output goes through its normalized target's stored bits: rounded to them as the target would store it, combined, and normalized again (`dm_logic_norm`); masks of zero, on a target with no such bits, pass it through. Every shader has the entry points, not a listed few, because which ones draw with an operation shows only in play (Skyward Sword HD: a second shader, `eb1648acad4e38e3`, in the Knight Academy); the side library is loaded only on first need. Each read depends on the function constant `DMSH_LOGIC_FC + n` (Metal refuses a pipeline that reads an output whose target the pass lacks); the host specializes the entry per set of targets and keeps each (`lo_fn`). A pipeline drawn with an operation other than COPY into an unsigned integer or normalized target has `pso_key.logic` set (`lop_key`; the field was padding, so older prewarm rows read it as off), blending off, since the operation takes its place, and its draws bind the operation and the masks (8, 16 or 32 bits a channel, RGB10A2's 10 and 2, the 5, 6 and 1 of the packed formats), a copy in the frame arena per value and pass (`lo_bind`). The operation is data, so one pipeline serves every operation; draws with COPY keep the base library and read no attachment. Logic-op draws count as reading the target for the first-draw load elision (`first_draw_covers`) and end a readable-tile merged pass before them. Not covered, with a red line: a floating-point, sRGB, signed normalized or signed integer target, a draw with an alpha test as well (no entry point does both), and a merged pass's first phase. `t_logic.m` replays all 16 operations into RG32Uint, RGBA8Uint, RGBA8Unorm and RGBA16Unorm targets; `test_dmsh_logic.py` checks the entry points and compiles them for the Mac and the iPhone.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#rendering): the probe-list writers, `shd_lgt.wishp` var3 (`ef07fd3d5046164f`, `aa9a0923524b7e46`, `2717a0aa47361bbd`, `d95874c161deb77d`, `f24b58784a33adea`), read by the indirect-lighting pass `93d0e7a7c14cdfb0` (GitHub issue #643). Normalized targets: [The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword HD](../../dm/games/01002DA013484000/README.md#rendering), `f11c15a99b0c1790` (GitHub issue #928; the cause and the approach were first reported by Zatteo in issue #497).

### Advanced blend modes as Porter-Duff factors

- **Symptom:** `nvnBlendStateSetAdvancedMode 8` (SRC_OVER) logged and blended with the state's own functions.
- **Cause:** the hardware ignores the blend state's own functions while an advanced mode is set.
- **Fix:** the blend state keeps the mode, the premultiplied-source flag and the overlap (`blend_st` in `nvn_obj.h`), and the pipeline (`adv_blend` in `nvn_mtl.m`) blends the Porter-Duff modes (ZERO, SRC, DST, SRC/DST_OVER, _IN, _OUT, _ATOP, XOR, the PLUS modes) with the fixed-function factors they amount to under uncorrelated overlap, with premultiplied colours as NV_blend_equation_advanced defines them; a source that is not premultiplied is multiplied by As where the source factor is ONE. Multiply, screen, overlay, the HSL modes and the other overlaps need the destination colour in the shader and keep the state's functions, with a red line naming the mode.
- **Found on:** [Mario vs. Donkey Kong](../../dm/games/0100B99019412000/README.md) (SRC_OVER, on one blend state in the levels).

### Depth clamp

- **Fix:** `SetDepthClamp` is recorded (`OP_DEPTH_CLAMP`) and replayed as Metal's depth clip mode (it used to be ignored).
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md) (the game turns it on).

### Telling depth rejection from no rasterization

- `DM_NVN_DEPTHOVR` (depth test forced to `ALWAYS`) with `HILITE` tells "rejected by depth" from "never rasterized". `TEXSNAP` copies used to run before the pass they were meant to show (classic-queue command buffer committed ahead of the Metal 4 one); they now go through the replay's copy encoder.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md), tracing the tile lighting: the G-buffer class byte selects per-class half-resolution passes through a depth buffer of class values (`gl_FragDepth = mix(0.5, 1, nibble-swapped class / 255)`, then full-screen or per-tile quads with depth `EQUAL`).

### Stencil-mode texture views read through `X32_Stencil8`

- **Symptom:** striped lighting: black and white bands that follow depth on every lit surface, as if every pixel were in or out of shadow at random.
- **Cause:** a shader that reads a depth-stencil texture through a view whose `nvnTextureViewSetDepthStencilMode` is `STENCIL`: the host ignored the mode and bound depth, so a stencil test read bits of the depth value's float bits.
- **Fix:** `tex_by_id` binds an `X32_Stencil8` view for such a view. The shader declares a float texture (the translator has no integer texture type) and takes the raw bits with `as_type<uint>`, and a read of the integer view gives the stencil value in those bits. Metal allows such a view only of a texture with the pixel-format-view usage: Apple GPUs read it correctly without, but the API validation layer aborts at the first one (PR #106, Luigi's Mansion 3). Upgrading the texture when the view appears recreated the depth buffer while it was attached, and the next occlusion-counted draw crashed inside `setVisibilityResultMode`, so every depth-stencil texture has that usage from the start (`tex_desc`; Vulkan depth-stencil images too, `vkCreateImage`), which costs nothing measured (on an M4 Pro the same allocated size and GPU time); `t_stencil_view` checks it under validation.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md): the deferred lighting pixel shader (`ead6e2a32540b19a`, 31 bindings) first reads the depth-stencil buffer through such a view, tests stencil bit 6 and discards. [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md) (PR #106, the validation abort).
- **Note:** inputs ruled out on the way in Z-A: the G-buffer albedo, the normals (12-bit pairs packed into 3 bytes, noisy by design), the screen-space shadow mask, the scaled depth copies and conditional rendering (`render_cond` changes nothing in this game and stays off).

### Stencil-mode textures from the texture builder

- **Symptom:** a character drawn as a dithered silhouette inside a dithered square (DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's hero).
- **Cause:** Unreal's stencil shader resources are second textures on the depth buffer's storage built with `nvnTextureBuilderSetDepthStencilMode(STENCIL)`, which the host only logged, so the character occlusion mask read depth where it tests stencil bits.
- **Fix:** the builder's mode now goes to the texture (`tex_t.stencil`), whose sampling view is the `X32_Stencil8` one a stencil-mode texture view already got.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md) (the character occlusion mask, `edbfc7a088be545e`).

### Raw depth/stencil storage written as a buffer

- **Symptom:** indoor lights flicker, the scene switching between bright and dark from frame to frame while the camera stands still, on the Mac and on Android, on every driver, with occlusion queries off too.
- **Cause:** a compute pass writes a depth-stencil texture's stencil through a storage buffer laid over the texture's storage. The hosts keep depth and stencil in a Metal or Vulkan image of their own, so those writes land in the pool's buffer and never reach the image, and the draws that sample the stencil read whatever an earlier pass left in it. The stencil-mode view is right ([Stencil-mode texture views read through `X32_Stencil8`](#stencil-mode-texture-views-read-through-x32_stencil8)): it is the plane's contents that are stale.
- **Fix / limitation:** keeping raw buffer writes and a depth-stencil image in step is open: neither API exposes the GPU's block-linear depth-stencil layout to write it from. For Skyrim a build-gated cut makes the scene initializer's own depth clear also clear the stencil, which puts in the image the upper bits the compute pass writes. The dispatch, ordinary depth-only clears, the interface and the shadows are unchanged. What the compute writes is one 16-byte pattern over the whole storage, a 4x4 ordered-dither matrix of even values 0–30 (`{20, 4, 16, 0, 12, 28, 8, 24, 18, 2, 22, 6, 10, 26, 14, 30}`), so its upper three bits are 0 everywhere and only the dither in the lower five is not carried; no draw of the frames dumped in Helgen (outdoors, the tower, the keep's first room with the first-person model) tests or reads those bits.
- **Found on:** [The Elder Scrolls V: Skyrim](../../dm/games/01000A10041EA000/README.md#helgen-keeps-lighting) (compute `a76fe13a8630d619`, shadow mask `f83614ab6954bec4`; droidplay-core#444), found on 1.0.12 by seedds/nxnative PR #444.

### Conservative rasterization remains unimplemented

- **Symptom:** `[nvn] SetConservativeRasterEnable on: ignored by host for now`, followed by `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetConservativeRasterDilate`.
- **Cause:** `hle_nvn.c` ignores the enable setter and has no implementation for the dilation setter. The requested raster coverage state never reaches Metal.
- **Fix:** **Open.** Neither setter has been implemented. The opening indoor scene renders and accepts input, but the visual contribution of the missing coverage state has not been isolated. The diagnostic remains visible.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Scarlet](../../dm/games/0100A3D008C5C000/README.md#open) (1.0.0, the first playable bedroom). [Super Mario RPG](../../dm/games/0100BC0018138000/README.md) calls the dilation setter and `nvnCommandBufferSetSubpixelPrecisionBias` repeatedly and did not turn the enable on along the route tested, so the dilation has nothing to act on there.

## Vertex input and draws

### Integer vertex inputs

- **Symptom:** characters (skinned models) invisible or stretched into streaks while the level renders; `vertex attribute format … does not match shader input type …` for a remaining mismatch.
- **Cause:** integer vertex inputs (bone indices) translated as float: without PC reflection the translator declares every vertex input `float4`. Skinned models read their bone index (`R8UI` / `RG8UI` / `RGBA8UI`) as raw integer bits (`as_type<int>(in.m_N.x) * 48`); Metal converted the integers to floats, and every bone index was garbage.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` (`int_inputs`) now re-translates a vertex shader with `uint` inputs when every use of an input is an integer bit cast (529 of 4,854 in Mario), and the host makes the Metal vertex format agree with the declared type (`vfmt_for_ct`; signedness swaps of 32-bit components and 32-bit float→int reinterpretation keep the bits, while float-type formats and 8- and 16-bit integers of the other sign go through [shadow vertex inputs](#shadow-vertex-inputs), next entry). A remaining mismatch is reported as `vertex attribute format … does not match shader input type …`.
- **Check:** finding the draws: log model file ranges with `DM_PROBE=main:480f0` (x1 = file data, x2 = size), match the vertex/index buffer addresses in `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL` against them, then `DM_NVN_HILITE` their fragment shader.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (skinned characters).

### Shadow vertex inputs

- **Symptom:** red lines 89 and 96 (`vconv_bind`, the CPU conversion every draw); about a quarter of Age of Calamity's replay thread in battle.
- **Cause:** an input that `int_inputs` declares an integer may still be fed a format of another class. A float-type format (half, unorm / snorm, 10:10:10:2, 11:11:10) leaves the converted float32 bits in the register; an 8- or 16-bit integer of the other signedness is widened by the format's own sign (Fire Emblem: Three Houses, [CPU conversion of vertex attributes the input type cannot take](#cpu-conversion-of-vertex-attributes-the-input-type-cannot-take)). Metal reads a format only into an input of its own class, so the replay used to convert those attributes on the CPU every draw (`vconv_bind`).
- **Fix:** shadow inputs (`<key>_bv`, `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_vin.py`): the build also writes a side library for each such vertex shader. In it, every integer input is declared again as a `float4` and as the integer of the other sign, at Metal attribute indices 16–30 (NVN uses 0–15), and the body starts with `in.m_N = dm_in.m_N | as_type<T>(dm_in.m_N_vf) | as_type<T>(dm_in.m_N_vo)`. The host sends each attribute to the index of its format's class and the zero buffer to the others (`vin_ct`, `vin_on` in `nvn_mtl.m`; pipeline keys unchanged), so a pipeline with any mix of formats reads Maxwell's register bits. The float shadows are assigned first: with more than 7 integer inputs the last ones get only the float shadow (169 of 8,201 inputs in Age of Calamity).
- **Note:** a format with no shadow keeps the CPU conversion, as do transform feedback draws (`main0_tf` has no variant) and `DM_NVN_VCONV_CPU=1`. The host loads a side library only for a pipeline that routes an attribute to a shadow, but the build cannot tell which shaders will need one (an input used only as an integer may hold bone indices or float bits), so it writes them all. Shadows cover only integer inputs, so 8- and 16-bit integer formats feeding float inputs still go through the CPU.
- **Found on:**
  - [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md): 2,259 libraries, 77 MB each for Mac and iOS. Its Mac battle at 60 fps against the CPU conversion: 0 vertices converted instead of 545,000 per submission, submit + present 10.0 → 5.7 ms a frame, CPU 3.97 → 2.44 W, GPU unchanged, the same picture.
  - Other games have 0–2,000 libraries (at most 31 MB on iOS), except [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md): 16,781 libraries, 344 MB on iOS (21% of its shader libraries, 1.5% of its IPA), and its Mac and iPhone runs so far load none. Its 8- and 16-bit integer formats feeding float inputs (red line 99, about 60,000 vertices per submission on its Mac route into the opening) still go through the CPU.

### Pulling vertex inputs

- **Cause:** a pipeline's vertex descriptor fixes the vertex layout it is drawn with, so a shader pair drawn with a layout it has no pipeline for waits until one is compiled.
- **Fix:** pulling inputs (`<key>_bp`, `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_pull.py`, decoder `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_pull.metal`): a side library of every vertex shader with stage inputs, whose `main0` / `main0_z` have no `[[stage_in]]`. They read their inputs themselves from a per-draw layout block at buffer 16 (`dm_vlayout`): per stream the base address, stride and divisor, and per NVN attribute the raw attribute state word. The base instance comes from `[[base_instance]]`, so indirect draws are right too. `dm_fetch` decodes the NVN format at run time into the register words Maxwell's fetch gives the shader, and the input takes them with `as_type`. A pipeline made with it has no vertex descriptor, so it serves every vertex layout a shader pair is drawn with; it is meant for drawing while the pipeline for the draw's own layout is still being built.
- **Note:** the words are bit-identical to Metal's vertex-descriptor fetch for every format `vfmt` knows, and to the host's CPU conversion for the integer-read-as-float formats. unorm / snorm go through the `unpack_*` builtins, because a plain division under fast math is off by one unit in the last place for some values; NaNs of R11G11B10F come out canonical, as from Metal's fetch. With a divisor the element is `base instance + (instance - base instance) / divisor`, Metal's own rule. Positions match the vertex-descriptor pipeline's (`[[invariant]]`, preserved invariance).
- **Note:** the run-time format decode costs GPU time: on an M2 Max, 8 attributes in a vertex-bound draw of tiny visible triangles took 65 % longer (about 1.0 ms per million vertices) than with the vertex descriptor. With the formats as compile-time constants it was 9 %, and with the whole layout compiled in, 0. So these pipelines only stand in until the fast one is built. Other entry points (transform feedback, geometry capture, layer) get no variant; shaders without stage inputs need none, since their pipelines have no vertex descriptor.
- **Symptom:** an unsigned iOS build reports `[RED] pulling library ... not usable (no library ...)` and its draws wait for pipelines, either with the profile's `pso_mtl4 on` or with **Metal 4 pipelines** and **Pipeline prebuild** turned on in the launcher for a game whose profile leaves `pso_mtl4` off. Then the prebuild ends at once as if finished (`pipeline prebuild done: 7495 pairs, 0 bases built, 0 already archived, 7495 left to the draws, 0 failed, 0.6 s`), and the shader image holds `_bp_left_out` instead of the `_bp.metallib` files. Or packaging with `IOS_PULL=1` fails with `SKIPS[@]: unbound variable`.
- **Cause:** the IPA packer used to strip `_bp.metallib` unless `IOS_PULL=1`, independently of the runtime's profile default. It later followed the profile's `pso_mtl4` (`build_ipa.sh`, and the NSP script's `ios_wants_pulling` with `DMSH_IOS_PULL=0`, so `dmsh_build.py` did not compile them either), as if nothing could read them with the key off. But the launcher offers the **Metal 4 pipelines** toggle for every NVN game, with the profile's key as its default only, and with it the device's prebuild and hybrid pipelines read the pulling libraries. The inclusion path also expanded an empty array under `set -u`, which macOS Bash 3.2 treats as unset.
- **Fix:** an iOS build keeps the pulling libraries whatever the profile's `pso_mtl4` says (`build_ipa.sh`, `ios_wants_pulling`); `IOS_PULL=0` leaves them out, for a smaller IPA whose launcher toggles then build no bases. An IPA that packs a [pipeline archive made offline](../development.md#the-pipeline-archive-made-offline) (`pso/`), or an Xcode project, always carries them, whatever `IOS_PULL` says, since the archive's bases are made of them. An image built without them (`_bp_left_out`) is built again at the next shader step. Packing options always contain `--store`, so both inclusion and exclusion work under Bash 3.2. A device that already wrote its prebuild stamp from an app without the libraries, on the same chain version, runs the prebuild again once the app carries them: [A prebuild stamp written without the pulling libraries](shaders.md#a-prebuild-stamp-written-without-the-pulling-libraries).
- **Measured:** the pulling libraries are 4–10 % of a game's library bytes (in the Mac images: Fire Emblem: Three Houses 10 MB, Luigi's Mansion 3 22 MB, OCTOPATH TRAVELER II 62 MB, Minecraft Dungeons II 137 MB). On the device they are worth the size: Echoes of Wisdom with both toggles on, without them on an iPhone 17, compiled 564 pipelines during play (23.3 s, the worst frame 2.6 s); with them on an iPad mini 6, its prebuild built 7,488 bases in 513 s once, and play compiled none.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_pull.m` (the words against Metal's fetch and the CPU conversion), `t_pull_inv.m` (a depth prepass with one pipeline and depth-equal with the other), `test_dmsh_pull.py`; `dm/ios/tests/test_profile_pack.py` packs tiny fixtures through the actual archive step and checks runtime-prebuild library inclusion, explicit overrides and an IPA with a pipeline archive in the ZIP.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): draws that waited for their pipeline on a cold start. [The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom](../../dm/games/01008CF01BAAC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): an iPhone build whose launcher toggles built no bases (issue #1684).

### Float32 vertex padding through integer inputs

- **Symptom:** a fullscreen post-process draw writes no color, then a later blur composite darkens scenery while the character remains visible.
- **Cause:** reinterpreting Float1/2/3 as UInt1/2/3 preserves supplied bits but pads missing `w` with integer `1`. A shader that bitcasts the input back to float receives `w=1.4e-45`, rather than `1.0`, and can clip the whole draw. Float4 has no missing components.
- **Fix:** short float32 vectors use the existing float shadow input, or CPU expansion with float `(0,0,0,1)` defaults when shadows are unavailable or disabled. Float4 keeps direct reinterpretation. `t_float_pad` checks signed/unsigned GPU fetch, CPU fallback and Float4 controls.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles: Definitive Edition](../../dm/games/0100FF500E34A000/README.md) (the game profile is a separate, pending contribution).

### CPU conversion of vertex attributes the input type cannot take

- **Symptom:** `Vertex attribute m_36(5) of type uint4 cannot be read using MTLAttributeFormatHalf2` (draws dropped: black silhouettes); UVs collapse onto one texel (flat tan characters); vertices far out of view (negative 16-bit values widened with zeros).
- **Cause:** `int_inputs` declares an input `uint` when its first translation only bit-casts it, and the shader then takes it back with `as_type<float>`. Maxwell's fetch converts by the format and the register holds the float32 bits, and widens integers by the format's signedness, whatever type the shader gives the register. Metal refuses a float-type format for an integer input; reading the raw bytes as integers gives denormals. For a signed format on an unsigned input `vfmt_for_ct` swapped `Short4` for `UShort4`, which keeps the 16 bits but widens them with zeros, so every negative value read as 32,768–65,535.
- **Fix:** the replay converts such attributes (half, unorm / snorm 8 and 16, 10:10:10:2, 11:11:10, BGRA8 into an int / uint input) on the CPU into float32 bits, four components with the fetch's (0, 0, 0, 1.0) defaults, in the frame arena, and binds them as UInt4 / Int4 on buffer slots 1–12 (`vconv_bind`, once per range and layout per submission; red line 89). 8- and 16-bit integer formats whose signedness differs from the input's go through the same conversion (`vconv_int` in `nvn_mtl.m`, red line 96 once): 32-bit values extended by the format's own sign, missing components (0, 0, 0, 1) as integers.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md): KTGL's character and soldier shaders, fed a Half2 (UVs); also [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md)'s soldiers (flat tan). The ground under close-up battle cameras was flat steel blue: the terrain's vertex shader (`56778feb1cfdee7e`) reads its grid position from an `RGBA16I` attribute (x and z the grid cell, y the height, which spans the whole int16 range), and so does the side pass that draws the river surface into targets of its own (`db725c85ca060403`).
- **Note:** [shadow vertex inputs](#shadow-vertex-inputs) (`dmsh_vin.py`) have since replaced both conversions; they remain for the formats shadow inputs cannot take (and for transform feedback draws and `DM_NVN_VCONV_CPU=1`), and the profile-side answer for one shader is [Integer-typed vertex inputs fed normalized formats](shaders.md#integer-typed-vertex-inputs-fed-normalized-formats).
- **Note:** how the steel-blue ground was traced, since two steps misled: where the ground was missing nothing wrote the depth (reversed-Z, cleared to 0), the lighting takes depth 0 for sky and paints the fog colour, and the never-cleared G-buffer colour targets kept an old image there (cobblestones of a mesh under the terrain, which from the map camera showed through the gaps like a road). A `HILITE` colour over the gap was stale too (the never-cleared albedo), and the end-of-frame depth showed 0 there. Recomputing every draw's clip positions from `CBDUMP` data showed that no draw of the shader reached the gap; then the side pass's layer, which showed only scattered tiles of the river, led to the vertex format. Ruled out on the way: depth clamp (Metal's clip and clamp modes gave the same depth), the pass without a depth attachment (142595 is a screen-space decal pass: it samples the depth and writes albedo and normals), polygon offset (none), dropped draws.
- **Note:** to confirm in Three Houses: 64-pixel rectangles in the albedo where decals land. The decal pass (`417a523053457352`, slots 16 and 18) and the lighting (`094a2d11253f126c`, `f54830a75e07e14a`, slots 18–20) read their decal and light lists from texture buffers that the translation declared `texture2d`, because those handles have no PC type (`texture type mismatch: shader declares 2, texture is 9`); skipping the decal pass removed the rectangles. Since row wrap ([Buffer textures read by 1D integer fetches (row wrap)](#buffer-textures-read-by-1d-integer-fetches-row-wrap), general) they read the real lists: slot 18 holds 1,052,640 elements, 65 rows. On the Mac prologue route the torches and burning houses now light the battle map and the close-up ground (they lit nothing before), and the blood decals take the scene's light instead of showing as dark blue patches. The rectangles did not appear on that route in either build (no 64-pixel structure in the close-up frames), so the scene where they were seen still has to be checked.

### More than 16 vertex attributes

- **Symptom:** `[RED] BindVertexAttribState n=17`, tens of thousands of times, and a scene that draws wrong or not at all: the bind was refused, so the draws kept the previous layout.
- **Cause:** NVN has 16 vertex attributes and NVN2 32, and every table in the host held 16.
- **Fix:** the attribute tables hold 32 (`p_bind_vattr`, the replay's state), and so does the answer to the device query. The pipeline key keeps the first 16 where they were and adds `attr_hi` at its end, so a prewarm row written before reads as 0 (`pso_skey_parse`); `dmsh_pull.py` and `dmsh_vin.py` leave out such shaders as they did, and they take the ordinary path. Vertex bindings stay 16.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): Unreal Engine 5.4's vertex factories use attribute 16 on stream 6.

### Vertex formats Metal lacks

- **Symptom:** `vertex attribute format … not mapped` (printed once).
- **Cause / Fix:** Mario uses `RGB10A2SN` (0x69) → `MTLVertexFormatInt1010102Normalized`. Unmapped formats fall back to a 4-component read and print the line once. The integer-read-as-float formats (`*_UI2F` / `*_I2F`, 0x51–0x68), which Metal has no vertex format for, are converted on the CPU at replay like the other conversions (`vconv_i2f`).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (`RGB10A2SN`); [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) has an `RG8_UI2F` attribute.

### Null vertex buffers bound past stream 15

- **Symptom:** `[RED] BindVertexBuffers first=16 n=1` … `first=31`, thousands of times a run and no visible fault.
- **Cause:** [Mario Tennis Fever](../../dm/games/0400D4201C6EA000/README.md) clears 32 streams before each model, while NVN has 16.
- **Fix:** a null range past stream 15 is dropped silently, as nothing can read it (`hle_nvn.c`); a real one still prints a red line, limited to 8.
- **Found on:** [Mario Tennis Fever](../../dm/games/0400D4201C6EA000/README.md).

### Vertex streams 14 and 15 at Metal slots 14 and 15

- **Symptom:** the assertion `buffer index (31) must be < 31`.
- **Cause:** the host bound NVN stream *n* to Metal vertex buffer 16 + *n*, but Metal has slots 0–30 only (30 is the host's constant fallback), so stream 15 went to 31.
- **Fix:** translated shaders use only `buffer(0)`, so streams 14 and 15 now go to slots 14 and 15 (`VB_SLOT`).
- **Found on:** [13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim](../../dm/games/01003FC01670C000/README.md) (a minute and a half into the prologue).

### Vertex streams at addresses that are not 4-byte aligned

- **Symptom:** Scaleform fills come out as solid white or light grey sheets over the whole screen, hard-edged triangles and bands, and the menu text is missing (a language list or a title screen that is all white behind its button prompt).
- **Cause:** Metal reads a vertex buffer only from a 4-byte-aligned address: bound at an address 2 mod 4, every vertex was read 2 bytes early. NVN takes any address, and Scaleform's vertex streams (16-byte vertices: two RGBA8 attributes and an RG32F position) sit at addresses such as `…6d6a`. The positions became garbage spanning the screen, and the shapes, white with alpha 0.2–0.7 by their colour transforms, overlapped many times.
- **Fix:** `bind_vbufs` copies a stream whose address is not 4-byte aligned into the submission's arena (`vbuf_unaligned`; one copy per address and size until guest writes or pool mappings change, up to 64 MB) and binds the copy. CPU copies require a host-readable, non-sparse range without pending GPU writers in this or an earlier submission; otherwise `vbuf_prepare` encodes an ordered GPU copy before the render encoder, including feedback and geometry captures. Submissions and pool mappings invalidate copies; buffer and shader writes invalidate the copies they can overlap, so unrelated uniform updates keep their vertex copies. The log's `vertex bindings` line counts the copies; `t_vbuf` checks byte-aligned readbacks, reuse, queued writes, writes between draws and pool replacement. The index buffers at addresses 2 mod 4 (16-bit indices) needed nothing.
- **Found on:** Batman: Arkham Knight (`0100ACD0163D0000`; its profile and note are in a PR of their own) (Scaleform GFx; 2–13 copies of 3–18 MB a frame in the menus and at the title, which the draws' vertex ranges could narrow). With the copy the language menu and the title screen draw right.

### Vertex fetches past a stream's bound size

- **Symptom:** hard-edged translucent slabs and triangles over the scene that move with the camera, where a full-screen pass blends an effect in (Super Mario Odyssey: white wedges through the Cascade Kingdom's waterfall mist, one edge through the centre of the screen).
- **Cause:** NVN binds each vertex stream with a size (`nvnCommandBufferBindVertexBuffers`), and Maxwell's fetch reads zeros past it: a draw may ask for more vertices than the stream holds, and those vertices collapse into degenerate triangles. Metal's fetch has no such limit and read on into whatever followed the stream in the pool. agl's half-resolution composite draws `DrawArrays(TRIANGLES, 0, 9)` from a 36-byte stream holding one full-screen triangle; vertices 6–8 read (0, 0), (0, 1), (0.98, 0.20) from the pool's next bytes, a second triangle that blended the mist in twice. A numpy model of the composite from the frame's inputs matched the GPU's output everywhere but there, where the output matched the composite applied twice (1 % against 39 % error).
- **Fix:** before the draw's render encoder, `vbuf_prepare` takes the inputs the vertex shader reads and the draw's last element per stream (`vstream_last`: the last vertex of a direct draw, the last instance of a per-instance stream, element 0 of a stride-0 stream), and where that reaches past the bound size (`vbuf_need`, element sizes from `vattr_bytes`), binds the stream as a copy of its bound bytes followed by zeros up to the last byte read. The copy is `vbuf_unaligned`'s (per submission, CPU or ordered GPU copy); the vertex table, the pulling pipelines' layout and transform feedback take it, and the CPU-converted inputs (`vconv_bind`) convert zero bytes past the bound range. `t_vbuf` reads the zeros back on the GPU; `t_pull` checks `vattr_bytes` against Metal's element sizes.
- **Open:** per-vertex streams of indexed draws, indirect draws and the batched geometry captures are not bounded (their last element is not known on the CPU without scanning the indices or reading the arguments).
- **Absent streams (resolved, found by clang-tidy):** CPU conversion already binds zero for a stream outside the sixteen stream slots, but its range query indexed `S.stream` first. `vstream_last` now returns unknown before accessing an absent slot; `t_vbuf` checks both boundary indices. No game failure was observed.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) (GitHub issue #558): the composite of agl's half-resolution translucent pass (`alProgramShader`, vertex `40cc99352976d0ff`, pixel `934386ce027e72be`), every frame the half-resolution effects draw.

### A vertex stream in no memory pool

- **Symptom:** `[RED] vertex stream N's buffer 0x… is in no memory pool; draw skipped` (`; its attributes read zeros` for a pulling pipeline, `; a geometry capture reads the buffer bound before` for one). Before the line existed: a draw whose vertices came from an earlier draw's buffer, with nothing in the log.
- **Cause:** the host failed to place a non-zero stream address in any memory pool it tracks (a pool it missed, or an address the game computed wrongly; on the Switch the GPU would fault). `bind_vbufs` skipped such a stream, so its Metal slot kept the buffer an earlier draw had bound there. A stream with address 0 is different: the vertex descriptor points its attributes at the zero buffer (`ZERO_ATTR`).
- **Fix:** `bind_vbufs` reports the first stream the vertex shader reads (`vs_streams`, from its reflection) that is in no pool, after binding the others. The draw is skipped with the red line, as for an index buffer in no pool, and so are a transform feedback capture and the vertex dump; a pulling pipeline's layout gives the stream's attributes zeros. A geometry capture shares its encoder with the job's later stages, which read what it writes, so it runs with the red line and the old binding. A stale address on a stream no input reads changes nothing. Not a fix for the address itself: the red line is there to find which pool the host missed.
- **Found on:** no game has shown the line. The S2Arm project reported the stale binding on its copy of this layer. `t_vbuf` checks that a read stream in no pool is reported and the others still bound, and that an unread one is not.

### Programs bound per stage

- **Symptom:** scenes and characters black, only foliage, light shafts and the HUD drawn; pass dumps full of `Z … draw skipped: no program bound`.
- **Cause:** NVN's `BindProgram(program, stageBits)` binds per stage, and a null program unbinds just those stages. Metroid Dread's depth prepass calls `BindProgram(program, vertex | fragment)`, then `BindProgram(null, fragment)`, and draws depth with the vertex shader alone. The host replaced its single bound program on every bind, so a null bind meant "no program bound", and all of the prepass's ~140 draws per frame were dropped. The depth buffer stayed empty, and the G-buffer passes, which test depth `EQUAL` without writing, passed no pixel.
- **Fix:** the replay tracks the program per stage (`bind_stages` in `nvn_mtl.m`). When the stages come from different programs, or the fragment stage is unbound, it builds a composite program, which gives a vertex-only pipeline for the prepass. Games that always bind every stage from one program (Mario, Engage) are unchanged.
- **Found on:** [Metroid Dread](../../dm/games/010093801237C000/README.md): the combinations it uses are (program, 0x3), (null, 0x2), (null, 0x7). [13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim](../../dm/games/01003FC01670C000/README.md) builds one program per GLSLC output and pairs them only through these composites ([Single-stage programs paired with a cover](shaders.md#single-stage-programs-paired-with-a-cover)).
- **Note:** pass dumps print a `Z` line wherever a draw is dropped (no program, no pipeline, empty scissor, both faces culled, no render pass). That is how this one was found: 144 `Z … draw skipped: no program bound` lines in a frame whose G-buffer pass had draws but stayed black.

### Conditional rendering survives queue continuations

- **Symptom:** a draw disabled by `SetRenderEnable(false)` executes after an event or fence, or a conditional clear erases a target whose preceding timestamp report should make it skip.
- **Cause:** physical submission initialization reset the global render-enable state and discarded known pending counter values. A logical NVN queue can continue across several Metal submissions, including [event waits](nvn-objects.md#gpu-event-waits-order-work-across-queues); those boundaries do not reset its render state. Timestamp reports are written to guest memory at GPU completion, so reading their old CPU bytes before that completion also gives the wrong predicate.
- **Fix:** render-enable state belongs to the logical queue. Known timestamp counter values survive physical prefixes until their completion writes land. Completion expires that knowledge before an event release can wake a CPU writer; a later CPU rewrite therefore remains visible. Later inline updates, fills and copies replace overlapping counter bytes and carry the latest writer’s completion fence. Copy sources with unfinished or unknown GPU writes remain unknown. This preserves the existing conservative treatment of unavailable occlusion-query results.
- **Check:** `t_render_enable_queue` uses the public NVN API and pixel readback to cover event, signal, sync and fence boundaries, independent queues, pending reports behind a GPU gate, and a later CPU counter rewrite. Seven checks failed before the fix; all 38 pass in both encoding modes with Metal validation. `t_render_counter_overlap` adds checks for explicit updates, fills, copies, partial writes and a second fence; five predicates failed before the metadata fix and all pass in both encoding modes. Both tests turn the profile's `render_cond` on themselves, whatever profile `GAME` names: built for Super Mario Bros. Wonder (the profile CI names), which has it off, the conditions were ignored and they failed every run. This does not resolve the older CPU-post report emulation: a later GPU read can still observe the report’s previous bytes, and a late CPU report write can overwrite a later GPU write. The fixture prints those bytes as OPEN lines and checks the predicates (37 checks): it had checked them, and failed on the race in 3 of 60 runs.
- **Open:** reports written on the GPU in command order (a counter heap, a resolve and a kernel at the end of each submission, keeping each game's time values) were built and measured: correct bytes in every case, but about 0.5 to 0.7 ms more GPU a frame on Ocarina of Time (about 14 submissions a frame) and no game symptom traced to the order, so not landed. The patch is kept outside the repository; each report's own GPU time instead of the segment times broke Ocarina's dynamic resolution ([GPU timestamps per submission segment](frame-rate.md#gpu-timestamps-per-submission-segment)). The game note records visual validation.
- **Found on:** the queue-ordering investigation for [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#open), M4 Pro.

### Composite program taken again after a replay state restore

- **Symptom:** `[RED] failed to create render pipeline (vertex 790573a52b9c5053_b fragment f39fb42850f50ecd_b): Fragment input(s) user(locn0),user(locn1) … not written by vertex shader`, once on entering the first investigation; `texture type mismatch: shader declares a cube, texture is 2D` (a pixel shader run with another pair's bindings).
- **Cause:** a draw ran with the vertex shader of another Shader object (`Legacy Shaders/VertexLit`'s shadow caster with the pixel shader of `PSYNC/BG_AlphaTest`, whose own pass pairs it with `00b462fe0dec6e06`). Unity binds the vertex and fragment programs apart, so every draw's pair is a composite of `bind_stages`, one of a ring of 64.
