Other draws still wait for theirs. The cost is the compile in the frame that meets the pipeline: on an Ayn Odin 2 with an empty pipeline cache, the first two frames after Skyrim's load took 447 and 92 ms with 26 and 11 pipelines built at once, against 144 and 112 ms with 9 and 5 when the depth-equal re-draws waited (the start-up list builds them before play from the next session on). `DM_VK_ASYNC_EQUAL=1` leaves them out again, for an A/B; `DM_VK_ASYNC_DELAY_MS=<n>` (diagnostic) makes each background build wait that long first, which shows what a slow driver leaves out. The `[vk] pipelines met for the first time` line counts background builds, draws left out and the pipelines built at once by this rule.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_apipe_rule.py`. On the Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740, Qualcomm's driver E031.41.03.36) with an empty pipeline cache, no start-up list and `DM_VK_ASYNC_DELAY_MS=1500`: with the old rule (`DM_VK_ASYNC_EQUAL=1`) the view after the load was the clear colour (0.255, 0.317, 0.307) for seconds, the first-person hands drawn over it; with this rule the room and the hands were drawn from the first frame and only blended effects (the candles' glow) came late. On the Mac, leaving out the hands' colour draw (`DM_NVN_SKIP_FS=af549618df61ad30`) draws them as flat (59, 77, 74), the tester's (63, 77, 74).
- **Found on:** [The Elder Scrolls V: Skyrim](../../dm/games/01000A10041EA000/README.md#first-person-draws-left-out-while-their-pipeline-builds), with slow background builds forced on an Ayn Odin 2. First taken for droidplay-core#444's flat first-person model, which turned out to be the stale low-resolution depth.

### Depth-stencil copies a blit cannot make, drawn on Vulkan

- **Symptom:** Android only: `[RED] [vk] texture copy with a format change or scaling between formats 130 and 130: not blittable on this GPU`, and whatever reads the
  copy's destination wrong: an effect that tests its stencil draws nothing (Link's Awakening: no ambient occlusion), or a depth-based one reads stale depth.
- **Cause:** NVN copies a depth-stencil texture into a smaller one (Link's Awakening: its 1024×576 target to 512×288 and 256×144 every frame). Vulkan's only scaling
  copy is a blit, and the Adreno drivers give `D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT` neither `BLIT_SRC` nor `BLIT_DST`, so `copy_t2t` skipped it.
- **Fix:** `ds_copy_draw` (`gfx_vk_dscopy.inc`) draws the copy into the destination as its depth-stencil attachment, as the Mac's `copy_t2t_draw` does: each fragment
  takes its source texel's depth as `gl_FragDepth` from a depth view, and its stencil from a stencil view, written through `VK_EXT_shader_stencil_export` where the
  device has it; without it, a draw writes depth and stencil 0, then a draw per bit discards where the source's bit is clear and writes that bit alone (write mask
  `1 << bit`, reference 0xff). Nearest texels; a mirrored copy reads its source backwards. The shaders are the replay's first of its own (`dm/android/shaders`, built
  by the NDK's `glslc`); its descriptor sets are the chunk's own, handed out again when the chunk completes. `DM_VK_STENCIL_EXPORT=0` (a diagnostic) takes the per-bit
  draws on a device with the extension.
- **Check:** Link's Awakening on an Ayn Odin 2: both copies drawn, the ambient occlusion target no longer its cleared 1.0 (mean 232 of 255 in the Ancient Ruins, contact
  shadows at the pillars' feet), 58.5 fps.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 8).

### The front face follows the queue's raster flip alone on Vulkan

- **Symptom:** Android only, a game that keeps NVN's lower-left window origin: the 3D world black (or missing post-processing) while closed meshes and the HUD draw;
  single-sided full-screen passes that cull back faces write nothing, so their targets keep their cleared values.
- **Cause:** the pipeline key inverted the front face by `g_origin_ll ^ g_queue_ll` (the device's origin and the queue's raster flip), the formula of Metal's CPU coverage
  test (`nvn_cover`), which works in other coordinates. Metal's draws invert by the queue's flip alone; the viewport (flipped by the device's origin) places vertices the
  same way on both backends, and both judge winding as seen in the framebuffer. Link's Awakening keeps the lower-left origin for its device and its queue, so the two
  flips cancelled: its back-face culled passes (ambient occlusion, its four blurs, the composite into the frame) were culled whole. Light volumes, closed meshes, still lit
  the scene, so a pass dump showed the scene drawn and everything after it at its clear value.
- **Fix:** the key's `ll` is `g_queue_ll` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`, three places: the key, the pipeline memo and its check). A game that switches its device to upper-left
  before making its queue (every game so far but two) is unchanged, and so is one that makes its queue first (Xenoblade Chronicles 2).
- **Check:** Link's Awakening on an Ayn Odin 2: the Ancient Ruins drawn, at 59.9 fps. A pass dump now logs each draw's depth-stencil attachment and its depth and stencil
  tests, which ruled out a failing stencil test for the blurs.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 8).

### Texture copies that change format convert on Vulkan

- **Symptom:** a whole frame, a movie or HUD art in bands of saturated colour that cycle as brightness changes (contour rings), the shapes intact.
- **Cause:** `copy_t2t` (`dm/android/gfx_vk.c`) made a raw `vkCmdCopyImage` of any same-size copy whose two textures had equal `conv` and `px`. Those fields describe
  textures the replay decodes from BC on the CPU, and every other format has 0 in both, so a copy between any two uncompressed formats copied bits. Link's Awakening copies
  its R11G11B10F frame into its RGBA8 window texture: the packed floats arrived as bytes. NVN's copy converts, as the Mac's `copy_t2t_draw` does.
- **Fix:** a colour copy between two float, normalized or sRGB formats is a blit (which converts) unless the formats are equal or both decode from one compressed family.
  Copies to or from an integer format, or between depth formats, stay raw: a blit cannot convert those.
- **Check:** Link's Awakening on an Ayn Odin 2: the opening movie, the file select and the HUD in their colours; Super Mario Odyssey, Brilliant Diamond, Animal Crossing,
  Smash and Skyward Sword HD unchanged (frame rates and pictures).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 8).

### Plain char is unsigned on Android

- **Symptom:** sparkling noise on lit surfaces and bright broken lines inside soft shapes (clouds), wherever a signed normal map is sampled.
- **Cause:** AArch64 Linux makes plain `char` unsigned; Apple arm64, where the host and its third-party code were written, makes it signed. `bcdec` reads BC4 / BC5 SNORM endpoints through `(char)`, so negative endpoints became 128..255 and every signed normal map decoded to noise (the Adreno has no BC: they are decoded on the CPU).
- **Fix:** the Android build compiles everything with `-fsigned-char` (`dm/android/CMakeLists.txt`).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): its normal maps are BC5 SNORM (`R8G8_SNORM` once decoded).

### Render targets attached in their view's format

- **Symptom:** the whole picture darker and more saturated than the Switch's.
- **Cause:** the render pass attached each colour target in its image's format, ignoring the format of the view the game binds it with. Wonder's last pass writes its RGBA8 window texture through an RGBA8_SRGB view, so the linear values were stored unencoded (the same as [the Metal finding](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md)).
- **Fix:** `rp_prepare` makes the attachment view in `view_format` (an uncompressed colour image is made for its own format and its sRGB twin: [Colour images keep Adreno's framebuffer compression](#colour-images-keep-adrenos-framebuffer-compression)).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Colour images keep Adreno's framebuffer compression

- **Symptom:** Android only: GPU time up to a third higher than it needs to be, and a colour target's memory exactly its texels (`[vk] colour target 1600x900 format 37 (MUTABLE_FORMAT): 5.49 MB of memory for 5.49 MB of texels`).
- **Cause:** every uncompressed colour image was made with `VK_IMAGE_CREATE_MUTABLE_FORMAT_BIT`, so that a view could take any format of its texel size (an NVN texture view's own format). Given that, Adreno's driver leaves the image without UBWC, its lossless framebuffer compression, and every pass stores and loads its targets uncompressed.
- **Fix:** `vt_create` gives an uncompressed colour image that is not a storage image a format list (`VK_KHR_image_format_list`) of its format and its sRGB twin (`srgb_twin`: RGBA8, BGRA8, ABGR8, R8 and RG8), and `MUTABLE_FORMAT` only when there is a twin. A view in any other format reads the image's own (`[RED] [vk] texture view in format … which lists only its own and its sRGB twin`). Storage images keep `MUTABLE_FORMAT` with no list: a shader declares their format. Found by nx2android ([github.com/shirai91/nx2android](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android), a1b4f54). `DM_VK_UBWC=0` (env.txt) makes them as before.
- **Check:** the same targets now take room for the compression's metadata (`5.60 MB of memory for 5.49 MB of texels`). GPU time a frame (p50) on an Ayn Odin 2, kept against `DM_VK_UBWC=0`: Super Mario Odyssey's opening 4.30 against 5.57 ms, Brilliant Diamond's Eterna City 6.22 against 8.41, Animal Crossing's island 8.59 against 9.80, Skyward Sword HD's Skyloft 3.64 against 5.59, Link's Awakening's Ancient Ruins 5.91 against 5.83. On a OnePlus Pad 3: Odyssey 8.05 against 9.33 ms, Animal Crossing 15.09 against 15.59. The runs' last screenshots match apart from animation. No red line in any of the six test games.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md), with the other test games in the [Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md).

### Compressed render targets through an uncompressed shadow on Vulkan

- **Symptom:** Android only: `[RED] [vk] texture view in format 95 over an image of format 139: read in the image's` (an RGBA16UI view of a BC4 image), and in the kernel log (`adb shell dmesg`) `CP opcode error interrupt | opcode=0x00000000` with `kgsl … ctx_type VK` lines, one per submission that holds the pass; whatever else those submissions recorded (uploads, other passes) never reaches the GPU.
- **Cause:** a GPU block encoder draws into a BC texture through an uncompressed integer view, one texel per block. Vulkan has no such attachment, so `rp_prepare` attached the BC image itself; the Adreno 740's command processor stopped on that pass and the kernel driver dropped the whole submission.
- **Fix:** as on Metal ([Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow](#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow)): `rp_prepare` attaches a shadow image of blocks × blocks in the view's format (`bcrt_shadow`, made once per base image, level, layer and format), copies the level's (layer's, or 3D depth plane's) blocks into it first, and `rp_end` copies them back (`bcrt_copy`: `vkCmdCopyImage` between a format and a compressed one of the same block size). The shadows go with their base image (`bcrt_drop` from `views_drop`).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): agl's BC4 block encoder writes a 32×32×32 BC4 3D texture one depth plane per pass through an RGBA16UI view; three faulting submissions at every start on an Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740, driver 0676.32), none after the fix. One of them held the sky's scattering-table upload, so the sky stayed murky olive.

### BC textures decoded on the CPU, viewed in their BC formats (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, on a GPU without BC textures (the device line says `BC decoded on the CPU`: the Pixel 9 Pro XL's Mali-G715): `[RED] [vk] texture view in format 139 over an image of format 9: read in the image's`, a BC4 view of a BC4 texture whose image holds R8 once decoded. A view in the texture's sRGB twin (BC7_SRGB over BC7) was read in the image's UNORM format, so its colours were not decoded from sRGB.
- **Cause:** on such a GPU `vt_create` decodes a BC texture into an uncompressed image (`bc_target`: RGBA8, R8, RG8 or RGBA16F), but `view_format` compared the view's BC format with the image's decoded one, and the decoded image was made without MUTABLE_FORMAT, so no view of it could take another format.
- **Fix:** `view_format` maps a decoded image's BC view format through `bc_target` first: the image's own BC format is the image itself, its sRGB twin is the decoded format's twin. A decoded colour image lists its format and that twin, as an uncompressed one does ([Colour images keep Adreno's framebuffer compression](#colour-images-keep-adrenos-framebuffer-compression)). A GPU with BC textures is unchanged: its images are never decoded.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) on a Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715, driver 0xd803000, Vulkan 1.4.343): the red line at the Start menu, gone with the fix; the Start menu and the opening cutscene draw right at 60 fps with or without it.

### Vertex streams stepping per N instances with the KHR divisor (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, on a GPU without `VK_EXT_vertex_attribute_divisor` (the Pixel 9 Pro XL's Mali-G715): `[RED] [vk] vertex stream divisor N > 1 on a GPU without VK_EXT_vertex_attribute_divisor: read as 1`, the stream's elements stepping every instance instead of every N.
- **Cause:** the replay enabled only the EXT extension. A Vulkan 1.4 device can offer only `VK_KHR_vertex_attribute_divisor` (core there), with the same structures and type values. Its `supportsNonZeroFirstInstance` can be false too: the Mali-G715 then reads element `instance / N`, base instance and all, where NVN reads `base instance + (instance - base instance) / N` (`dm/android/tests/t_divisor.c`, divisor 3 at base instance 5: 101 102 102 102 … instead of 105 105 105 106 …).
- **Fix:** `vk_create` enables the KHR extension where the EXT is missing and reads that property. Where it is false, a draw with a stream stepping per N > 1 instances starts at instance 0 and its instance-rate streams at the base instance's element (`inst_shift` in `bind_vbufs` and the draws). Translated shaders read their instance as `InstanceIndex - BaseInstance` and NVN's base instance from c0, so they see the same numbers. A device with the EXT is unchanged.
- **Found on:** the Pixel 9 Pro XL ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md#mali-super-mario-odyssey-on-a-pixel-9-pro-xl)) from its device features; `t_divisor` passes there with the fix's way and fails with the first instance. Neither [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) nor Final Fantasy Resonance draws such a stream.

### A GPU block encoder's blocks decoded on the GPU into a texture the CPU decoded (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, on a GPU without BC textures (the device line says `BC decoded on the CPU`: Mali, Xclipse): `[RED] [vk] texture view in format 95 over an image of format 9, which lists only its own and its sRGB twin (DM_VK_UBWC=0 views it in any): read in the image's` (an RGBA16UI view of a BC4 texture whose image holds R8), during the game's first load, and the texture the encoder wrote holds what the pass's `uint` outputs left in an R8 image instead of the encoded values: in a frame dump (`DM_VK_DUMP`), Breath of the Wild's 32x32x32 BC4 volume is all zeros on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950), and zeros but for an 8x8 square of 255 in a corner of each slice on a Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715).
- **Cause:** a GPU block encoder draws BC blocks into a BC texture through a one-texel-per-block view ([Compressed render targets through an uncompressed shadow on Vulkan](#compressed-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow-on-vulkan)). On such a GPU `vt_create` keeps the texture decoded ([BC textures decoded on the CPU, viewed in their BC formats](#bc-textures-decoded-on-the-cpu-viewed-in-their-bc-formats-android)), and only the CPU decoded it, on uploads and buffer copies. `bcrt_shadow` left decoded images out (its copies move blocks, and a decoded image holds texels), so the pass attached the decoded image itself, in its own format, and its blocks were never decoded.
- **Fix:** `bcrt_shadow` shadows a decoded BC4 or BC5 image too, and `rp_end` decodes the shadow into it on the GPU (`bcd_decode`, `dm/android/gfx_vk_bcdec.inc`): the blocks copied into an arena buffer, a compute pass (`dm/android/shaders/bc_decode.comp`, compiled by the NDK's glslc with the replay's other shaders) writing the bytes bcdec's precise decoder writes, rows of whole blocks, and those copied into the level and layer or depth plane the pass drew, clipped to its size. The shadow keeps its blocks between passes, so a pass that draws only some blocks keeps the rest from its last one (zeros before the first). Another BC format keeps the old behaviour with `[RED] [vk] a GPU block encoder draws into a BC texture decoded on the CPU (format …)`. A GPU with BC textures never decodes, so nothing changes there. `DM_VK_BC_GPU_DECODE=0` draws into the decoded image, as before.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_bc_gpu_decode.py` compiles the shader and checks it with spirv-val; with `DM_TEST_SERIAL` it runs `dm/android/tests/t_bc_decode.c` on the device, which makes `bcd_decode`'s commands for BC4, BC5 and their signed forms (a 32x32 level, a depth plane of a 32x32x32 volume, edge blocks clipped, a 1x1 level) and compares every byte with bcdec's: all match on a Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715) and a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950). In the game, a probe (not kept) read back each decode's shadow and decoded plane and decoded the blocks with bcdec on the CPU: 63 of 63 planes, 37,449 bytes, equal on both phones. Frame rate and GPU time over the `outdoors` route's window (two runs after a warm-up, main against the fix): the Flip 29.6 and 29.9 fps against 29.8 and 29.8, GPU p50 19.8 and 17.4 ms against 17.7 and 16.8; the Pixel 28.8 and 28.8 fps against 28.8 and 28.7, GPU 19.6 and 20.0 ms against 19.9 and 20.1. A GPU with BC textures takes none of this: on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, driver 0x8032004a) the log has no decode, and main against the fix gave 30.0 fps in all four runs, GPU p50 16.16 and 16.21 ms against 16.11 and 16.33, the screenshots within run-to-run noise.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): agl's BC4 encoder (`8e2cc2671cae6020`) fills a 32x32x32 BC4 volume with six levels during the save's load, one pass a depth plane (63 passes): a tiling cellular noise, smooth from slice to slice, once decoded. In the Shrine of Resurrection only one shader samples it (`f318a09c33f75f82`, at a fixed coordinate, so one filtered value), and its pass's output differed by 0.2% between the decode on and off, at the animated sparks: the route's screenshots show no change of their own. Where else the game samples it (outdoors, not run) is open.

### Replay syncs limited to what is read

- **Symptom:** low frame rate with the GPU and the CPU both mostly idle; the render thread waiting in `view_` / `submit_wait`.
- **Cause:** a draw or dispatch after any pending GPU write to guest memory (a storage buffer a shader wrote, copied back when the chunk completes) submitted the chunk and waited for the GPU, whatever it read. Uniform buffer updates and small copies did the same.
- **Fix:** `draw_view` / `mem_view` compare the ranges a draw, dispatch or CPU write touches (vertex streams, indices, uniform slots, storage buffers, indirect arguments) with the pending write-backs and queued copies, and sync only on an overlap. Vertex streams a dispatch of the same chunk wrote are bound from the dispatch's own arena copy (`fwd_find`) instead of waiting for them. The `[vk] guest reads of draws` and `syncs by site` lines count what still waits.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): about one sync per draw after the first storage-buffer write of a frame; a compute shader writes a few hundred bytes of vertices once a frame.

### Buffer textures synced by their range, and dispatch outputs read on the GPU

- **Symptom:** Android only: a 3D scene far below the GPU's rate, the submission's wall time about twice its CPU time (`@sub_wall_ms` against `@sub_cpu_ms` in a bench's `frames.tsv`), and the `[vk] syncs by site` line counting `buffer texture` and `buffer image` syncs by the hundred thousand, or `[vk] guest reads of draws` counting as many waits for the current submission.
- **Cause:** two kinds of syncs that [Replay syncs limited to what is read](#replay-syncs-limited-to-what-is-read) left:
  - a draw or dispatch whose shader had a buffer texture or buffer image synced on anything pending (`cpu_view`), since its memory is found only through the texture pool;
  - a storage buffer, buffer texture or buffer image that an earlier dispatch of the same submission wrote was copied from guest memory, so the read waited for the GPU to finish and the write-back to land first. A chain of dispatches, each reading or updating what the one before wrote, was a GPU round trip per dispatch.
- **Fix:**
  - `draw_view` looks each buffer texture's and image's memory up in the texture registry, as `stage_set`'s `tbuf_view` does (`tbuf_bytes`), and syncs only when that range overlaps a pending write.
  - `buf_fwd` binds such a read from the dispatch's own arena copy instead, when that write-back alone covers it and the offset keeps the binding's alignment, as vertex streams already were (`fwd_find`). A dispatch begins and ends with a full memory barrier, so any later draw or dispatch sees its writes.
  - A dispatch that reads and writes the buffer (a read-write storage buffer, a buffer image) is bound in place on the current submission's write-back. It adds no write-back of its own, and that one lands what both wrote. Draws never write in place: a render pass has no barrier between them.
  - The `[vk] read on the GPU from a dispatch's pending write` line counts both kinds. `DM_VK_TBUF_RANGE=0` and `DM_VK_BUF_FWD=0` turn each part off, for an A/B. `DM_VK_DEP_LOG=<n>` logs one in every n reads that still meet a pending write-back, with the shader, the binding kind and what wrote it.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md)'s real-time opening: 713 draws a frame and about 100 syncs.
  - Most came from a compute shader (`f3c5ced35cb619d0`) whose dispatches run image atomics on 8 KB and 32 KB buffer images the dispatch before wrote.
  - The rest came from one (`f632c6a1bc76cd19`) reading a 64 KB storage buffer the dispatch before wrote, then writing a 3D image.
  - The opening ran at 15.9 fps on an Ayn Odin 2 and 10.3 on a OnePlus Pad 3; it now runs at 40.6 and 41.0 (frame p50 24 ms). The Pad 3's GPU time per frame went from 28.7 to 15.5 ms.

### Buffer textures read through a 2D slot, as rows on Vulkan (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: the 3D scene is black, and so is anything drawn as a lit 3D scene (a title or menu backdrop), while text, menu shapes and the HUD draw, at the usual frame rate. The Mac draws it lit and logs `texture buffers bound as 2D rows for wrapped 1D fetches N times`; the Android log had `[RED] [vk] buffer textures are not on Vulkan yet` once.
- **Cause:** a slot the translator reads by wrapped 1D fetches ([row wrap](#buffer-textures-read-by-1d-integer-fetches-row-wrap), res.json `fetch1d`), or one whose handle sits in a uniform buffer ([handles in uniform buffers](#buffer-textures-behind-handles-in-uniform-buffers-and-rows-of-finalized-descriptors)), is declared 2D. The Mac binds a buffer texture there as a linear 2D texture of 16,384-texel rows over the pool memory (`tbuf_as_2d`). The Vulkan replay had no such form: `vt_create` refused every buffer texture, `stage_set` bound the dummy image, and the slot read 0. Its buffer-texture slots (uniform and storage texel buffers, `tbuf_view`) were already done. The game multiplied its lit passes by what it reads there, so all of them came out black. The Mac with the rows left out (a local switch at `tbuf_as_2d`'s call) drew the same black picture.
- **Fix:** such a slot (`tex_rows_slot`: one 2D binding, not an array, multisample or shadow slot, that `fetch1d` names by the translator's slot number, `tbr_slot`, or whose handle is in a uniform buffer) looks its texture up with `vt_get_s`, and a buffer texture gets an image of the rows (`dm/android/tbuf_rows.h`):
  - an optimal 2D image of `min(n, 16384)` × `ceil(n / 16384)` texels in the texture's format (32-bit integer formats as the float format of their size: the translator declares every texture float, and the shader takes the bits back, as `nvn_mtl.m` `tbuf_view_format` does), refused past the GPU's largest 2D image;
  - filled from guest memory as a linear texture is: uploaded at its first use and again when its pages change (`vt_upload_` with `tbr_fill`: the buffer's bytes are already the rows back to back, and the rest of the last row reads 0);
  - a draw whose shader has met one lands a pending write to the buffer first (`draw_view`, `vshader.tbrows`), so a GPU write-back is in guest memory when `stage_textures` checks its pages.
  - Every other lookup of a buffer texture (a slot of another type, an image slot, a target, a copy, a texture pool read) still gets no image (red line 198). `DM_VK_TBUF_ROWS=0` (env.txt) makes such a slot read 0 again, for an A/B.
- **Note:** a copy, not a view: Vulkan can't view buffer memory as an optimal image, and a linear image over the pool would need the driver's row pitch and offset alignment to fit the buffer's (on Apple GPUs the alignment already forces a copy, [Buffer texture rows at a pool offset the device will not view](#buffer-texture-rows-at-a-pool-offset-the-device-will-not-view)). An image filled from guest memory takes any pool offset.
- **Check (2026-11-13):** `dm/tools/tests/test_tbuf_rows.py`: slot numbers, shapes and fill on the host, the test shader through `spirv-val`; with `DM_TEST_SERIAL`, `dm/android/tests/t_tbuf_rows.c` on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830): from 1 to 1,048,576 elements, every element read back at the wrapped address and the texels past the buffer 0.
- **Found on:** Assassin's Creed: The Ezio Collection (`0100670014482000`, II), on a Galaxy S25 Edge: a 48-element R32F buffer texture its passes read through a 2D slot (on the Mac, compute shader `2405a4193559eaec`, slot 132). With the rows, the title's and the menus' white backdrops, the Animus load and Abstergo's lab draw as on the Mac, at the same 30 fps and GPU time (p50 14.9 ms).

### Pending writes read on the GPU by the first draw of a submission (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game whose draws read what an earlier dispatch (or a draw under rasterizer discard) wrote runs far below its GPU's rate, the GPU at a low clock. The `[vk] guest reads of draws` line counts waits for submissions in flight, about one for each submission sent in the middle of a frame.
- **Cause:** reading a pending write on the GPU ([Buffer textures synced by their range, and dispatch outputs read on the GPU](#buffer-textures-synced-by-their-range-and-dispatch-outputs-read-on-the-gpu)) needs the chunk the draw is recorded in. The read binds the write-back's copy there and holds the chunk in flight that has it until the reading chunk completes (`fwd_find`).
  - `draw_view` ran before anything of the draw had opened a chunk.
  - So the first draw after each submission in the middle of a pass (`MID_DRAWS`, 64 draws, or new targets after 48) found no chunk. `buf_fwd` and `stream_fwd` refused, and the draw read the range on the CPU instead, which waits for every chunk in flight to complete.
  - Xenoblade Chronicles 3's character draws met their skinning draws' write-backs that way about 6 times a frame. Each was a drain of the GPU at its lowest clock.
- **Fix:** `draw_view` takes the chunk first (`cur`), as the draw itself would right after. `DM_VK_FWD_OPEN=0` leaves it as before.
- **Check (2026-11-12):** Xenoblade Chronicles 3 1.0.0 on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Android 16), its game app with only the host swapped, from a fresh start through the opening to the first battle and the field after it:
  - Measured on top of the stream clip and the draw-write change (their own pull requests). Without this change: 6 waits for submissions in flight a frame, 5 of them character draws reading a skinning write-back as the first draw after a submission. The battle and the field ran at 13.1 fps (the median of 454 two-second windows, all at 160 MHz), frame p50 77 ms, GPU 48 ms a frame.
  - With it: 1 such wait a frame (a draw's write in a submission in flight). The battle and the field run at 30 fps, frame p50 33.2 ms (the Switch's rate), GPU 25.4–26.1 ms a frame.
  - The GPU's clock went from 160 MHz to 342–443 MHz. No longer starved, the GPU was clocked up by its governor, so this step has no comparison at one clock.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md#android).

### Two bindings of the same memory in one shader

- **Symptom:** Android only, nothing visible found yet: a shader that binds one guest buffer twice (two image slots, or two storage buffers) and writes it through one of them.
  - What one binding writes isn't what the other reads in that dispatch.
  - When both write, the bytes that land are a mix of the two copies.
- **Cause:** the replay gives each written binding an arena copy and a write-back of its own. `buf_fwd` binds a write in place only on a write-back from before the draw or dispatch (`g_fwd_nwb`), so when the draw or dispatch is the first in its submission to write the buffer, its second binding of it gets a second copy. On the Switch the two views are one buffer. The two write-backs carry one snapshot and land the bytes each changed, so the later one wins where both changed a byte.
- **Fix:** `own_alias` (in `tbuf_view` and in `stage_set`'s storage buffers) binds a binding that lies inside a write-back this same draw or dispatch made on that write-back's copy. It adds no copy and no write-back of its own; `wb_alias.h` decides, and `test_wb_alias.py` tests it.
  - The `[vk] read on the GPU from a dispatch's pending write` line counts these bindings.
  - `DM_VK_ALIAS=0`: each binding gets its own copy, as before.
- **Found on:** [Final Fantasy Resonance](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md)'s Niagara argument shader `544e80ec57b3a10b`. It binds its 8 KB counts buffer as an R32ui image it reads and as a format-less image it clears. As the first field loads it's the first in its submission to write that buffer, and own_alias binds 16 bindings in those frames. Later in the field an earlier dispatch has written it, so both bindings are already in place.
- **Check:** Final Fantasy Resonance's `field` route on a Moto Razr Plus 2023 (Adreno 730), with and without (`DM_VK_ALIAS=0`).
  - The 16 bindings come 54 s in, as the field loads.
  - 15.9–16.0 fps against 16.0, GPU 38.5–38.6 ms against 38.3. The picture at the route's end is the same.
  - Super Mario Odyssey on a Retroid Pocket 5 binds none (59.4 against 59.5 fps).

### A draw's storage buffer write read by a later draw after a barrier (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game reads, in a later draw, what an earlier draw wrote to a storage buffer, and plays far below its GPU's rate, the GPU at a low clock. The `[vk] guest reads of draws` line counts a few waits for the current submission each frame, and the replay thread and the GPU take turns instead of overlapping.
- **Cause:** a draw's storage buffer write is a write-back of its own that later shaders don't read on the GPU. Draws in a pass have no barrier between them, so only a dispatch's write-back is forwardable ([Buffer textures synced by their range, and dispatch outputs read on the GPU](#buffer-textures-synced-by-their-range-and-dispatch-outputs-read-on-the-gpu)). A draw reading such a write submitted the chunk and waited for it to complete.
  - Xenoblade Chronicles 3 writes 16 and 64 bytes from a draw, and a later draw reads them as a uniform buffer, twice a frame.
  - With the GPU at its lowest clock, each wait took about 4 ms. Around them the replay thread (37.6 ms a frame) and the GPU (48.8 ms) ran one after the other: an 80 ms frame.
- **Fix:** a read that the newest write of the current chunk holds whole, where that write is a draw's plain arena copy, ends the targets' pass, puts a full barrier after everything recorded and marks the write forwardable (`wb_promote`; the rule in `dm/android/wb_promote.h`).
  - The read then binds the write-back's copy, as a dispatch's: a storage buffer through `buf_fwd`, a uniform buffer through `ub_fwd` at the uniform buffer alignment. The pass begins again at the next draw.
  - These still wait, as before: a queued copy into the range, a write of another kind (a staging slice, in place, a linear target's, a depth / stencil readback), a read the write does not hold, and a draw's write in a chunk already in flight.
  - `DM_VK_WB_PROMOTE=0` leaves such reads waiting. The `[vk] guest reads of draws` line counts the writes made readable so.
- **Check (2026-11-12):** `dm/tools/tests/test_wb_promote.py`. Xenoblade Chronicles 3 1.0.0 on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Android 16), its game app with only the host swapped, from a fresh start through the opening to the first battle and the field after it:
  - Measured on top of the stream clip (its own pull request). Without this change: 2 waits for the current submission a frame (these reads), 12.0 fps in the battle and the field (the median of 127 two-second windows, all at 160 MHz), frame p50 84 ms.
  - With it: none. 12.7 and 13.1 fps at 160 MHz in two runs (121 and 454 windows), frame p50 77 ms, about 20,000 writes made readable in a run of 20 minutes. The rest of the turn-taking is the first draw of each submission waiting for those in flight, fixed in its own pull request (30 fps with the three).
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md#android).

### Storage buffers draws write in an order-free way (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: a scene runs at a few frames a second, and the `[vk] guest reads of draws` line counts hundreds of waits for the current submission a frame, with as many submissions and as many `[vk] arena write-backs` a frame. The `DM_VK_DEP_LOG` lines name fragment shaders reading a storage buffer whose pending write-back is an arena copy with a snapshot, the same buffer each time.
- **Cause:** many draws of a pass write one storage buffer, and the shaders only write it: Tears of the Kingdom's fragment shaders store `1` at the cell of a 131,584-byte grid under each pixel (`fp_s0[i] = 1u`), and others take a tile's depth bounds with `atomicMin` / `atomicMax` on a 23,232-byte buffer. The replay gives each draw's written storage buffer an arena copy of guest memory and a write-back against a snapshot. The next draw that binds the buffer needs the bytes the last one wrote before it can copy them, so `draw_view` submitted the chunk and waited for it to complete, once a draw. Each wait also cut the render pass, and the targets were stored and loaded again around it.
  - At the save of droidplay-core#457 (Thyphlo Ruins by night) that was about 720 submissions, 665 waits and 720 write-backs (86 MB) a frame on a Galaxy S25 Edge: 2.9 fps, frame p50 341 ms, GPU 118 ms a frame in 847 render passes.
- **Fix:** a binding whose shader writes it only in an order-free way needs nothing of guest memory (`dm/android/ofw.h`, `gfx_vk_draw.inc` `ofw_bind`).
  - **Order-free:** every store through the binding stores the same 32-bit constant and nothing else touches it, or every access is the same commutative 32-bit atomic (add, subtract, increment or decrement; signed or unsigned min or max; and, or, xor) whose old value nothing reads. The translator stores an atomic's old value in a local that's never loaded, and that counts as unread. `ofw_scan` decides from the module as packed, once per shader. It reads only the operands that are ids, since a literal such as GLSL.std.450's `FMin` (37) equals a common variable id.
  - **The copy:** such a draw's copy starts as the operation's identity (any word but the constant for stores, all ones for unsigned min and for and, 0 for add, or, xor and unsigned max, and so on), so it holds only what the draws wrote. The draws of a submission that write the range the same way share it. The newest write of the submission to the range decides: after a write of another kind (a dispatch's, another constant, another operation), a new copy starts, so the copies land in the draws' order.
  - **Landing:** when the submission completes, each word that differs from the identity is combined with guest memory by the operation (`gfx_vk.c` `ofw_apply`), after what earlier write-backs land. Stores of one constant and commutative atomics come out the same in any order, so the shared copy needs no barrier between its draws.
  - `draw_view` makes no sync for such a binding. No later shader reads the copy on the GPU: it's never forwardable (`fwd_hit`), and `wb_promote` and `own_alias` leave it alone. A read of the range waits for it to land, as for any write-back. Dispatches keep writing on their chunk's copy as before (`buf_fwd`), and so do task and mesh shaders in compute form.
  - The `[vk] storage buffers draws write in an order-free way` line counts the bindings on a shared copy, the copies made and landed, and the shaders. `DM_VK_OFW=0`: each draw takes its own copy and waits for the one before, as before.
- **Check (2026-11-14):** `dm/tools/tests/test_ofw.py`, including a randomized check that copies landed in order equal the draws run one after another. Tears of the Kingdom 1.4.3 on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Qualcomm's driver, Android 16), its files from a3bbaa9a with only the host built from this branch, the #457 save from a private save folder:
  - At the save's spot: 14.2 fps, frame p50 70.5 ms, GPU 48 ms a frame in 211 render passes. About 85 submissions, 16 waits for the current submission and 87 write-backs (4.8 MB) a frame; about 620 bindings a frame on a shared copy and 26 copies made. The same build with `DM_VK_OFW=0`: 3.0 fps, frame p50 332 ms, GPU 111 ms, about 645 submissions and 590 waits a frame, as main (8a511cc2: 341 ms, GPU 118 ms, 725 and 667).
  - A new game's cave beneath Hyrule Castle, standing at the first point Link is under control: 20 fps, frame p50 50.4 ms (the frame step's 3 vsyncs), GPU 28.9 ms, replay CPU 23.3 ms, about 33 submissions and 8 waits a frame. With `DM_VK_OFW=0`: 11 fps, frame p50 89.7 ms, GPU 34.2 ms, replay CPU 35.8 ms, about 107 submissions and 85 waits a frame. The two screenshots there differ only in the torch and the dust (0.34 % of pixels by more than 24).
  - In the 1.4.3 shaders, 1,712 fragment shaders' written storage buffers are stores of one constant, 38 are unsigned min or max and 4 are adds; 905 aren't order-free (they read the buffer too, or store computed values). Ocarina of Time has 80 order-free fragment shader bindings (unsigned min and max) and 1 add, plus task and mesh shader adds that stay as they were in compute form; Mario Kart World 72 (unsigned min and max); Final Fantasy Resonance 1; Animal Crossing: New Horizons, Super Mario Odyssey, Super Smash Bros. Ultimate, Breath of the Wild, Brilliant Diamond, Link's Awakening and Skyward Sword HD none, so nothing changes for them.
  - Ocarina of Time's `kokiri` bench route on the same phone (its files of 2026-11-12, its defaults: raw global memory, the recorder): 29.9 fps, frame p50 33.3 ms, GPU 25.95 ms, against 30.0 fps, 33.3 ms and 25.91 ms with `DM_VK_OFW=0`. One of its order-free shaders loads there, and no draw binds such a buffer on that route.
- **Left:** the 16 waits a frame left at the save's spot are draws whose shaders read the grid's header and store computed values into it, and compute shaders reading what the order-free copies hold. A copy made from guest memory when nothing is pending could be read on the GPU after a barrier, as `wb_promote` does.
- **Found on:** [Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#android), at droidplay-core#457's save.

### Render passes kept over CPU-side commands

- **Symptom:** hundreds of render passes a frame on the same targets; on a tiler GPU each loads and stores its attachments.
- **Cause:** every command other than a draw ended the render pass, among them uniform buffer updates, which the replay does on the CPU; full clears ran outside passes (`vkCmdClearColorImage` and a full barrier, then the pass loaded the result).
- **Fix:** CPU-side commands (uniform updates, buffer copies and fills, events, counters, fences) and clears leave the pass open; full clears of a target the size of the pass use `vkCmdClearAttachments` in it.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): 403 passes in a level frame, 234 of them on the same two 960×540 targets (a uniform update before nearly every draw); about 60 after.

### Indices not read back from write-combined memory

- **Symptom:** over half of the render thread's time in the index copy of `vk_draw`.
- **Cause:** the vertex range of an indexed draw was computed by reading back the indices just written to the arena, which is host-visible write-combined memory (uncached reads).
- **Fix:** the range is taken from the indices as they are written; storage buffer snapshots are copied from guest memory, not from the arena.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): 78 → 27 ms of replay CPU a frame in a level.

### Storage buffer write-backs read from cached memory on Vulkan

- **Symptom:** Android only, on Mesa's Turnip: a third of the phone's own driver's frame rate with a faster GPU, and the `vk-done` thread busy in `top -H`.
  The game writes storage buffers from its shaders. `NVN submit+present` in the `timing up to frame` lines is most of the frame, `vk-done` is on a CPU nearly all
  the time, and the replay thread sleeps in `cur()`, waiting for a free chunk.
- **Cause:** the arena took the first host-visible coherent memory type. A storage buffer or buffer image a shader writes lives in the arena, and the completion thread
  reads it back to copy the bytes the GPU changed into guest memory (`chunk_done` → `wb_apply`, compared with its snapshot a byte at a time where a word differs). Qualcomm's
  driver on an Ayn Odin 2 offers the arena's buffers a single host-visible type, cached and coherent (types 0 and 6, flags `0x1` and `0xf`); Turnip lists a write-combined
  type first (types 0 to 2: `0x7`, then `0xf`), so every read of the write-backs went to DRAM, the completion thread fell behind, and all four chunks stayed in flight.
- **Fix:** `ar_mem_type` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`) prefers a host-visible, coherent and cached type for the arena, as staging does, and falls back to the first host-visible
  coherent one; the log names the type (`[vk] arena: memory type`). `DM_VK_ARENA_CACHED=0` takes the first host-visible coherent type, as before.
- **Check:** Pokémon Brilliant Diamond's `eterna` route (`scripts/bench_android.py`) on an Ayn Odin 2 with Mr. Purple's Turnip T30 (`DM_VK_DRIVER`): 20.4 and
  21.0 fps before (submit+present p50 about 44 ms, 19 command buffers a frame, `vk-done` 78% of a CPU; simpleperf: 37 ms a frame in `wb_apply` and its `memcpy`),
  60.0 fps after (submit+present 13.4 ms, `vk-done` 27%), GPU time 6.1 → 5.9 ms. On the system driver both settings take type 6: 60.0 fps either way.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md).

### Storage buffer write-backs read from memory the CPU maps uncached

- **Symptom:** Android only, on Qualcomm's own driver on a Retroid Pocket 5 (Adreno 650, V@0746.0): a quarter of Turnip's frame rate, `vk-done` busy.
  `NVN submit+present` is most of the frame and `vk-done` on a CPU most of the time (`top -H`), although `[vk] arena: memory type` names a cached coherent type;
  simpleperf puts `vk-done`'s time on `wb_apply`'s loads and `memcpy`, as in [the entry above](#storage-buffer-write-backs-read-from-cached-memory-on-vulkan).
- **Cause:** a memory type's flags do not say how the CPU maps it. The driver lists type 4 as `0xf` (device-local, host-visible, coherent, cached), the only host-visible
  type it allows a buffer of the arena's usage, and the arena takes it; but the CPU reads it at 1.3 GB/s whether the GPU or the CPU wrote it, and a 1 MB buffer read a
  second time is no faster, where malloc'd memory reads at 11–13 GB/s: it is mapped uncached. Wider loads do not help (NEON 64-byte loads 1.4 GB/s), and `wb_apply` is
  slower still (its 8-byte compares 0.45 GB/s, its byte loop over changed bytes 0.17 GB/s). The driver's type 2 (`0xb`: cached, not coherent) reads at 12–15 GB/s after
  `vkInvalidateMappedMemoryRanges` (0.05 ms a MB), and it is offered to a buffer only when the buffer is a transfer destination and not a transfer source (memory type
  bits `0x15` against `0x11`), which the arena cannot be: texture uploads read it. An AHardwareBuffer reads at 1.3 GB/s through gralloc's mapping too, and the driver has
  no `VK_EXT_external_memory_host`. (A standalone probe on the phone measured each of these.)
- **Fix:** `gfx_vk_readback.inc`: at a submission's end, after its closing barrier, the GPU copies each of the chunk's arena write-backs (storage buffers, buffer images,
  transform feedback) into a buffer of the chunk's own in such a type (`rb_record`: one `vkCmdCopyBuffer` per arena block, then a transfer-to-host barrier), and
  `chunk_done` invalidates the copied range once and reads the write-backs from there. Whether a driver needs it is measured, not taken from the flags: the first
  submission with arena write-backs times a CPU read of 256 KB in the arena's type and in the readback type, and copies only where the arena's is under a third of the
  other's speed; the log gives both (`[vk] arena write-backs: the arena's memory type 4 reads at …`), and the memory line every 300 frames counts them
  (`[vk] arena write-backs (…), frames …`). `DM_VK_WB_READBACK=0` reads from the arena as before; `=1` copies wherever such a type exists.
- **Check:** Pokémon Brilliant Diamond's `eterna` route (`scripts/bench_android.py`) on a Retroid Pocket 5, `main` at `4743c9da` and the fix installed in turn on the same
  game files: 24.0, 24.6 and 24.6 fps on `main` (frame p50 40.4–41.3 ms, GPU 16.8–17.0 ms), 60.0, 54.6, 60.0 and 60.0 with the fix (frame p50 16.6–18.2 ms, GPU
  13.5–15.0 ms; the 54.6 run GPU-bound at 15.0 ms), the same 1,068 draws and the same picture. In the fix's own build the six write-backs a frame (494 KB) took the
  completion thread 26.2 ms a frame with `DM_VK_WB_READBACK=0` (25.2 fps), 1.7 ms through the copy. The probe read 1.5–1.6 GB/s from the arena's type and 28–34 GB/s
  from type 2. The `boot` route held 60.0 fps. On a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, V@0800.74) the probe read 49.3 GB/s from the arena's type 6 and 48.9 GB/s from type 3
  and kept reading the arena: 60.0 fps on `main` and with the change, two runs each (GPU 10.1–10.4 ms), and with `DM_VK_WB_READBACK=0`.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 13).

### Storage buffer write-backs landed a byte at a time

- **Symptom:** Android only: the completion thread takes milliseconds a frame to land storage buffers' write-backs although it reads them through cached memory
  (`landing every write-back took the completion thread … ms a frame` in the `[vk] arena write-backs` line, `100% read through the GPU's cached copy`); simpleperf
  puts `vk-done`'s time in `chunk_thread` itself (`wb_apply` inlined) and in `memcmp` and `memcpy` through the PLT.
- **Cause:** a storage buffer's write-back (and a storage texel buffer's) always carries a snapshot, but `wb_apply` took the snapshot path (only the span from the first
  changed byte to the last prepared, the bytes compared a word at a time by `wb_row_diff`) only with `DM_VK_LINWB_SNAP=1`, the linear render targets' switch, which is
  off by default. Otherwise it prepared every page of the buffer and walked it with a `memcmp` call per 8 bytes and a `memcpy` call per run of changed bytes: quick
  when nothing changes or long runs do, slow when the changes are scattered bytes, such as floats whose high bytes stay. The span's ends were found a byte at a time.
- **Fix:** a write-back with a snapshot takes the snapshot path whatever `DM_VK_LINWB_SNAP` says (which still decides whether a linear render target takes a snapshot);
  the span's ends are found a word at a time (`wb_first_diff`, `wb_last_diff`), and the first and last rows are compared from them. The same bytes are written; pages
  without a changed byte are no longer prepared, so they are not stamped and nothing kept from them is uploaded again. `DM_VK_WB_WORDS=0` (env.txt): the byte loop and
  every page prepared, as before.
- **Check:** Super Smash Bros. Ultimate's `battle` route on a Retroid Pocket 5 (Adreno 650, V@0746.0), which writes back 1.1 to 1.8 MB of storage buffers a frame. A
  test build that landed every storage write-back both ways into scratch copies of the destination found no difference in 16,000 of them (3.6 GB), and the span's ends
  equal to a byte scan's. Landing took 0.79 to 2.25 ms per MB on `main` (seven battles, median 1.44; 1.1 to 4.0 ms a frame) and 0.71 and 0.72 ms per MB with the fix
  (two battles; 1.0 and 1.1 ms a frame), on the stages the route picked at random. A test build that turned it on and off every 600 presents, in two battles (each
  on one stage): 0.39 to 0.70 ms per MB with it against 0.78 to 1.57 without (means 0.64 against 1.36 in one battle, 0.47 against 0.83 in the other). The frame rate
  stayed within the stages' spread: 59.2 and 57.9 fps with the fix, 56.9 to 59.8 on `main`. On a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, which reads the arena directly), the
  same on-and-off build in one battle: 0.44 to 0.96 ms per MB with it against 1.17 to 1.82 without (means 0.84 and 1.60); battles at 59.8 and 60.0 fps with it and
  59.9 and 59.9 without, no fault, the picture the same.
- **Found on:** [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md)).

### Linear render targets written back to guest memory

- **Symptom:** Android only: what a game reads on the CPU from textures it draws into is zeros or stale. Breath of the Wild: Link sinks into grass up to his knees while he stands still, and the grass presses in a pass dump carry the presser's world height (`c3[3].z` about 200–270 instead of below 1) with a flat slope.
- **Cause:** a linear texture (`LINEAR` / `LINEAR_RENDER_TARGET`) in a pool the CPU can read is how a game hands GPU results to the CPU: it draws into the texture and reads the pool's memory after a fence. On Metal such a texture is a view on the pool's memory, so the GPU's writes land there; the Vulkan replay uploads linear textures from guest memory but never wrote what a pass drew into one back.
- **Fix:** `rp_end` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`) copies each linear colour target the pass drew (level 0, its layer) into a staging buffer, and the chunk's write-backs put it into guest memory at the texture's stride when the chunk completes, before its fence is signalled (`lin_writeback`). Only the bytes the pass changed are written: `rp_prepare` snapshots the target's guest bytes right after `vt_get` (`lsnap_take`) and `wb_apply` writes, and prepares, only the span of bytes that differ from the snapshot, nothing when none do; a pass whose target still has a write pending at `rp_prepare` takes no snapshot and is written back whole (`DM_VK_LINWB_SNAP=0`: always whole, every page prepared, as at first). A whole write-back put back any byte the CPU wrote during the pass (`DM_VK_WB_REVERT=1` counts those bytes per channel and texture; on the base's write-backs and timing with `DM_VK_LINWB_SNAP=0 DM_VK_WB_ABSORB=0 DM_VK_UP_RANGE=0`, since the other two change the uploads' syncs and so each pass's window from `rp_prepare` to its write-back). The texture's own write-back is not new content for it: the completion thread protects the storage again and notes the stamp when guest memory then equals the GPU's copy, and `vt_get` keeps the image instead of uploading the same bytes again, the lookup after it checking the pages again (`ab_note` / `ab_take`; one level and one layer; `DM_VK_WB_ABSORB=0`: uploaded again). The scheme is upstream nxnative's for storage-backed render targets on Metal (#604, not in this fork's `nvn_mtl.m`), which notes without comparing guest memory with the readback. An upload waits only for pending writes overlapping the bytes it reads, after ending the open pass so that its write-backs are seen (`up_view`; `DM_VK_UP_RANGE=0`: it waits for every pending write-back). Open: a pool's `Finalize` racing the completion thread's protect.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md), on an Ayn Odin 2: about twenty linear targets (1×1 to 512×512, CPU_CACHED | GPU_CACHED pools), from which the game reads ground heights for its grass presses (`3c79f4112a877d13` / `9786d84e2006896d`). With the heights read as zero every press had zero strength and the player's own press (`a08dbfa4a0f98f44`) was not drawn at all. A Mac run from the same save (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` with `DM_NVN_CBDUMP`) gave the reference values; after the fix the Odin's match them (`c3[3]` (0.376, 0.028, −0.268) against (0.377, 0.028, −0.268)).

### A linear render target uploaded again after its own write-back, every frame

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game holds 30 fps in play with its GPU mostly idle, switching its present interval between 1 and 2; long frames show `texture uploads 0
  first + N re-uploads` of a frame-sized target and `submit+present` far above the GPU time.
- **Cause:** a linear render target's write-back (above) stamps every page it writes, so the next frame that samples the target uploads it again from guest memory:
  Skyward Sword HD's 1920×1080 RGBA8 target is written back and uploaded again, 7.9 MB, every frame. `DM_VK_WB_ABSORB=1` keeps the image after its own write-back
  (what was written back is what the image holds), but it is off by default: with it, `DM_VK_LINWB_SNAP` and `DM_VK_UP_RANGE` on together, Breath of the Wild's tall
  grass collapsed in single frames, and which of the three does that is not known.
- **Fix:** a game's profile turns it on for that game alone: `dm/games/<id>/android_defaults.txt` (KEY=VALUE per line), which the packing script appends to the game's
  `defaults.txt` and `front_android.c` reads after `launcher.txt`, without overriding it or `env.txt` (a game app sets them in `GameStart.java`). Skyward Sword HD lists
  `DM_VK_WB_ABSORB=1`.
- **Check:** Skyward Sword HD in Skyloft: 30 fps before, 59.7 on a OnePlus Pad 3 and 59.3 on an Ayn Odin 2 after, the picture unchanged. Each switch alone on the Pad 3:
  `DM_VK_WB_ABSORB` 59.7 fps, `DM_VK_LINWB_SNAP` 56.3, `DM_VK_UP_RANGE` 30. The Odin 2's figure was one run: later runs spread from 33 to 57 fps until the write-back's
  staging was pooled too ([next entry](#a-1080p-linear-render-targets-write-back-staged-in-a-buffer-of-its-own-every-frame)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword HD](../../dm/games/01002DA013484000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 11).
### A 1080p linear render target's write-back staged in a buffer of its own every frame

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game with a frame-sized linear render target switches its present interval between 1 and 2 in play, and settles at 30 fps in some runs
  and near 60 in others; its long frames show `submit+present` far above the GPU time and no uploads, and `DM_VK_STG_STAT=1` counts about one `vkAllocateMemory` and
  one `vkFreeMemory` a frame, with requests given a buffer of their own `over 4 MB`.
- **Cause:** staging (`gfx_vk.c`, slices of pooled blocks) cut requests up to 4 MB from 8 MB blocks, and a larger one got a buffer of its own. Skyward Sword HD
  writes its 1920×1080 RGBA8 target (8.3 MB) back every frame, so every frame the render thread's `vkAllocateMemory` zeroed 8 MB (13.6% of that thread on an Ayn
  Odin 2), and the completion thread took the first-touch faults of reading the new pages and freed them, 7 to 8 ms in all before the chunk's fence, which the game's
  next frame waits on. `DM_VK_WB_ABSORB` (above) took away the upload that followed the write-back, not this.
- **Fix:** blocks of 16 MiB, and a request as large as a block is cut from one (a 1920×1080 RGBA16F target, 16.6 MB, fits too). At most 128 MiB of blocks, the same
  eight as before, and blocks left idle are still freed (`sp_trim`).
- **Check:** Skyward Sword HD in Skyloft on an Ayn Odin 2: 53.4, 32.9 and 56.9 fps before (frame p95 32 to 36 ms, 42 to 128 interval switches a run), 60.0, 59.9 and
  59.9 after (p95 17.6 to 18.8 ms, 4 switches), write-backs landing in 5.6 ms a frame on average instead of 7 to 8, and no staging buffer of its own in a run. On a
  OnePlus Pad 3, 57.5 fps before and 60.0 after. Odyssey, Brilliant Diamond, Animal Crossing and Link's Awakening unchanged on both, and Smash on the Pad 3.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword HD](../../dm/games/01002DA013484000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 11).
### An absorbed write-back compared whole every frame

- **Symptom:** Android only: with `DM_VK_WB_ABSORB=1`, a frame-sized linear render target still costs its completion thread milliseconds a frame (`ms landing and noting them` in the `[vk] frame …` line), before the chunk's fence the game waits on.
- **Cause:** `ab_note` compared every row of guest memory with the GPU's copy after each write-back, to keep a target the CPU had written from being adopted: for Skyward Sword HD's 1920×1080 RGBA8 target, two more reads of 8.3 MB every frame.
- **Fix:** before the write-back lands, `ab_clean` asks the dirty tracker whether any whole page of the target was written since its last note (`g_abg`, the stamp each note took). If none was, guest memory still holds that write-back, the snapshot the pass took equals it, and the write-back makes guest memory the GPU's copy by itself, so `ab_note` compares only the rows with bytes in the edge pages a neighbour may write. nx2android ([github.com/shirai91/nx2android](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android), 70139b2) syncs only what the CPU wrote from the same write-protect tracking. `DM_VK_AB_CLEAN=0` (env.txt) compares every row, as before.
- **Check:** Skyward Sword HD in Skyloft on an Ayn Odin 2, two runs each way from the same install: landing and noting the write-backs took 4.51 and 4.55 ms a frame against 5.21 and 5.27 with `DM_VK_AB_CLEAN=0` (frames 900 to 3000), 2,995 of 2,998 notes compared in their edge rows alone, none refused, none differing; 60.0 fps every run.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword HD](../../dm/games/01002DA013484000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 11).

### The pipeline cache written during play

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game that stutters while it builds pipelines does it again at every start after the app was stopped by force (swiped away, updated, the
  benchmark's stop), though `pipelines made again from the list` at start shows the list was kept.
- **Cause:** the list of pipelines to make again at start (`<DM_PIPELINE_CACHE>.keys4`) is appended as each one is made, but the driver's compiled binaries live in the
  `VkPipelineCache`, which was written only when the app paused and after the start-up list finished. A stop by force lost what the session compiled, and the next start
  made the list again without the binaries: about 13 ms a pipeline on a OnePlus Pad 3, while the game played.
- **Fix:** `pcache_made` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`) counts the pipelines `pipe_get` makes and, after every 64 and at most every 15 s, writes the cache on a low-priority thread
  of its own (copying out tens of megabytes held the game's draws up when done on its thread). The write goes to a `.new` file renamed over the cache, so a stop during
  it leaves the last one whole.
- **Check:** Super Smash Bros. Ultimate's battle on a OnePlus Pad 3: the cache written twice in the first run's battle (10.6 and 11.6 MB), the second run starting from
  the 11.6 MB one; the battles at 32.3 and 34.0 fps with frame p95 37.7 and 38.6 ms, against 16.8 to 29.7 fps and 73 to 81 ms in the baselines.
- **Found on:** [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 5).

### Pipelines met for the first time built in the background

- **Symptom:** Android only: short drops to 40 or 30 fps the first time a game shows an effect, a character or a menu in a session, with `new pipelines` in the long-frame lines; worse on a OnePlus Pad 3, whose driver builds pipelines several times slower than an Ayn Odin 2's.
- **Cause:** a pipeline the replay met for the first time was compiled on the replay thread, in the frame that first drew with it: 20 to 35 ms a graphics pipeline on an Adreno 830. The start-up list (`prewarm_thread`) only covers what earlier sessions met.
- **Fix:** `pipe_get` hands such a pipeline to two threads of their own (`vk-apipe`, `apipe_thread`) and leaves its draws out until it is built, when every target of the draw's pass was drawn into in this present and the two before (`rt_note`, `vtex rt_runs`): on a target drawn every frame the draw shows a few frames late. A target drawn for the first time (an environment map made once) still builds at once, as do a linear render target's pipelines (the CPU reads it back) and transform feedback's (later draws read what it writes). Shaders, pass and fragment variant are made under the replay's lock (`pipe_prep`) and the compile without it, as for the start-up list. The rule is nx2android's ([github.com/shirai91/nx2android](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android), 0d0468d). `DM_VK_ASYNC_PIPE=0` (env.txt) builds every pipeline at once.
- **Check:** runs from an empty pipeline cache (`DM_PIPELINE_CACHE` in a new folder), with and without `DM_VK_ASYNC_PIPE=0`, from one install. Super Smash Bros. Ultimate's battle on a OnePlus Pad 3, two runs each way: 40.0 and 40.2 fps against 27.9 and 26.5, frame p95 33 and 35 ms against 96 and 85; pipelines built on the replay thread 114 and 95 (2.1 and 2.2 s) against 564 and 465 (14.6 and 12.5 s), long frames 43.8 ms on average against 88.6 and 84.7. Super Mario Odyssey's opening on an Ayn Odin 2: 59.9 fps against 56.0, 295 pipelines (2.7 s) on the replay thread against 392 (5.5 s), most of the rest at the scene's load, where every target is new; 14 long frames against 44. The runs' last screenshots draw whole: Smash's stage, fighters, effects and HUD, Odyssey's airship.
- **Found on:** [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) and [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 5).

### Shader modules made only for pipelines the driver's cache lacks

- **Symptom:** Android only: every start takes seconds longer than on other phones before the first frame, with a warm pipeline cache. A frame 1 of 7.7 to 15 s in the long-frame line, whose `in all` is mostly shader modules (the cumulative line's `shader modules N (T ms)`) while its pipelines take a fraction of a second.
- **Cause:** some drivers do a compile's work in `vkCreateShaderModule`, which their pipeline cache does not keep. Samsung's Xclipse 950 driver takes 0.8 to 0.9 ms a module (a standalone probe over 2,000 of Brilliant Diamond's modules: 0.77 to 0.94 ms), against about 0.1 ms on the Adreno 830. Brilliant Diamond meets 8,844 modules before its first frame (Unity's shader warm-up), so its 2,982 pipelines came out of the cache in 0.2 to 0.4 s while their modules took 6.1 to 9.6 s, at every start.
- **Fix:** where the GPU has `VK_EXT_shader_module_identifier` and pipeline creation cache control, a shader's module is only its identifier, a hash of its code (`vkGetShaderModuleCreateInfoIdentifierEXT`, 0.005 ms there), until a pipeline needs it (`gfx_vk_lmod.inc`). A pipeline is first asked for from the identifiers of the modules not made yet, with `VK_PIPELINE_CREATE_FAIL_ON_PIPELINE_COMPILE_REQUIRED_BIT`. The driver's cache serves it (2 ms in the probe) or answers `VK_PIPELINE_COMPILE_REQUIRED` (0.02 ms). Only then are the modules made, from the files again through the same rewrites (`lm_code`: fragment variants, `spv_readonly`, `spv_pool_fit`, `spv_fit`), and the pipeline compiled as before. The SPIR-V is not kept in memory meanwhile: Brilliant Diamond's modules average about 28 KB. Compute pipelines take the same path. `DM_VK_MODULE_ID=0` (env.txt) makes every module at once, as before.
- **Check:** Brilliant Diamond on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950), warm cache: the first frame at 3.29 s against 9.7 to 17.9 s on `main`, its frame 1 1.2 s against 7.7 to 15 s. All 3,082 pipelines came from identifiers, with no module made; the language menu and Eterna City at 60 fps, as on `main`.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), the Xclipse 950).

### Set layouts sized by their highest binding number

- **Symptom:** Android only: memory runs out as a game builds its pipelines. The game process grows by 64 to 192 MB with many new pipelines, and the low-memory killer ends it while a scene loads. The growth isn't in shader modules or the pipeline cache, and destroying the pipelines doesn't give it back. Ocarina of Time on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 went from 3.8 GB at the title to 13.9 GB while loading Kokiri Forest. Its start-up list of 191 pipelines alone took the driver's heap to 9.4 GB.
- **Cause:** Samsung's Xclipse driver (24.0.533) keeps 64 bytes per binding number, up to a layout's highest binding.
  - The cost is paid in `vkCreateDescriptorSetLayout`, and again in every `vkCreatePipelineLayout` that uses that set layout.
  - A probe with one uniform buffer: binding 7 costs 0 MB, 65,535 costs 4 MB, 1,048,576 costs 64 MB, and 2,101,266 costs 128 MB.
  - Arm's Mali-G715 driver (a Pixel 9 Pro XL) sizes them the same way: 104 MB of native heap for such a shader's set layout and 16 MB for each pipeline layout over it. Ocarina of Time's start-up list of 621 pipelines took the native heap to 6.7 GB within 12 s, and Android 17's per-app memory limiter killed the game at Kokiri Forest's load. These are the Mali bring-up's measurements for #277, which moved only the bindings behind the pool arrays; this entry's fix replaces it.
  - The translator puts some of NVN2's constant buffers far apart. 4,823 of Ocarina of Time's 42,848 shaders bind one at 1,048,576 or more, and its Kokiri fragment shaders use 2,101,266 (`0x201012`).
  - How it was found: the driver library's heap imports were redirected to counters by thread and call stack (a test build only). simpleperf can't run on this phone (SELinux), and heapprofd disconnects from the host's threads.
- **Fix:** each binding at 1024 or past it gets a dense number, in the old order, from just past the set's other bindings and past the capture variant's feedback buffers at 100 to 103 (`spv_bind.h`).
  - `sh_get` makes the map from `res.json` before the shader's modules, and gives its layout and `stage_set`'s writes the new numbers once the matching of `res.json` against the file (`spv_cb_ext`) is done.
  - `lm_code` rewrites the module's `OpDecorate … Binding` last, after every other rewrite. The lazy module (`gfx_vk_lmod.inc`) keeps the map, so a module made later for a pipeline gets the same numbers.
  - Nothing on disk changes, so there's no ANDROID_CHAIN bump.
  - It applies on every GPU. Only these shaders' modules change, so the driver's pipeline cache misses once for them. The start-up list's records name shaders, not bindings, so they still hold. `DM_VK_BIND_COMPACT=0` keeps the old numbers.
- **Check:** on the Galaxy Z Flip 7:
  - **Start-up list:** Ocarina of Time's 193-record list rebuilt in 4.2 s, with the native heap at 157 to 173 MB and 3.8 GB in total at the title (9.4 GB of heap and 12.9 GB in total before).
  - **Kokiri Forest:** it loaded, with 95 shaders (341 bindings) renumbered, at 8.6 GB at most and no kill.
  - **Other games:** Brilliant Diamond (60.0 fps at Verity Lakefront) and Frostpunk (29.6 fps in the city) had no shader renumbered and draw as before.
  - **Tests:** `test_spv_bind.py` checks the map and the rewrite. Over Ocarina of Time's spv folder, 4,897 of 59,199 modules change, all pass spirv-val, and no two resources share a binding.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950).

### A crash in the driver's shader compiler names the pipelines being built

- **Symptom:** Android only: a crash in the Vulkan driver's shader compiler (`libllvm-qgl.so` in the stack) while a game builds pipelines, and nothing in the log says which shaders, or on which thread.
- **Cause:** the replay noted nothing while it built a pipeline, and the crash line did not name the crashing thread. Builds run on the replay thread, the start-up list's (`vk-prewarm`) and the background builds' (`vk-apipe`), so the stack alone did not say which build it was.
- **Fix:** every build takes one of eight slots for its compile (`pb_begin` / `pb_end` around `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` in `pipe_make` and `vkCreateComputePipelines` in `cpipe_get`), with its shader keys, topology, colour target count and whether it takes the vertex `.z` or a fragment variant. The crash dump prints every slot in use after the backtrace (`[dm] pipeline being built by the crashing thread: …`), and the `CRASH` line ends with the thread's id and name (`crash.c`, through `crash_set_gfx_hook`). The keys are the translator's bundle keys, which name the shader's `spv/<key>.spv` and `res.json` in the build folder. From nx2android ([github.com/shirai91/nx2android](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android), 3433389).
- **Check:** a test build that crashed the 40th pipeline build on purpose, Super Mario Odyssey on an Ayn Odin 2: `[dm] CRASH sig=11 … thread 12790 "vk-prewarm"`, then `[dm] pipeline being built by the crashing thread: thread 12790 "vk-prewarm", graphics, shaders 384597a04ae3928e 12bd959fa3ef29ce - - (vertex .z variant), topology 3, 6 colour targets`.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) on a Retroid Pocket 5 ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 2): its pipeline led to [subgroup operations in a stage the Adreno 650 runs none in](#subgroup-operations-rewritten-for-stages-the-gpu-runs-none-in).

### A start-up list entry with a mesh shader in its vertex slot

- **Symptom:** Android only: every start crashes a few seconds in, soon after the 30th frame, in the driver's shader compiler (`libllvm-qgl.so`), with `[dm] pipeline being built by the crashing thread: thread … "vk-prewarm", graphics, shaders <A> <B> - - (vertex .z variant)`. Shader A's `res.json` says `"stage": "ms"` (or `ts`, `tc`, `te`), and with `DM_VK_PREWARM=0` the game plays. Ocarina of Time on a OnePlus Pad 3: `CRASH sig=11` at `libllvm-qgl.so+0x63a4e4`, shaders `0c1ffecd02018fb0` and `7ad8843afe105a32`, with raw global memory on or off.
- **Cause:** a pipeline Vulkan forbids, built by the replay; not a driver bug.
  - `sh_get` makes every stage the replay doesn't draw (task, mesh, tessellation) `SH_TS`, with the vertex stage bit. A draw with one is skipped before it has a key (`vk_draw__`), so play never builds such a pipeline.
  - The start-up list (`prewarm_thread`, [the pipeline cache written during play](#the-pipeline-cache-written-during-play)) makes its entries from their recorded shader keys alone. The list on that Pad 3 held an entry another build had written, with Ocarina of Time's mesh shader `0c1ffecd02018fb0` in the vertex slot; test builds of task and mesh shaders as compute had run there.
  - Made as a vertex stage, the `MeshEXT` module crashed the compiler at every start.
- **Fix:** `pk_stages_ok` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`): each shader of a key must be its slot's stage (vertex, fragment, geometry; the fourth slot empty, as tessellation draws are skipped).
  - The start-up list leaves out an entry that isn't, with one red line naming the shader, and counts them in its `pipelines made again from the list` line.
  - `pipe_make` refuses such a key from any caller (`[RED] [vk] a pipeline with … shader … in its … slot is not made`).
- **Check:** in Ocarina of Time's packed shaders on the Mac, `0c1ffecd02018fb0.res.json` has stage `ms`, and its `.spv` is a `MeshEXT` entry point (SPIR-V 1.4, `SPV_EXT_mesh_shader`). On the Pad 3, from its own list: `main` crashed as above, and with this check Kokiri played (4.0 fps, raw global memory off). The list logged `an entry with task, mesh or tessellation shader d4171cf9adf0504b in the vertex slot is left out`, and its summary said `755 made, … 45 failed, 32 left out (a shader in the wrong slot), of the first 832 of 833 records`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on a OnePlus Pad 3.

### A start-up list entry whose compile never finishes is skipped from the next start

- **Symptom:** Android only: every start ends at the same point a few seconds in, during the start-up list (`vk-prewarm` in the crash dump's `pipeline being built` line), while the game plays with `DM_VK_PREWARM=0`. Since this guard, the start after such an end logs `[RED] [vk] start-up list: the entry with shaders … crashed the driver's shader compiler at an earlier start, so it is left out from now on`, or `… was being compiled when an earlier start ended (a crash, a kill or a stop) …`; after two crashed starts in a row, `start-up list: two starts in a row crashed in it, so the whole list (N entries) is set aside …`.
- **Cause:** the list (`<DM_PIPELINE_CACHE>.keys4`) makes the pipelines of earlier sessions again at every start. An entry the driver's compiler crashes on, or hangs in, therefore ends every start, and the list is in the app's internal storage, where a player can't clear it. [A mesh shader in the vertex slot](#a-start-up-list-entry-with-a-mesh-shader-in-its-vertex-slot) did this on a OnePlus Pad 3, which the replay itself built wrong; a driver's own compiler bug would do the same.
- **Fix:** `pk_guard.h`, used by `prewarm_thread`:
  - Before each entry's shader modules are made (`sh_get`, `pipe_prep`), its record goes to a marker file (`<cache>.making4`), emptied when its compile returns. If that thread crashes meanwhile, the crash dump's graphics hook (`pb_dump`) adds a flag after the record: one `pwrite`, which a signal handler may make.
  - At the next start (`pk_guard_init`, before the session's first pipeline), an entry still in the marker goes to the skip list (`<cache>.skip4`) with the red line above. The list is written again without it, and it is not appended again when play makes that pipeline.
  - **Two starts in a row that crashed inside the list** (the marker flagged both times, counted in `<cache>.strikes4`) set the whole list aside as `<cache>.keys4.aside`, with one red line (`two starts in a row crashed in it, so the whole list (N entries) is set aside …`). The session starts a new list. A list with many bad entries then costs two crashes, not one per entry; Ocarina of Time's on that Pad 3 held 32. A start that ended in the list without the crash hook (a kill, a stop) starts the count again.
  - When play does need it for a draw, `pipe_make` says so first (`[RED] [vk] a pipeline the start-up list skips … is compiled now for a draw`), so a crash in that compile names it in the log as well.
  - A start that ends during a compile for another reason (killed for memory, stopped) also skips that entry, which costs only its compile ahead of play.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/tools/tests/test_pk_guard.py`: a session that finishes its compiles leaves nothing; one that ends in a compile leaves that entry once, flagged when the crash hook ran; a record missing its busy byte still counts, while a part record does not; the skip list holds each entry once and survives a part record at its end; the next start leaves out the entry the last one ended in. The host compiles for Android (NDK 28.2) with its usual flags. On a OnePlus Pad 3 whose Ocarina of Time list held 32 entries with a mesh shader in the vertex slot (this branch without the check that leaves them out): the first start crashed in `libllvm-qgl.so` on `0c1ffecd02018fb0 7ad8843afe105a32`. The second logged that entry's `crashed the driver's shader compiler at an earlier start` line, left it out, and crashed on the next one, `25c4a740753e7d7a`. That is one entry cleared per crashing start, before the whole-list fallback, which is host-tested only (`test_pk_guard.py`: two flagged starts in a row set the list aside; a clean start or an unflagged one starts the count again).

### A GPU hang names the submission it was in

- **Symptom:** Android only: `[RED] [vk] vkQueueSubmit failed: -4` (`VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`), after which the picture stays as it was (a loading screen for good) and the GPU time reads 0, with a frame of about 3 s just before it and nothing else in the log to say where.
- **Cause:** the GPU hung on a submission. The kernel's report of it (kgsl) needs root on a phone, and the replay noted nothing of what a submission held. On a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830) the driver's hang recovery cut a hung submission short (its fence signalled, its end timestamp never written) about 250 ms in, and gave the context up only when one ran past 3 s.
- **Fix:** each chunk keeps breadcrumbs of what it recorded (`crumb`: its passes with their size, its draws with their shader keys, vertices and instances, its dispatches with their groups), and the completion thread logs them for a chunk that took over 200 ms on the GPU (`gpu_slow`, `GPU_SLOW_NS`; the one before it had completed, so it is the chunk the GPU was in), at most three: `[RED] [vk] submission N took T ms on the GPU, its end timestamp never written (the driver's hang recovery cut it short)`, then a line per pass, draw and dispatch. The first lost fence or submission logs what `VK_EXT_device_fault` gives where the driver has it (`gpu_fault_log`: each fault's kind and address, the vendor's records). A file `vk_mid` beside `DM_DUMP_DIR` holding 1 (`chmod 666` when the shell writes it) cuts the chunks after every draw, so the slow one names a single draw.
