- **Check:** Breath of the Wild on a OnePlus Pad 3: three submissions of 249, 277 and 281 ms, each with its end timestamp unwritten and each holding the draw of vertex shader `2decd3e894b3736a` and fragment shader `26ae1640f3448ba4`, before the device was lost; with chunks of one draw, that draw alone. `VK_EXT_device_fault` gave no address and no vendor record (a hang, not a page fault).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) on a OnePlus Pad 3 (the hang itself is open there).

### A GPU hang names the dispatch it was in, with its bindings

- **Symptom:** Android only: the device is lost (`vkQueueSubmit failed: -4`) after a few submissions the driver's hang recovery cut short. The replay names each of those submissions and what it recorded, but they hold twenty or more passes, draws and dispatches, the same ones each time, and `VK_EXT_device_fault`'s addresses name no buffer (Mario Kart World: reads at `0x3f800580`, 1.0f's bit pattern plus an offset, and at `0xaac0`, null plus an offset).
- **Cause:** a chunk's breadcrumbs ([A GPU hang names the submission it was in](#a-gpu-hang-names-the-submission-it-was-in)) say what the replay recorded into it, not how far the GPU got. Finding the item meant skipping shaders by key one run at a time, and robustness features (`robustBufferAccess2`, `robustImageAccess2`) did not stop the faults, which left the bindings and the shader itself to check.
- **Fix:** `DM_VK_BC=1` (a diagnostic, off by default; `gfx_vk_bc.inc`):
  - Before each dispatch the GPU writes the dispatch's number into a host-visible ring (`vkCmdFillBuffer`, then a barrier, so the dispatch starts after the write). After it, a barrier then a fill writes the number into the next word. A dispatch with only the first word written began and never ended.
  - The host keeps each number's shader key, groups and bindings, as `stage_set` resolved them: uniform buffers with their size and first 16 words, storage buffers with their address, size and first 32 words, textures and images with their serial, size, layers, levels and Vulkan format, and whether a binding fell back to a stand-in.
  - `gpu_slow` lists a slow chunk's dispatches up to its first one never begun, and a lost device lists the last eight begun and never ended.
  - The barriers make each dispatch wait for the one before, so frame times with it on are not the game's.
  - The recorder thread (`DM_VK_RECORDER=1`) makes its calls later, so breadcrumbs stay off with it.
- **Check:** Mario Kart World on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Android 16):
  - Over two runs, three slow submissions and the loss each named compute shader `2a89b6d4df731b8d` (one workgroup) as begun and never ended, with the next dispatch never begun.
  - Its bindings: a 24-bone skeleton's records (parents `-1 0 0 1 1 1 2 2 3 3 4 5 10 11 12 12 13 13 13 13 16 17 18 19`), identity matrices in its storage buffer, and a 24×636 RGBA16F texture with 3 layers. The data was sound, which pointed at the shader's three 24-iteration loops instead.
  - With it skipped, the next one named was `e2a5bd13907993e3`, the same kind of shader.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md) on a Galaxy S25 Edge (the hang itself is open there).

### A refused submission doesn't stop the game (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: `[RED] [vk] vkQueueSubmit failed: <n>` with `<n>` not -4 (an AYN Thor Pro's Qualcomm driver gave -3 in Skyrim's Riverwood), after which no frame comes, the sound goes on, and Android reports the app as not responding.
- **Cause:** the chunk's fence went with the refused submission, so it was never on the queue and never signalled, and the completion thread waited on it for good (`vkWaitForFences` with no timeout). The replay waited behind that chunk. -3 isn't one of `vkQueueSubmit`'s results; the specification's failures leave the fence as it was, so it can go again.
- **Fix:** `submit_or_fence` (`gfx_vk.c`), for the replay's and the recorder's submissions: a refused one goes again without its command buffer, so its fence reaches the queue and the chunk completes with its work dropped (`[RED] … its fence went alone`). When the queue refuses that too, the GPU counts as lost (`gpu_fault_log`: the start record's `device_lost=`, and the next start on the system's driver after a `DM_VK_DRIVER` one), and the chunk keeps the queue's result in `sub_r`, so the completion thread doesn't wait on its fence. The present's own submission (`front_android.c`) is unchanged.
- **Check:** `DM_VK_REFUSE_SUBMIT=<n>` refuses the nth submission as that driver did, `=-<n>` its fence alone too.
- **Found on:** [The Elder Scrolls V: Skyrim](../../dm/games/01000A10041EA000/README.md) on an AYN Thor Pro (Adreno 740), droidplay-core#444.

### Command recording on a thread of its own (Android)

- **Symptom:** a game with thousands of draws a frame is held by the replay thread's CPU time. In a profile of that thread the driver's recording calls are a large share: Breath of the Wild on a OnePlus Pad 3, `vkCmdDrawIndexed` 9.9%, `vkCmdBindDescriptorSets` 8.2%, `vkCmdBeginRenderPass` 1.4%, and binds, copies and dynamic state besides. Meanwhile the phone's other cores are idle.
- **Cause:** the replay made every `vkCmd*` call itself, and ended and submitted each chunk, on its one thread.
- **Fix:** `DM_VK_RECORDER=1` (`dm/android/gfx_vk_rec.inc`): off by default, and on for Breath of the Wild and Ocarina of Time through their `android_defaults.txt`.
  - Each wrapper copies its call's arguments (arrays and structures included) into a ring, and a recorder thread (`vk-record`) makes the calls in order. It also ends each chunk's command buffer and submits it with its fence (`rec_submit`). A command buffer that fails to end is not submitted: its fence goes alone, so the chunk still completes.
  - The replay waits for it only in `mtl_present`, before the frame goes to the front end, whose blit uses the same queue.
  - The completion thread waits for a chunk's submission before its fence (`rec_wait_submitted`), and before it starts timing the chunk for the hang report.
  - Every `vkCmd*` of the replay is made under `g_bk`, so the ring has one producer.
  - The recorder joins the frame's performance hint session.
  - The idea is nx2android's (`784c9d4`).
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild on a OnePlus Pad 3, with descriptor set reuse, 512 draws a chunk, and one draw skipped by a local test switch (it hangs that GPU, [Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md) problem 14):
  - replay CPU 30.7 → 24.2 ms a frame;
  - with `DM_FPS_TARGET=30`, 30.0 fps (frame p95 38.4–38.9 ms, replay 17.3–17.5 ms) against 29.2–29.6 fps without it;
  - at the 60 fps target it stayed at 20 fps in four runs of five.
- **Check, Ocarina of Time** (its default since 2026-11-11), Kokiri Forest, about 2,100 draws a frame, bench route `kokiri`, with the recorder's descriptor writes ([below](#descriptor-set-writes-on-the-replay-thread-with-the-recorder-on-android)):
  - Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740), one run each from thermal status 0, recorder off → on: the replay thread 30.2 → 18.8 ms of CPU a frame, 14.5 → 14.9 fps (the GPU is busy 50 ms a frame either way), the same picture, 6.2 GB of PSS either way. The whole process used 106 → 120 ms of CPU a frame: the recorder thread's work is new.
  - OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830), the recorder together with the growing report address set ([Report tables that dropped entries](#report-tables-that-dropped-entries)), against main before both: the replay 34 → 14 ms, the GPU 34.9 → 21.4 ms as its clock went from about 650 to 1,100 MHz, and 19.5 → 19.9 fps, because the frame step still holds 3 vsyncs. With 2 held by hand (`DM_FPS_TARGET=30 DM_FPS_HOLD=off`): 27.5 fps.
  - Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830), the same comparison: the replay 38 → 17 ms, 17.6 → 19.0 fps; the GPU stays at 41 ms a frame, its clock at 525 MHz.

### Descriptor set writes on the replay thread with the recorder on (Android)

- **Symptom:** with `DM_VK_RECORDER=1`, `vkUpdateDescriptorSets` is still in the replay thread's profile, as its largest call into the driver: 5.3 ms a frame of Mario
  Kart World's race on a Galaxy S25 Edge (about 2,400 draws, 5,000 sets), 2.1 of Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest on a Galaxy Z Fold 8, 10.4 of the race on a OnePlus Pad 3 with raw global
  memory.
- **Cause:** the recorder took the `vkCmd*` calls and the submissions, and a set was written on the replay thread before its bind was queued.
- **Fix:** the writes go through the ring too (`gfx_vk_rec.inc` `rec_vkUpdateDescriptorSets`, record `R_UPDDS`), packed by `rec_desc.h` (each write with its image
  infos, buffer infos or buffer views), ahead of the set's bind, so the recorder writes a set before it binds it, as the replay did.
  - Every write of the replay takes the same road (transform feedback's bindings, the texture pool's set, the copies' and decodes' sets), so two writes of one set
    keep their order and never run on two threads.
  - A set's pool is reset, or the set handed out again, only once its chunk completed, after the recorder submitted it and so after every write queued for it.
  - The replay may take sets from a pool while the recorder writes others of it: Vulkan asks for one thread at a time per set written and per pool allocated from.
  - A call this cannot pack (a `pNext` chain, copies between sets, more than 128 writes or 256 KB: the texture pool's first whole write) is made directly after a drain.
  - `DM_VK_REC_DESC=0` (env.txt): the writes stay on the replay thread, as before. Without the recorder nothing changes.
- **Check:**
  - `dm/tools/tests/test_rec_desc.py`: a set with every kind of descriptor comes back whole from its packed block after the caller's arrays are reused; chains, unknown
    types and oversized calls are left to a direct call.
  - Mario Kart World's race on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830), recorder on, in the replay thread's profile: `vkUpdateDescriptorSets` 5.3 ms a frame with
    `DM_VK_REC_DESC=0`, the packing and queuing 0.6 ms with it, and no driver code left on the thread; the recorder thread used 41% of a core. The race's picture was
    right, and 4.2 million writes were queued with none made directly.
  - Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest on a Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840), main's defaults: the replay 30.7 ms a frame with the recorder against 35.2 without, of which the
    report-address set's walks (`rc_occ_has`, `rc_occ_add`) are 21.0 and 19.9; so everything else is 9.7 ms against 15.3. 17.4 against 17.8 fps: the GPU holds the frame
    there, at 43 ms. The forest was lit as before. With `DM_VK_MID_DRAWS=512` as well it fell to 10.2 fps (the GPU 64 ms a frame), and with set reuse it stayed at 17.1.
  - Breath of the Wild's `outdoors` route there (the recorder and set reuse are its defaults; a host on the phone's installed base with this change): the replay 16.4–16.9 ms
    a frame against 18.5–18.6 with `DM_VK_REC_DESC=0` at 3,630 draws, 29.8 fps and the same picture both ways.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md) and
  [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### Descriptor sets bound again within a chunk (Android)

- **Symptom:** a game with thousands of draws a frame spends a large part of the replay thread in `vkUpdateDescriptorSets`: about 7,300 sets a frame in Breath of the Wild. The Odin 2's profile of 2026-11-07 put it at 3.4 ms a frame.
- **Cause:** every draw copies its constant buffers to new arena offsets, and the offsets were part of each set. So no set matched the draw before, and `stage_set` wrote every set of every stage.
- **Fix:** `DM_VK_SET_REUSE=1` (off by default) (`gfx_vk_draw.inc` `sh_dyn`, `sr_find`, `sr_put`).
  - Constant buffers become dynamic uniform buffers, at most `maxDescriptorSetUniformBuffersDynamic / 2` for a vertex or fragment shader, and their offsets go to the bind.
  - A set already written with the same descriptors in the chunk being recorded is bound again. Lookup is a hash of the layout and every descriptor, compared whole.
  - An entry holds only for its recording, because a set is one chunk's and written once per recording.
  - It is off by default because it changes every shader's set layout, so pipeline caches and start-up lists miss once. Dynamic uniform buffers have run only on the Adreno 830 so far.
  - The design is nx2android's, BOTWRecompAndroid's and triasbrata's nxnative-android's, written anew.
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild on a OnePlus Pad 3, with one draw skipped by a local test switch (it hangs that GPU, [Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md) problem 14):
  - 86.9% of sets bound again (6,312 of 7,267 a frame);
  - the replay's CPU 33.4–33.6 → 32.1–32.3 ms a frame, interleaved runs;
  - GPU and picture unchanged.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md).

### The texture pool read by ID on Vulkan (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, an NVN2 game: `[RED] [vk] shader …: one descriptor set of its layout is larger than a whole pool (an array of 1048576 elements): its draws are dropped`, and what those shaders draw is missing.
- **Cause:** a texture handle the translator cannot trace to one slot indexes arrays sized to the whole ID ranges ([Texture handles from game memory index the pools](shaders.md#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools)): 2^20 images per image type and 4,096 samplers. The replay made a descriptor set per shader and stage, and a set that size fits no descriptor pool, so `ds_fits` dropped those draws.
- **Fix:** one descriptor set, at set 4, serves every such shader (`gfx_vk_pool.inc`). `spv_pool.h` rewrites each module when it is made.
  - The set holds four bindings:
    - 0: the texture pool's IDs as sampled images, as many as the game's texture pools have (33,792 for Ocarina of Time, about 2 MB of descriptors). Every image type of the shaders aliases this binding, and each element holds the view the reads asked for.
    - 1: the sampler pool.
    - 2: the same samplers made with comparison on, which a depth read takes.
    - 3: the reads' marks.
  - Indices are clamped to the binding and decorated `NonUniform` (`VK_EXT_descriptor_indexing`).
  - Only the textures the shaders read are made, as on the Mac (`pool_gpu`).
    - Each read sets its view type's bit in a word per ID. The word is read first, and stored only when the bit is clear.
    - A chunk's first pool draw takes the IDs marked since and clears their marks, so a later read marks an ID again. It makes their textures and views, and looks again at the IDs read in that chunk only, as `stage_textures` looks at a draw's own. That picks up CPU rewrites and new registrations, and brings no released texture back.
    - A registration whose storage is in no memory pool the host maps (the game finalized it) is not made: `vt_create` would read the freed pool object. The Mac guards the same with `tex_storage_live`.
    - An ID not made yet reads 0 through a null descriptor (`VK_EXT_robustness2`), whatever type it is read as. The pool set is off on a GPU without null descriptors.
  - The set is never written while a chunk that bound it may run. A change goes to a version no chunk in flight holds (3 at most), brought up to date with the elements changed since it was last written. When every version is in flight, the newest serves as it is only if it lacks nothing but new views. If a view was replaced or dropped since, the oldest version's chunk is waited for, since the replaced view may be destroyed later.
  - A fragment shader whose result the mark's store cannot change keeps its depth test before it runs (`EarlyFragmentTests`), as `dmsh_build.py` does on the Mac.
  - The first shader that has a pool array starts the pool set, so a game without one never makes it. Room is left for the stages' own descriptors, and `maxPerStageResources` is checked too.
  - `DM_VK_POOL=0` turns it off; `DM_VK_POOL_LOG=1` logs each ID as it is first read.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_pool.py`; with `DM_SPV_DIR`, all 4,892 of Ocarina of Time's modules with pool arrays are rewritten and pass `spirv-val`, 3,759 of them with early depth tests added. Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest on a Galaxy Z Fold 8 (`bench_android.tsv` route `kokiri`, two runs each, `DM_VK_POOL=0` against on):
  - before, the run's 103 pool shaders were refused and 49,592 of their draws dropped in its first minute in the forest; after, none refused or dropped, and 6,031 draws bound the set;
  - the forest's draws read 12-15 IDs (the distant scenery's texture sets), and each chunk's sync took 0.33 ms on average;
  - 1.6 → 1.7 fps, frame p50 640-650 → 593-611 ms, GPU p50 138.6-138.8 → 119.4-128.2 ms;
  - the picture did not change there: the forest stays black for another cause, see the game's note.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#android) (Switch 2).

### Descriptor arrays longer than the replay writes (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android: the game's graphics memory climbs while a scene plays, until the low-memory killer ends it in the foreground (`lmkd: Reclaim 'org.nxnative.android:game' … device is low on swap … thrashing` on a Galaxy S25 Edge). The process's own resident memory stays flat; the kernel's GPU pages (`/sys/class/kgsl/kgsl/page_alloc`) grow, and `DM_VK_MEMREPORT=1` puts nearly all of it in descriptor pools. Without the fix, `[RED] [vk] texture array of 8192 elements: the first 1024 are bound` at the shader's first draw.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's texture array from a buffer ([An array of handles in a uniform buffer](shaders.md#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer)) is as long as a 64 KB uniform buffer's handles since [A table of handles longer than the array](shaders.md#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array): 8,192 elements, its index clamped to 8,191. The Mac reads it through a pointer with a run-time bound. The Vulkan replay writes at most the first 1,024 elements (`stage_set`), but a set layout must hold as many descriptors as the module's array is long, so each set of such a shader reserved 8,192 combined image samplers and two sets filled a pool (16,384 of them, 12.4 MB on the Adreno 830). Sets are kept per shader and chunk, and a chunk's pools are reset only past 16,384 sets, so the pools grew with each scene that drew those shaders: Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door's 75 reflection shaders (a light's texture per handle in c14) took 140 pools (1.7 GB) by Mario's house and 539 (6.7 GB) in the prologue's sea scene.
- **Fix:** `spv_harr.h` shortens each such array to the 1,024 elements written when its module is made (`gfx_vk_draw.inc` `spv_fit_harr`), outside the pool set, and clamps every index into it to 1,023 again with `UMin` (decorated `NonUniform` when the index was). `sh_get` gives the layout the same count once every module of the shader was rewritten; a module with another use of the array (loaded whole, passed to a function) is left as it is with a red line, and its layout keeps the whole array. An index past 1,023 read an unwritten descriptor before (undefined) and reads element 1,023 now. `DM_VK_HARR=0` turns it off.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_harr.py`; with `DM_SPV_DIR`, every module with such an array is rewritten and passes `spirv-val`: Paper Mario's 75, Ocarina of Time's 5 and Link's Awakening's 1. Paper Mario on the Galaxy S25 Edge, a new file through the prologue: before, the low-memory killer ended the game at the sea scene in four runs of four (the pools at 539, 6.7 GB, in the run that reported them); after, 34 pools (427 MB) there and 44 (550 MB) after 37 minutes, the kernel's GPU pages at most 1.6 GB against 7.6 GB, no kill, and the stretch played on through the first battle into the plaza at a median 29.9 fps.
- **Found on:** [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md).

### Raw global memory on the Vulkan replay (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, a game whose shaders load or store at addresses they read from memory: `[RED] [vk] storage buffer of shader … (c255 +0) is not a bound buffer: it reads 0`, printed once. Every such load reads 0 and every such store goes nowhere, and whatever those loads feed is wrong: zeros, or NaN that later passes spread. 2,247 of Ocarina of Time's shaders bind the table (731 compute, 802 vertex, 605 fragment, 98 mesh and 11 task; 33 of them store). All 1,108 of Frostpunk's `meshsolid` and `meshtranslucent` shaders bind it, and its Generator, buildings and props are invisible.
- **Cause:** the translator sends those accesses through the raw pool table, a storage buffer under the sentinel slot 0xFF, and physical pointers: the address plus the delta of the pool holding it ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](shaders.md#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)). The Mac's pools are no-copy buffers over the guest's own pages (`nvn_mtl.m` `gmem_map`). The Vulkan replay copies what each draw reads into its arena, so no pool had a device address, and `stage_set` bound 16 zero bytes for the table.
- **Fix:** for a game that opts in, on a Qualcomm Adreno or a Samsung Xclipse with `VK_KHR_buffer_device_address` and either `VK_EXT_map_memory_placed` or `VK_ANDROID_external_memory_android_hardware_buffer` (`gfx_vk_gmem.inc`):
  - **Which games:** those that opt in with `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM=1`, in their `android_defaults.txt` (Frostpunk's and Ocarina of Time's, and Mario Kart World's for its storage buffers in place alone: its shaders don't read the table) or `env.txt`. `front_android.c` reads both before the window's surface, so the device is made with the extensions only then; for every other game the device, its pools and its bindings are as before.
  - **Which GPUs:**
    - **Qualcomm's Adreno**, with its driver or Turnip.
    - **Samsung's Xclipse:** its driver compiles the physical pointers, and it takes the AHardwareBuffer route (it has no `VK_EXT_map_memory_placed`). On a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950, driver 24.0.533), Frostpunk's meshes draw. The pages read at 17.6 GB/s, so they're cached. The city runs at the same 29.5 fps, and PSS grows about 0.2 GB. Raw stores aren't shown on it: Frostpunk has none.
    - **Another GPU** with BDA and AHardwareBuffer import (Mali) would take the AHardwareBuffer route too, whose dma-buf mapping and CPU coherence aren't shown there. It stays off unless `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_ANYGPU=1`.
    - The dma-buf's size is checked against the piece before it's mapped.
  - **Placed pools:** a pool's bytes are copied into an allocation of a host-visible, coherent and cached type with a device address, and the allocation is mapped at the pool's own address, replacing its anonymous pages. The game, the replay's CPU copies and the GPU's raw accesses then reach the same memory, as on the Mac, and the raw stores land in guest memory themselves. Banana Native maps its pools the same way.
  - **Without placed maps** (the Galaxy S25 Edge's Adreno 830 driver), the same design as nx2android's zero-copy pools, written anew: a piece's bytes go into an `AHardwareBuffer` (a CPU-cached BLOB), its dma-buf is mapped `MAP_SHARED | MAP_FIXED` over the pool's pages, and the buffer is imported into Vulkan with a device address. It is imported before the pages are replaced, so a failure leaves the pool as it was. Pieces are at most 256 MiB, since the Adreno 830's allocator service aborted on an 812 MB BLOB in nx2android's tests. `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_AHB=1` takes this route where both exist.
  - **Why not mirrors:** without `VK_EXT_external_memory_host` (the Adreno 840 has none) a host pointer can't be imported. GPU copies of the pools would double their memory and need every CPU write tracked or copied again before each draw that may read anywhere in them.
  - **Which pools:** those the game took a buffer address in (`mtl_pool_addressed`, as the Mac lists only addressed pools), each placed when the game first takes one, before it has the address. Driver memory is committed when it is allocated: placing every pool at its Initialize committed 4.9 GB in Ocarina of Time, and the low-memory killer ended the game at Kokiri Forest. Its buffer-addressed pools are 1.8 GB (3,982 pools); the others are 3.4 GB, one of them 2.4 GB, and it takes no texture addresses.
  - **Pools in chunks** (`gfx_vk_gmem_lazy.inc`): placed memory is committed when allocated and can't go to swap, so placing whole pools pinned pages the game never touched. Every pool is placed in 2 MiB chunks (one under 2 MiB as a single chunk), each at its first use:
    - when a storage buffer in one is bound, the chunks it covers, as one allocation, so it binds in place;
    - when a word in a storage or uniform buffer that a shader binding the raw pool table reads points into one, or a word in the first 4 KiB at such a pointer does (`gm_lazy_ptrs`, two levels): that chunk and the next, at once. A buffer is scanned again at most every 100 ms. Storage buffers alone missed one: in the Sacred Grove's Mad Scrub fight Link and the scrubs went dark, lit with every written chunk placed (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_WRITTEN=1`; the OoT session's q148, q156 and q170), with "wanted for pointers" flat as the area loaded;
    - in a pool a scanned pointer reached, every 200 ms the chunks the CPU has written: a shader may index far into a pool from one pointer (an array of light probes, say), past the two chunks placed for it;
    - every 200 ms at a submission's start, those a binding or a pointer wanted but a pending write held back.
    - **Not because the CPU wrote it.** Placing every chunk the game had written (a page present or swapped out, from `/proc/self/pagemap`) placed 1,172 MiB in Kokiri Forest. A test-only count on a Galaxy S25 Edge (each 64 KiB marked when a texture is made over it or a draw, dispatch or copy reads it as a buffer) found 306 MiB read as buffers, 64 MiB only as textures and 801 MiB neither.
      - Placing on bindings alone (685 MiB) drew Link and the Kokiri black again with the scene washed out, and no raw store went astray (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SINK=1`): their data is reached through pointers.
      - The pointer scan found it in 4 chunks: 693 MiB placed, the picture as with every written chunk placed.
      - `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_WRITTEN=1` places the chunks the CPU wrote too, as before.
    - **Small pools too:** the pools under 4 MiB were placed whole at their first buffer address at first, about 500 MiB in Kokiri Forest, 356 MiB of it memory no binding reads. Chunked like the rest, 123 MiB is placed in all against 693 on a Galaxy S25 Edge, the picture as before and no raw store lost, for about 1-3 ms more of the replay thread a frame. A lookup by address is a binary search over the chunked pools, kept sorted (4,041 in Kokiri Forest, none overlapping), and the submission tick visits only the pools with a chunk waiting. `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_LAZY_MIN=<bytes>` places pools under that size whole.
    - A run of chunks that holds a piece of another pool placed already (a smaller pool over the same storage, placed whole at its own buffer address) is refused, with a red line: placing over it would map the range twice. `gm_place` only checks a new pool against placed pieces, not against chunked pools not placed yet.
    - A raw load of an address missing from the table reads 0, so a chunk only the CPU wrote, which no binding or scanned pointer reaches, reads as zeros. Raw stores into a chunk not placed go nowhere, and a buffer across chunks placed at different times is copied as before.
    - A chunk is placed while the game may be writing it, so the swap freezes it: read-only from the copy to the placed map, with a fault hook and a host pre-write hook (`dm_fault_hook_register`, `dm_guest_write_prep_register`) that make a write wait until the map is in place.
    - The game fills its dynamic pools a little further each frame, so chunks next to each other were placed at different times, as separate allocations, and a storage buffer across two of them was copied again (Kokiri Forest: 26,420 such bindings in a run, 2.1 fps). Such a pool is made one allocation at the next submission's start: the GPU waited for, the pool frozen (reads too, since a piece's range is unmapped for a moment while it is given back), its pieces given back, then placed again in one swap.
    - `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SINK=1` counts the raw stores a chunk not placed would lose: every such chunk gets a table entry onto one zeroed 2 MiB sink, so a store there sets its bit in the write log.
    - This is nx2android's lazy pool backing and freeze window, written anew. Its chunks are 400 MB, its freeze is part of `nvn_dirty`, and it backs every addressed pool once a shader uses raw memory; here a chunk is placed only when bound or pointed to. `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_LAZY=0` places whole pools.
  - **Shader-code pools** (`NVN_MEMORY_POOL_FLAGS_SHADER_CODE`) are left out: the replay runs translated shaders and reads a program's own constants on the CPU, and no raw pointer reaches shader code. Ocarina of Time has 205 of them, 345 MiB that placing would commit and pin; BananaNative leaves them out by default (after Anka Native). `nvnMemoryPoolInitialize` gives the replay a pool's flags through a weak hook (`mtl_pool_flags`, a no-op on the Mac). `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_CODE=1` places them.
  - **The swap:** made with the replay held, so nothing copies from the pool meanwhile, and inside `nvn_dirty_open` / `nvn_dirty_close`: the dirty tracker's protected pages are opened and hashed, then protected again on the placed map. Nothing is logged inside it, since a thread holding the log's lock may wait for the tracker (Banana Native: Pokémon Pokopia stopped there). A pool made again where one had a buffer address is placed at its Initialize, since `pool_mark_addressed` doesn't ask again for the same storage. The GPU is waited for only when a write still to land overlaps the pool (a write-back, a queued copy, a counter report or an event word, which the completion thread writes): the GPU reaches a pool only once it is placed, and the chunks in flight copied what they read of it when they were recorded. Ocarina of Time places pools all through Kokiri Forest (about 2,000 pieces a minute), and waiting for the GPU at each emptied its queue every time.
  - **Pieces:** at most `maxMemoryAllocationSize` each (1 GiB on the Adreno 840), each a table entry of its own. Every page is faulted in right after the placed map (`MADV_POPULATE_READ`): the Adreno's kernel driver fills a page at its first touch from the touch's distance to the start of its mapping, which is the wrong page once the dirty tracker's `mprotect` has split the mapping (found by Banana Native in Donkey Kong Bananza's textures).
  - **Given back** at the pool's Finalize: the GPU is waited for, the bytes go to new anonymous pages (zero pages skipped), and those pages are moved over the range. A placed allocation is freed before the move, because the Adreno's `vkFreeMemory` takes the placed range down even after `VK_MEMORY_UNMAP_RESERVE_BIT_EXT`; an `AHardwareBuffer`'s mapping is the replay's own, so it is released after.
  - **The table** is laid out as `gmem_map` does it (`gmem_table.h`), from the placed pieces, and built again when one comes or goes. The one it replaces is filled again once the chunk recorded then has completed (8 kept): a new buffer for each of Kokiri Forest's 500 or so builds a minute was 8.6% of the replay thread.
  - **Storage buffers in place** (`gfx_vk_gmem_inplace.inc`): a storage buffer in a placed piece is bound as the piece's own buffer at its offset, rather than copied into the arena.
    - A read-only one was copied once per chunk and compared whole with that copy at every later draw (`ucopy`), unless the geometry cache kept it (`sb_keep`: up to 8 MB, and not written by the game in two frames running). In Kokiri Forest that compare was 83% of the replay thread on the Galaxy Z Fold 8 (simpleperf: `__memcmp_aarch64` under `ucopy`, `stage_set`'s read-only storage buffer line).
    - A written one was copied in with a snapshot and written back by comparing the two when its chunk completed: about 101 write-backs and 49 MB a frame there, copied twice by the replay and compared by the completion thread (13.9 ms a frame). In place it gets a write-back entry that copies nothing: CPU reads of its range wait for its chunk as before (`chunk_hits`), a later dispatch reads it from the piece (`fwd_hit`), and when the chunk completes its pages are prepared (marked written, so a kept copy or a texture over them is made again). The chunk ends with a barrier to the host.
    - The GPU reads such a buffer when it runs, not when the replay records. The game's own writes are ordered by its fences, which complete when the GPU has run the work before them, as on the console. The replay's are not: it writes guest memory while it records (uniform buffer updates, small copies and fills, `mem_view`). So each page read in place keeps the chunk that last read it, and such a write waits for that chunk first. Write-backs and large copies land when their chunk completes, after the work recorded before them; a draw recorded after them lands them first when it reads their range (`draw_view`), as before.
    - Not in place: a buffer in no placed piece, one crossing two pieces, one at an offset that isn't a multiple of `minStorageBufferOffsetAlignment` (64 on the Adreno 840; about 0.2% of Kokiri Forest's bindings). Pieces take every buffer usage, since a forwarded write can be read as a vertex stream or a buffer texture. `DM_VK_INPLACE=0` and `DM_VK_INPLACE_WRITE=0` copy them as before.
  - **Stores** land in guest memory when the GPU runs them, but the replay reads guest memory when it records (arena copies, uploads, CPU-side copies). So a chunk that ran raw stores counts as a write-back of unknown range until it completes (`chunk_hits`): the next CPU read of guest memory waits for it, as the Mac counts them as a write anywhere until it has learned their pools. nx2android counts them the same way. Each store also sets its table entry's bit in a write log: each draw with stores gets a zeroed 1 KiB log from the arena, read when its chunk completes, and the pools it names are logged. The chunk ends with a barrier to the host.
  - **Switches:** `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_MAX` caps the bytes placed; `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_STORE_WAIT=0` lets CPU reads go on past raw stores; `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SYNC=1` lands every pending write-back before each draw that binds the table; `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_CHECK=1` logs where the pointers in those draws' storage buffers lie; `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST=1` runs the self-test below.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_gmem_table.py` builds `gmem_table.h` and checks the translator's lookup over it: gaps, adjacent ranges, hinted chunks, two chunks sharing a hint, addresses above 4 GiB and 2^40, the cached entry, and a full table. A hint naming the wrong entry, or unused entries starting at 0, fails it.
  - **Frostpunk** on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, driver 2150760449, the `AHardwareBuffer` route), the note's route into the city with longer waits, once off and twice on: the Generator and the wrecks draw with the switch on, and the crater is empty with it off (only the Generator's shadow). `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST`: 64 of 64. 30 fps either way: frame p50 33.7 off, 33.6 and 33.7 ms on; GPU p50 23.0 off, 24.9 and 24.4 ms on; PSS 2.74 GB off, 2.88 and 2.87 GB on. About 408,000 bindings of the table a run; the mapped dma-buf read at 18 GB/s.
  - **Ocarina of Time** on a Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840, driver 0x8034a013, Android 17, placed maps), Kokiri Forest:
  - `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST`: 64 of 64 16-byte reads through the table on the GPU match the CPU's bytes, words aligned to 4 bytes included;
  - 3,566 to 3,794 pieces, 1.32-1.38 GiB placed, 0.45-0.55 s of placing in all; placed pages read at 26-31 GB/s on the CPU;
  - the write logs name placed pools, so the stores' lookups find their entries on the GPU;
  - `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_CHECK`: the fog compute's pointers lie in a placed 149 MiB pool holding plausible records (counts, boxes, colours);
  - placing alone, before storage buffers were bound in place (`bench_android.py --route kokiri`, two runs each): off 1.7 / 1.6 fps, frame p50 625 / 624 ms, GPU p50 130 / 132 ms, PSS 8.7 / 8.8 GB; on 1.6 / 1.4 fps, 656 / 696 ms, 133 / 139 ms, 10.8 / 10.8 GB;
  - with storage buffers in place, against main `898ac92f` (route `kokiri`, window 95-140 s, launches alternated, two sessions): main 2.8 / 2.4 / 2.3 / 2.3 fps, frame p50 385-446 ms, submit CPU p50 283-338 ms, GPU p50 147-164 ms, PSS 8.6-9.5 GB; this 9.8 / 10.6 fps, frame p50 107 / 104 ms (p95 115 / 113), submit CPU p50 37 / 35 ms, GPU p50 88 / 81 ms, PSS 11.2 GB. A third run ended at 138 s when Android killed the game for memory (below). Simpleperf afterwards: the replay thread's `memcmp` is gone, `vkUpdateDescriptorSets` is about 10% of it, and the completion thread is mostly the dirty tracker's `prep_write` (below);
  - **Chunks and code pools left out** (the defaults), Kokiri Forest on the Fold, 10 runs in a row: no low-memory kill. PSS 4.34-4.46 GB with 0-0.19 GB in swap, GL mtrack 0.77-0.85 GB, 1,172 MiB placed (674 of the chunked pools' 1,122 MiB), 9.7-11.4 fps, submit CPU p50 32-37 ms, GPU p50 70-88 ms.
    - The same session: whole pools without code pools placed 1,621 MiB at 11.7 fps (GL mtrack 4.06 GB with 4.6 GB in swap, 1.46 GB with none); with code pools, as before, 1,967 MiB.
    - 11 pools were merged in a run for a binding across two pieces (176 MiB, 199 ms in all), and 3,074 bindings still crossed pieces (at a pool's edge or before their merge). With `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SINK=1`, three runs counted no raw store into a chunk not placed.
    - The device's state differs from run to run: these ten ran with almost nothing in swap, while the two kills below came with 4.5 GB of the game in swap, so they are not the same conditions.
  - 3,700-4,300 pieces, 1.6-2.0 GiB placed, the GPU waited for at none of the swaps, 236 table builds served by 3 buffers; about 1.3 million read-only and 47,000 written bindings in place a run, 0.1% not in place (an offset not a multiple of 64), and the replay's own writes met pages read in place by a chunk still to run 0 times;
  - Breath of the Wild (Switch 1), when an earlier default made every device take the extensions with raw memory off for the game: route `outdoors` 24.9 fps, frame p50 40.6 ms, GPU p50 29.3 ms, against 24.5 fps, 41.2 ms and 29.3 ms without them, the same two red lines. Off now makes the device as before.
  - **Frostpunk** on the Galaxy S25 Edge again, this branch at `bd5bb301` (storage buffers in place) merged with main `5736949c`, `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM=1` from its `android_defaults.txt` (#200), three scripted runs into the city of 5-7 minutes (by the Frostpunk session):
    - on: the Generator and the wreckage drawn as on the Mac; frame p50 33.6-33.9 / p95 34.3-34.6 ms, GPU p50 24.2-25.3 ms, submit+present 18.6-20.4 ms, PSS 2.86-2.88 GB, flat over 4 minutes in the city;
    - off: invisible; the same frame times, GPU 22.7-22.9 ms, submit 19.6-20.3 ms, PSS 2.69 GB;
    - 345-370 MiB placed (peak 372) in 612-699 pieces, 675 given back a run, none over the 2.71 GiB cap and none failed; the red line for slot 255 gone and nothing new in red.
- **Open:**
  - **Ocarina of Time's forest stays black.** With the table working, the fog compute `9e6b00b050e30801` still writes NaN into every texel it writes of its 64x36x96 volume, and `9ed48d1011f1b48b` carries that into the 128x72x96 pair (13.3% and 8.6% of their texels: the region written). The partner volumes it writes beside them hold finite values. Neither timing switch changes it (above). So another input of that shader differs from the Mac's; see the game's note.
  - **Timing:** the GPU reads guest memory when it runs. For raw loads, a write-back still pending can give a draw older bytes (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SYNC=1` lands them first), and a uniform update, copy or fill the replay records later can give it newer ones (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_WSYNC=1` makes such a write wait for chunks that bound the table); in-place storage buffers are covered by their page stamps. Neither is the cause of Ocarina of Time's black forest: in a test build with the mesh draws (#83) and the upscaler replacement (#173), Kokiri Forest stays black with `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SYNC=1` (6,425 draws landed write-backs first), with `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_WSYNC=1` (2,142 of the replay's writes waited for a chunk) and with both. For raw stores, the write anywhere costs a wait at the next CPU read after each chunk with stores; learning their pools from the write log, as the Mac does, would narrow it.
  - **The dirty tracker's walk:** `nvn_dirty.c` `prep_write` (every host write into guest memory) and `on_fault` (every guest write fault) walk all its range slots, thousands in Ocarina of Time. With the copies gone it was 98% of the completion thread in Kokiri Forest; written ranges are now prepared once per chunk, and `prep_write` remembers a page's ranges ([A host write into guest memory looked at every pool range](nvn-objects.md#a-host-write-into-guest-memory-looked-at-every-pool-range)).
  - **Raw stores and the CPU's caches:** the dirty tracker doesn't see GPU writes, so a texture or the geometry kept on the GPU over pages the stores wrote isn't uploaded again.
  - **The swap's window:** a guest thread writing a pool between the copy and the placed map loses its write. A pool's first buffer is normally made by the thread that made the pool, before anything else uses it.
  - **Memory:** a placed pool is committed whole, the bytes the game never touched too. With whole pools, Ocarina of Time's PSS in Kokiri Forest went from 8.6-9.5 GB to about 11.2 GB, on a Fold with 10.8 GB: 2 of 11 runs ended at 130-138 s, the low-memory killer reclaiming 4.5 GB of swap and then killing the game. Of the 1,967 MiB placed then, 571 MiB had been resident at their first buffer address, but the game writes further into its pools as it plays: by the end of the scene it had touched 800 of the chunked pools' 1,326 MiB. Chunks and leaving code pools out cut what is placed to 1,172 MiB, and placing only the chunks bindings and pointers reach to 693 MiB (above; the measurements below were made at 1,172).
    - **Under app pressure on the Fold:** ten heavy apps opened before each run, with Android reclaiming most of them during it. 10 of 10 chunked runs went through with no kill: PSS 4.28-4.38 GB, 2.86-4.30 GB of it not in swap, GL mtrack 0.78-0.85 GB, 9.5-10.4 fps. Three whole-pool runs survived too (PSS 4.80-6.13 GB, GL mtrack 1.62-1.90 GB): this pressure didn't reproduce the two earlier kills.
    - **On a OnePlus Pad 3 (12 GB, the AHardwareBuffer route)** lmkd killed the game in 7 of 11 Kokiri launches with it on, chunked or whole, with or without other apps open; 4 of 4 ran with it off. The kills are OnePlus's own lmkd reason 13. The pools add about 1.1 GB of EGL mtrack that can't go to swap. The Fold has the same memory and holds more of the game resident, so a memory-size gate would not separate the two; the route and OnePlus's reason do (by Runner A, q109). The default cap (a quarter of the device's memory, 2.7 GB there) is above all of it. The chunks' arena is 128-144 MiB, so trimming it is no lever (measured, and taken out again).
  - **Other GPUs:** placed maps were tried on the Adreno 840 and `AHardwareBuffer`s on the Adreno 830 (Frostpunk before storage buffers were bound in place; a OnePlus Pad 3 run of Ocarina of Time by the OoT session went from 1.5 to 4.0 fps with it, frame p50 390 → 247 ms); Mali, Xclipse and Turnip were not tried. A GPU with `VK_EXT_external_memory_host` (the Pixel 9 Pro XL's Mali-G715, the Galaxy Z Flip 7's Xclipse 950) could import the pool's pages where they are, with no swap and no commit; that isn't done. A GPU with neither route keeps reading 0, with a red line.
  - **Task and mesh draws** (#83) bind the table through their own path.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#android) (Switch 2), from a frame dump: the fog compute `9e6b00b050e30801` carries the translator's "traced to no storage buffer: it goes through the memory pool table" notes and wrote NaN only, and the Mac's fog volumes under the same serials have none; Frostpunk (an NVN2 game on a Switch 1 sdk, its note in #140).

### Raw global memory on a driver that refuses OpConvertUToPtr (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, a game with raw global memory on (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM=1`) on an older Qualcomm driver: every pipeline of a shader that binds the raw pool table fails with `VK_ERROR_UNKNOWN` (-13), and the driver logs `INTERNAL ERROR: Instruction OpConvertUToPtr not supported` (tag `AdrenoVK`, which the bench's logcat filter hides). Ocarina of Time's characters and water go dark on an Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740, driver V@0676.32), 77 failures in a Kokiri Forest load; Frostpunk's buildings are invisible on a Motorola Razr (Adreno 730, V@0615).
- **Cause:** the translator's raw helper makes a physical pointer: it adds the table entry's delta to the address in two 32-bit halves, builds a `v2uint`, bitcasts it to `ulong` and converts that with `OpConvertUToPtr` ([Raw global memory on the Vulkan replay](#raw-global-memory-on-the-vulkan-replay-android)). These drivers offer `bufferDeviceAddress` but reject the conversion, and the 740 has no `shaderInt64`. Shrinking the failing modules with `spirv-opt` changed nothing; stubbing the helpers' pointers made them all build.
- **Fix:** the pieces go through a descriptor array instead (`gfx_vk_gmem_noptr.inc`, `spv_noptr.h`):
  - **The array:** each placed piece's buffer is a slot of one storage-buffer array in a set of its own, and each table entry's delta is `(slot << 32) - start`. The helper's own sum then comes out as the slot in the high word and the offset in the low word, and its code stays as translated.
  - **The modules:** `spv_noptr` rewrites each module that declares `PhysicalStorageBufferAddresses` as it is made (`lm_code`). Each `OpConvertUToPtr` of a bitcast `v2uint`, and every load, store or atomic through its result, becomes an access to `pools[high word].d[low word >> 2]`: a vector as its components, each its own access, bitcast where they aren't `uint`, decorated `NonUniform`. The pointer types, the capability, the extension and the memory model's addressing go, and `Int64` too when nothing else uses the `ulong` type. A module whose pointers are made or used any other way is left as it is, with a red line once per reason.
  - **One set for every table:** a slot is its piece's while it is placed, and pieces are given back only with the GPU idle. A new piece's slot is written as it is placed, into a set that is update-after-bind with its slots partially bound and updated unused while pending, so the write never waits for draws already recorded. Slots are handed out round the array, so a freed one is the last to come back.
  - **Pieces** are capped at `maxStorageBufferRange` (128 MiB on the 740), so each is one descriptor and an offset fits the low word.
  - **Which set:** set 5, past the stages' sets (0 to 3) and the texture pool's (4), where the GPU binds 6 sets at once (the 740 binds 7). On a GPU that binds only 4 or 5 it is set 3, which no stage of Ocarina of Time's or Frostpunk's raw-memory shaders has (vertex, compute and mesh 0, fragment 1, task 2); a pipeline with such a stage is refused with a red line. A task / mesh draw (`gfx_vk_mesh.inc`) gets the array in its layout and bound as the other draws do, and its per-primitive fragment variant goes through `spv_noptr` too. In compute form (`gfx_vk_meshemu.inc`, which is what a driver that refuses the pointers runs, having no `VK_EXT_mesh_shader`) the kernels' pipelines and the final draw take the array as well. Neither is run on a device: no game's profile has both the array and task / mesh shaders, since Ocarina of Time keeps the array off. The array is as long as the table (8,192 slots), or as the GPU's update-after-bind storage buffers a stage allow.
  - **Which drivers:** at the device's making, on a device with runtime descriptor arrays, non-uniform storage-buffer indexing, update-after-bind storage buffers and partially bound slots, a probe pipeline (`shaders/gmem_probe.comp`, a pointer made by `OpConvertUToPtr` as the translator makes it) is built; the array is used only if the driver refuses it. The Adreno 830 and 840 build it and keep physical pointers. `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_NOPTR=1` uses the array without the probe; `=0` never does. Ocarina of Time's profile sets `=0` (`android_defaults.txt`): with the array it no longer fits a 12 GB device (the Odin 2's check below).
  - **Self-test:** with the array, `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST=1` reads through it (`shaders/gmem_test_noptr.comp`).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_noptr.py` builds `spv_noptr.h` and runs it on modules in the helper's form: uint, `uvec4` and `vec4` loads, a `vec4` store and an atomic, at SPIR-V 1.3 and 1.5 (the array in the entry point's interface); a `ulong` used elsewhere keeps `Int64`; the array goes to the set asked for; a pointer copied, a pointer bitcast from a vector, and a 64-bit pointee are left as they are. Every result passes `spirv-val`. With `DM_SPV_DIR` set to Ocarina of Time's chain-6 shaders, all 3,147 modules that declare physical pointers are rewritten and pass `spirv-val` at their own SPIR-V version, none left.
  - **Frostpunk on a Moto Razr Plus 2023** (Adreno 730, V@0615.98, Vulkan 1.1.128, 7 descriptor sets bound at once, 524,288 update-after-bind storage buffers a stage), the phone's app with only Frostpunk's host replaced, the route into the city, 2026-11-11:
    - the probe is refused (`VkResult -13`, the driver's `OpConvertUToPtr not supported`), the array is at set 5 with 8,192 slots, and 96 modules are rewritten, none left;
    - no pipeline fails (31 with `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_NOPTR=0`, as before), and the Generator and the wreckage draw (a crater without);
    - `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST=1`: 64 of 64 reads through the array match the CPU's bytes;
    - 29.4-29.5 fps either way; GPU p50 27.1-28.3 ms in four launches against 17.1 ms without the meshes. The two don't compare as they stand: the GPU clocks down when the frame is light (the frame's terrain pass takes 1.44 ms with the meshes drawn and 3.93 ms without). What the meshes cost is in the note below;
    - `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_NOPTR_SET=3`: the same picture and times with the array at set 3.
  - **The array against pointers on a driver that takes both,** a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950, driver 24.0.533), whose probe passes: the phone's app with Frostpunk's and Ocarina of Time's hosts replaced, the default (pointers) against `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_NOPTR=1`, 2026-11-11:

    | | Pointers (the default there) | The array (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_NOPTR=1`) |
    |---|---|---|
    | Frostpunk, the city | 29.5 fps, frame p50 33.95 ms, GPU p50 12.41 ms, PSS 2.50 GB | 29.5 fps, 33.91 ms, GPU 12.57 ms, PSS 2.51 GB |
    | Ocarina of Time, Kokiri Forest (`kokiri`), warm caches | 13.0 and 13.0 fps, frame p50 77.8 and 78.5 ms, GPU p50 50.6 and 50.5 ms | 12.8 fps, frame p50 79.7 ms, GPU p50 52.1 ms |
    | Ocarina of Time: placed, PSS | 95-102 pieces, 390-408 MiB; 8.27-8.31 GB | 99-102 pieces (as many slots), 401-407 MiB; 8.33-8.37 GB |

    - The pictures match: Frostpunk's Generator and wreckage; Link, the ferns and the water lit in Kokiri Forest. `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST=1` reads 64 of 64 either way (26 of them not all zero in Ocarina of Time).
    - Ocarina of Time's raw stores go through the array too (298 of its 311 write logs held stores in the array run), and 6 pieces were given back and their slots freed in each run.
    - The first launch with the array compiled the rewritten shaders' pipelines anew (60 of them, with stalls of up to 16 s on this driver); the runs above are the ones after.
  - **Ocarina of Time on an Ayn Odin 2** (Adreno 740, V@0676.32, Vulkan 1.3.128, 11.5 GB of RAM and 4 GB of swap), the device's app with only its host replaced, the `kokiri` route, 2026-11-11. The shaders build, and the game no longer fits:
    - the probe is refused, and with the array no graphics pipeline fails (23 with `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_NOPTR=0`, with 88 of the driver's lines); 4 compute pipelines fail where 14 did (the note below); `DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_TEST=1` reads 64 of 64 through the array;
    - the low-memory killer ends the game about 20 s into the Kokiri Forest load, in each of 12 launches ("device is not responding"). With the array off it runs, at 13.2-13.6 fps, with MemAvailable at 0.3-0.5 GB and the swap full;
    - the pipelines that now build are 0.63 GB of the driver's memory: at the title `DM_VK_MEMREPORT=1` counts 2,871 MB in 1,623 pipeline memory objects with the array and 2,241 MB in 1,525 without. No `NonUniform` decorations, 256 slots and file-backed pools each changed nothing;
    - no picture of Kokiri Forest through the array was seen on this driver;
    - so the game's profile keeps the array off, and it runs there as it did: dark characters, about 13 fps.
- **Note:** what Qualcomm's two drivers do with the array:
  - **Each fetch through it costs on V@0615.98** (the Razr). Frostpunk's mesh vertex shaders load a word and 15 vectors a vertex: 61 fetches, a vector being four. Per pass (`prof_now`), in units of the same frame's terrain pass and with the meshes' triangles culled, so vertex work only: 4.4 as built; 0.5 with the lookups kept and no fetch; 2.3 with each vector in one fetch; 4.3 with a sized array and 4.8 with plain-memory copies in the slots. With its fragments the mesh pass is 12.8 of a frame's 19.6. On Xclipse the array costs what pointers do (above).
  - **An atomic on an element of a storage-buffer array doesn't link on V@0676.32** (the Odin: `Failed to link shaders`, then -13): at a dynamic or a constant slot, in a runtime or a 16-long sized array, with or without `NonUniform`. Loads and stores link, and so does the same atomic on a plain storage buffer. 16 of Ocarina of Time's compute shaders make atomics through pointers. The four a Kokiri load builds (`a039399e6336de1b`, `9bdbca01d3ee6022`, `5a2e37058f21c8a5`, `3a26262eee1427c9`: an `OpAtomicIAdd` and an `OpAtomicUMax` each) fail there with the array, as they did with pointers.
  - **Pipelines are driver memory on V@0676.32:** Ocarina of Time's are 2.2 GB at the title with the array off (every pipeline the prewarm list names), in objects of up to about 8 MB. `DM_VK_PREWARM=0` leaves 0.4-0.5 GB at the title, but the Kokiri load then makes about 960 pipelines in a few seconds and the killer ends the game, with the array or without.

### Raw global memory helpers read their last entry from the table on every access (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, a game with raw global memory on: compute dispatches of one to a dozen workgroups that take hundreds of microseconds each. Ocarina of Time's culling and scene compute shaders in Kokiri Forest: 25 dispatches of 20 shaders that bind the raw pool table are 42-48 % of the frame's 148 game dispatches (3.6-3.9 ms of 8.4-8.6 on a Galaxy S25 Edge / Z Fold 8 at their 600-660 MHz, 2.2 of 5.2 ms on a OnePlus Pad 3 at 1,100 MHz), and 12 of them run in 12 workgroups or fewer.
- **Cause:** each raw access is a call of the translator's helper ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](shaders.md#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)). The helper keeps the index of the table entry it chose last in a private word ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](shaders.md#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)), but reads that entry's start and end from the table to see whether the address is still in it (4 storage loads), then its delta to make the pointer (2 more): six dependent loads before the access itself, on a hit as on a miss. The 20 shaders make 757 such calls a dispatch (743 loads, 5 stores, 9 atomics); a small dispatch of pointer-chasing threads can't hide that latency. On the Mac the table reads cost nothing (they stay in registers); on the Adreno 830 and 840 they don't.
- **Fix:** `dm/android/spv_gmcache.h`, at module creation (`gfx_vk_lmod.inc` `lm_code`, `gfx_vk_draw.inc` `spv_fit_gmcache`), before `spv_noptr` and the other rewrites: each helper gets six private words beside the index, the last entry's start, end and delta (zero at the start, as the index is: entry 0 is the empty one). The entry block's four table reads and the chosen block's two delta reads become reads of those words, and the block that records a new index (after the hint or the search, which re-reads the entry's end anyway) fills the six from the table. The words always mirror the entry the index names, so the results are the same; a hit reads no table word. Nothing on disk changes (no `ANDROID_CHAIN` bump, no re-translation); the host's table is the same. A module whose helpers aren't in that form (another count of table reads in the entry block, no store of the index) is made as translated, said once. `DM_SPV_GMCACHE=0` (env.txt) makes every module as translated.
- **Numbers:** Kokiri Forest's `kokiri` route, one profiled frame (`prof_now`), the game's 148 dispatches, fresh shaders, #411's kernels, the phones' own OoT defaults; the 20 raw-table shaders' GPU time as a ratio to the frame's unswapped game dispatches (the Samsung phones' clock drifts run to run), and as it is on the Pad 3, whose clock holds 1,100 MHz. The rewrite's form was first tried as a file swap of the 20 shaders (`gmvar.py cache`), then as the host's rewrite of every module: OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830): 2.22 → 1.94 ms (−13 %; the game's dispatches 5.16 → 4.87), the one-workgroup pointer chasers most (`9e6b00b050e30801` 341 → 227 us, `3d0d6d61bb9076e7` 52 → 36, `aba48e1a7f54e527` 33 → 18); Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840): ratio 3.92 → 3.31 (−16 %; 5.45 → 4.64 ms at 578 → 461 MHz), and with the helpers inlined as well 3.07 (−22 %, a chain change not made); Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830): 3.05 → 2.67 (−12 %; 5.18 → 4.03 ms at 525 MHz both). The 11 heaviest shaders without raw memory are unchanged (ratios 2.74 → 2.73, 3.24 → 3.23, 3.20 → 3.08). With the host's rewrite of every module on the Pad 3 (`DM_SPV_GMCACHE=0` against on, adjacent runs at 1,100 MHz): the game's dispatches 5.17 → 4.89 ms (the raw-table shaders 2.25 → 1.96), the task / mesh kernels in compute form 3.2 → 2.9 ms (they read raw memory too), the 1440x810 passes 11.5 → 11.0 ms (their raw vertex shaders), the GPU frame p50 24.56 → 23.35 ms, and again 24.59 → 23.59 in a second pair (−4-5 %; the game's dispatches 5.13 → 4.90, the kernels 3.1 → 2.8); the picture the same, 30 fps either way (the Pad 3 is at the cap).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_gmcache.py` builds the header for the PC and runs it on a helper assembled in the translator's form (DebugMode locals and SSA, SPIR-V 1.3 and 1.4), checking the entry block reads no table word and each result with `spirv-val`; with `DM_SPV_DIR=<a game's packed spv folder>` it runs over every module with physical pointers (Ocarina of Time's 3,256 and Frostpunk's 1,647: all rewritten, all valid).
- **Not it:** the DebugMode form itself. `spirv-opt`'s mem2reg without inlining (`--eliminate-local-*`, `--ssa-rewrite`) over the 31 heaviest compute shaders changed nothing on any of the three phones (the drivers promote the locals themselves); with the helpers inlined too (`-O`) the Fold's 840 driver ran them 10-14 % faster and the two 830s 0-5 %. Stubbing the helpers (loads read 0) leaves 7 % of the raw-table shaders' time, but the shaders then do less work (their loops end on the zeros), so that is not the lookups' share.

### A texture without NVN's image flag bound to an image slot (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, Ocarina of Time's title: `[RED] [vk] image format 43 is not storage-capable here: skipped` (a 2560x1440 RGBA8_SRGB texture), and with #149 `[RED] [vk] image of format 37 bound to an image slot (format 37) without storage use` (a 1920x1080 RGBA8 one).
- **Cause:** the game builds these textures without NVN's image flag, registers them with `RegisterTexture`, and binds them to image slots all the same. An image slot reads a texture-pool entry as a texture does.
  - `vt_create` gives `STORAGE` only to a texture with the image flag. So the RGBA8 one got a storage view of an image without `STORAGE`, which is invalid, and the sRGB one's dispatch was skipped: sRGB is no storage format on Adreno.
  - The flag was also part of the image's sharing key. A texture drawn into and then registered with `RegisterImage` (which adds the flag, `mtl_reg_image`) got a second, empty image beside the first.
  - The Mac upgrades such a texture when it's bound to an image slot (`nvn_mtl.m` `image_prepare`, `tex_upgrade`), and its sharing key leaves the flag out.
- **Fix:** the same on Vulkan.
  - The sharing key and the description check leave the image flag out (`share_of`, `desc_same`).
  - An image slot's texture without `STORAGE` is made again with it and `MUTABLE_FORMAT`, its levels and layers copied over, and swapped in under the same texture (`vt_storage_up`). Every name of it keeps it; the old image, its views and framebuffers go when the chunk completes.
  - **sRGB, behind `DM_VK_STORAGE_UP=2`:** a format the GPU can't store to takes `STORAGE` for its UNORM twin's views alone (`EXTENDED_USAGE`), and each view takes the usage its format has (`view_get`). `storage_view` stores sRGB through that twin: image stores write texels as the shader gives them, as Maxwell's do and the Mac's linear-twin views (`img_fit`).
    - Not the default: with it, Ocarina of Time's title runs the compute that writes the 2560x1440 sRGB target, its upscaler's. On the Pad 3 that drew noise over the whole picture and took the GPU from 24 to 108 ms a frame. DeathKhan's nx2android fork met the same flood and replaced the upscaler (`8569eeb`); Banana Native measured the upscaler at 58.5 ms a frame on the Fold.
    - By default (`1`) such a texture's draw or dispatch is still skipped, as before, now with red line 125. `0` binds every such texture as it is, as before.
  - A decoded BC texture, a multisample or depth one, or one the GPU takes no storage image of is still skipped, with red line 123. So is one bound to an image slot of a draw that renders to it (124).
- **Check:** OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830), against main `61eb7c31`, launches alternated:
  - Ocarina of Time's title (route `title`): the RGBA8 target is upgraded and red line 104 is gone. The sRGB target is skipped with red line 125, the picture is main's, and the speed too (20.1 fps, GPU p50 24.3 ms on both). With `DM_VK_STORAGE_UP=2` the sRGB one is upgraded too, and its compute draws noise over the picture at 8.4 fps, GPU 108 ms (twice the same).
  - Super Mario Odyssey (`opening`) and Brilliant Diamond (`eterna`), two runs each: no upgrade and no red line. 60 fps throughout, frame p50 within 0.05 ms. GPU p50 7.94–7.97 against 8.04–8.10 ms (SMO) and 10.07–10.11 against 10.14–10.36 ms (BD); the branch always ran second in each pair.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#android) (Switch 2), by the nx2android picks run on a Galaxy S25 Edge.


### An E5B9G9R9 texture bound to a compute shader's image slot (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest: black under the HUD with OoT's own upscaler replaced (#173), or white streak noise with it running. On Samsung's Xclipse, which does store E5B9G9R9: the 3D view solid red under the HUD with the upscaler replaced, red noise with the network running. The forest itself is drawn: the 1440x810 scene target is finite through every pass. No red line names it: red line 125 ("a format the GPU cannot store to: skipped") was said once for the whole run, and the title's sRGB target took it first.
- **Cause:** `a335c1db6270c355` writes the upscaler's input, a 1440x810 E5B9G9R9 texture, through an image slot, and the upscaler (`fa3033d05c0a41d6`) samples it. No Adreno here stores to a shared-exponent format, so `img_up` refused the texture and the dispatch was skipped. The input was never written: the network turned the empty texture into noise (16% of its 1920x1080 output non-finite), and the replacement squared it to 0. The Mac writes RGB9E5 natively.
- **Fix:** the texture keeps its format and its bits; the shader packs them.
  - `vt_storage_up` makes such a texture again with `STORAGE` for R32_UINT views alone (`MUTABLE_FORMAT`, `EXTENDED_USAGE`), as it does for an sRGB texture's UNORM twin, and `storage_view` binds it through an R32_UINT view.
  - `storage_view` takes the R32_UINT view on every GPU, also one that stores to E5B9G9R9 itself: the shader's stores are packed words, and `stage_set` takes the packing pipeline whatever the GPU. On Samsung's Xclipse the view used to stay E5B9G9R9, so each packed word was stored again as the red channel's float with green and blue 0, and the upscaler made the picture solid red.
  - `stage_set` notes the compute shader's image bindings that hold one, and `vk_dispatch` takes that shader's variant pipeline for them (`cpipe_e5`; two kept per shader, by bindings: a third set is skipped, red line 127). Its module goes through `spv_e5.h` (`lm_code`): the image becomes an R32ui image. A raw store (Maxwell's SUST.B, which the translator writes as vec4(the word's bits, 0, 0, 0)) stores the word as it is: `a335c1db6270c355`'s stores are these, the game packing RGB9E5 itself. A formatted store packs its texel into RGB9E5, and a load unpacks it, with alpha 1.
  - The packing is Vulkan's conversion (Shared Exponent Formats): channels clamped to [0, 65408], NaN to 0; the shared exponent from the largest channel, one higher where that channel would round to 512; each mantissa rounded to nearest. So later reads, copies and the CPU see the bits a GPU with the format would write.
  - A draw's image slot with such a texture is still skipped (red line 126), and so is a module whose image has another use (a format query, a texel pointer for an atomic). `DM_VK_E5_STORE=0` skips them all, as before.
  - Red line 125 is now said once for each format, so a second format refused behind the first shows up.
- **Check:** Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840; `E5B9G9R9 storage no, R32_UINT storage yes`).
  - `t_e5_store` (`test_spv_e5.py` with `DM_TEST_SERIAL`): a formatted store's 256 words match the specification's conversion bit for bit (0, denormals, NaN, infinities, 65408, the 511 / 512 edges, random values), and a raw store's 256 words come back unchanged, 56 of them with NaN or infinity float bit patterns.
  - Ocarina of Time's `kokiri` route, on a test build with #191 (the c0 + 4 counts): `a335c1db6270c355` now runs ("its storage images at binding mask 0x8 are E5B9G9R9 textures"), and Kokiri Forest is drawn instead of black. With #189 (compute warp ops) the game's own upscaler network shows it cleanly. The picture is still too bright (open, not this entry's).
  - Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950; E5B9G9R9 storage yes): Kokiri Forest drawn and lit, where it was solid red. A frame dump placed the red: the tone-mapped 1440x810 scene right, the 1920x1080 target after the upscaler solid red (255, 0, 0).
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): `a335c1db6270c355`, the upscaler's input. Across its compute modules, 421 of the 424 storage image bindings can be rewritten, all valid under `spirv-val` (`test_spv_e5.py` with `DM_SPV_DIR`).

### The driver compiled with the game's flush-to-zero (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, Qualcomm's driver: Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest is drawn far too bright, yellow and white, with characters as orange silhouettes. The log is clean.
- **Cause:** the replay and its pipeline threads run on threads the game's NVN calls started, or on the game's own, so they carry the game's FPCR: `0x1000000` (flush-to-zero) for Ocarina of Time. Qualcomm's compiler runs on the CPU inside `vkCreate*Pipelines` (Samsung's inside `vkCreateShaderModule`). Under flush-to-zero it folds constants with denormals flushed and makes different code. Swap2-Native measured the scene about 2^12 too bright at the bloom pass on driver 891.11; BananaNative sets FPCR 0 around these calls (`vka_fpenv.h`).
- **Fix:** `vk_driver_instance` wraps the calls that compile: `vkCreateDevice`, `vkCreateShaderModule`, `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`, `vkCreateComputePipelines`, and `vkQueueSubmit`, where a driver may compile what it deferred. Each runs with FPCR 0 and gives the caller's back after, so every caller is covered, the start-up list and the pipeline threads too, and the game's own threads keep their FPCR. The pipeline cache's file is named apart (`pipeline.cache.fp0`), so pipelines compiled under flush-to-zero aren't taken from the old one. `DM_VK_FP_HOST=0` compiles with the caller's FPCR, as before. The log says the inherited value once ("the calling thread's FPCR is 0x1000000").
- **Check:** OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, Qualcomm's driver), Ocarina of Time's `kokiri` route on a test build with #191, #193 and #189. With this change Kokiri Forest is lit as it should be: rocks, moss, lanterns, Navi, a Kokiri, leaves and fog. With `DM_VK_FP_HOST=0`, the same run is the yellow-white blow-out. 3.7 against 3.8 fps, GPU 47 against 46 ms: no cost.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### Ocarina of Time's upscaler network, replaced for speed (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830): the frame's tail is one compute dispatch, the upscaler network, 21 ms of the frame's 45 ms of GPU work, and the game waits for it in `nvnSyncWait` (stream C, q99, raw global memory on). Before #193 and #189 its output was streak noise.
- **Cause:** the game upscales its 1440x810 scene to 1920x1080 with a neural network in one compute dispatch (agl's NDSR: HMMA tiles through shared memory, emulated). It picks one of four networks by its dynamic resolution: `fa3033d05c0a41d6`, `7c511cc23ff85d14`, `b106afba1a18faa9` and `d99fbeeb53d38847`.
  - Its noise was not the network's: its input, `a335c1db6270c355`'s E5B9G9R9 texture, was never written ([An E5B9G9R9 texture bound to a compute shader's image slot](#an-e5b9g9r9-texture-bound-to-a-compute-shaders-image-slot-android), #193), and its warp operations ran on 64-lane subgroups ([Maxwell's 32-lane warps in a wider subgroup](#maxwells-32-lane-warps-in-a-wider-subgroup), #189). With both, the network draws the picture right.
  - What is left is its cost.
- **Fix:** an opt-in swap, the game's default through its `android_defaults.txt` (`DM_VK_UPSCALER_SQUARE=1`): the network is replaced by a bilinear scale and a signed square (`gfx_vk_upsq.inc`, `shaders/upscale_square.comp`), DeathKhan's nx2android idea written anew. No network detail is added.
  - **Which shaders:** by interface, as BananaNative matches them (`ndsr_iface`): one combined 2D texture, one 2D storage image loaded and stored (no atomics), one read-only storage buffer, nothing else (`upsq_match`). All four networks have it, and so do 49 more of the game's compute shaders, other networks. `DM_VK_UPSCALER_SQUARE_CS` still lists keys besides.
  - **Which dispatches:** only one whose input is E5B9G9R9 and whose output is a larger B10G11R11 image (`upsq_run`). E5B9G9R9 is the networks' square-root input; a B10G11R11 input is linear (BananaNative), and the square would be wrong for it. Any other dispatch of those shaders runs as the game made it.
  - **What:** it samples the input at each output texel's centre, writes `x * |x|` into an RGBA16F intermediate and blits that into the output.
  - `DM_VK_UPSCALER_SQUARE=0` (or `DM_VK_UPSCALER_SQUARE_CS=0`) runs the network. A GPU that can't filter, store or blit those formats runs it too, with a red line (129).
- **Check:** Kokiri Forest with raw global memory on (#150).
  - OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830): replaced, the frame goes from 103 to 62 ms (10.4 to 15.8 fps), and `nvnSyncWait` from 21 to 1 ms (stream C, q99).
  - Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840), on Ocarina of Time's opt-in tree (#217): the network 11.0 fps (frame p50 88 ms, GPU 73 ms); replaced 17.8 and 16.0 fps over two launches (frame p50 54 and 63 ms, GPU 41 and 48 ms). The log names `fa3033d05c0a41d6`, "replaced (by its interface)".
  - The picture: the same scene, lit and coloured alike (mean difference 6.4 / 255 at the route's end, within the 7.0 between two replaced launches, which differ by falling leaves and Navi), and softer: the network adds detail the bilinear scale does not (mean gradient 8.5 against 4.5–4.7).
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#android) (Switch 2).

### A render pass with colour slot 0 empty hangs or crashes Qualcomm's Adreno driver (Android)

- **Symptom:** Breath of the Wild loses the GPU on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, driver 0x8032004a) at its first frame in the field: `vkQueueSubmit` gives -4 and the loading screen stays up. Before that, one submission a frame takes 250–340 ms on the GPU with its end timestamp never written: the driver's hang recovery. The hang report names one draw, a vertical blur into a 640×360 RGBA8 target (vertex `2decd3e894b3736a`, fragment `26ae1640f3448ba4`). An Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740) runs the same frames. And on a Moto Razr Plus 2023 (Adreno 730, Qualcomm's driver V@0615.98), Mario Kart 8 Deluxe crashes at every course load: `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x4` in `vulkan.adreno.so` (`+0x16ff5c`) on the replay thread, in `vkCmdEndRenderPass` (`rp_end`). The title's demo race crashed at about frame 2,100 in every boot, and a Grand Prix race as its course loaded; the title and menus run. On the same Razr Divinity: Original Sin 2 crashed the same way, at the same address, about 8 s into every start, before its title. That backtrace ran through `vt_create` and `stage_textures`, as the host ended the pass to make a texture for the next draw.
- **Cause, Adreno 730:** a one-draw pass (a full-screen triangle) with colour slots 0–2 empty (`VK_ATTACHMENT_UNUSED`) and an A2B10G10R10 target alone in slot 3, no depth, from 480×270 to 640×360 with the game's dynamic resolution. The pass's end crashes in the driver. It was found with a crash dump of the render pass in flight (a local diagnostic, not in `main`).
- **Cause, Adreno 730, a pass with no colour target:** Divinity's pass has one colour slot, empty, and a 1152×648 `D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT` target. Its one draw is a full-screen triangle (vertex `c59b59c1049fe6f7`, fragment `7c3ace420d40de79`) with every colour channel masked off: a depth-stencil pass the game binds with an empty colour slot. It was the first pass of that shape in the run, and its end crashed. Any end of it would have: the texture upload (`vt_clear_new` → `cb()` → `rp_end`) only ended it there. The fill below needed a used slot above slot 0, so it never reached this pass. Found with the same local dump.
- **Cause, Adreno 830:** an earlier pass of the frame binds that target alone in colour slot 5, with slots 0–4 empty (`VK_ATTACHMENT_UNUSED`).
  - That pass itself runs. The later pass that draws into the target as slot 0 is the one that hangs.
  - The hanging draw's pipeline, shaders, textures and depth image are the healthy horizontal pass's, so only the target and its history differ ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md) problem 14 lists what was ruled out).
  - Skipping the first pass's draws doesn't help, because its clear still begins it.
  - An empty slot 6 below a used slot 7 (a G-buffer pass) needs nothing. Only an empty slot 0 does.
- **Fix:** on every Adreno on Qualcomm's own driver such a pass gets a scratch RGBA8 target in slot 0 (`gfx_vk_draw.inc` `slot0_fill_on`, `slot0_scratch`). It was on Adreno 8xx only until Mario Kart 8 Deluxe crashed on the 730.
  - The driver is told by its Vulkan driver ID (`VkPhysicalDeviceDriverProperties`, core in 1.2, `VK_KHR_driver_properties` before): `VK_DRIVER_ID_QUALCOMM_PROPRIETARY` turns it on (`gfx_vk.c` `g_qcom_driver`, logged as `[vk] driver ID …`). A driver that gives no ID counts as Qualcomm's when its `driverVersion` has the top bit set (0x80267062 on the 730), as Mesa's never does. The device name can't tell them apart: Turnip builds report `Turnip Adreno (TM) 650`, `PurpleVK-public Adreno (TM) 650`, or `Adreno (TM) 840 (git-62ac221a33)/unknown`.
  - One image serves every pass, since an attachment may be larger than the framebuffer. It is made again larger when a pass is larger, and the earlier ones are kept, because cached framebuffers name their views.
  - Its sides are rounded up to 512. Mario Kart 8 Deluxe's dynamic resolution grows the pass a few texels at a time (480×270, 484×272, 493×277 …), which used up the 8 sizes allowed in seconds; the next larger pass kept slot 0 empty and crashed (red line 152).
  - Layered and multisample passes with a used slot above an empty slot 0 keep slot 0 empty, with a red line.
  - A pass whose colour slots are all empty is made with none (`colorAttachmentCount` 0, `g_slot0_none`), as for a game that binds no colour target, and its draws' pipelines are made for that pass. It needs no image, and it covers layered and multisample passes too. A scratch target in slot 0 also stopped Divinity's crash, but would be loaded and stored in each such pass: 28 a frame at Divinity's title, and its 1536×1536 shadow-map passes are of that shape too. The log's periodic line counts both kinds (`made with no colour slot as every one was empty`).
  - `DM_VK_SLOT0_FILL=1` turns both on for any GPU, `0` off.
- **Check, Adreno 730:** Mario Kart 8 Deluxe 4.0.1 on the Razr Plus 2023, a game app from `main` 0d83f7df with this change and no switches set. The demo race played through to the title again, the scratch image took 2 sizes (512×512, then 1024×512), and about 1,790 passes were filled in it. A 50cc Grand Prix race at Mario Kart Stadium ran to its results, and the save loaded after a cold start. Without the change it crashed in 4 of 4 boots. With the 8xx-only default and `DM_VK_SLOT0_FILL=1` it ran out of sizes and crashed in 2 of 3 runs. Not run with it: another game on the 730 or a 6xx; Breath of the Wild's 830 path is unchanged but for the rounding.
- **Check, Adreno 730, Divinity:** Divinity: Original Sin 2 (v196608) on the Razr Plus 2023, the phone's own APK with only Divinity's host from `main` 25bcaf95 plus this change, no switches set, a private copy of `saves/combat`'s `AutoSave_2`. Two launches reached the title, and Continue loaded the save. The first, at the game's interval, then ran the walk below deck and the top deck's first fight for about 7 minutes. Below deck it ran at 30.0 fps (GPU 24.9 ms, about 1,050 draws), and the fight at 23.4 fps (GPU 34 ms, replay CPU 41 ms, about 1,700 draws). The second launch, with `DM_PRESENT_INTERVAL=display`, ran 58.7 fps below deck (GPU 13.0 ms). The log counted 182,166 passes made with no colour slot in the first launch's 13,182 presents, and no red line beyond the game's usual ones. Without the change it crashed 8–10 s into every start: 3 of 3 with a host from `main` (q324), and 3 of 3 here (`main` with the local pass dump, twice, and this change with `DM_VK_SLOT0_FILL=0`).
- **Check, the passes with no colour slot on the other Qualcomm GPUs:** whether a pass has that shape depends on the game's targets, not the GPU. Hosts from `main` 2fd8209e with and without the change, swapped into copies of each phone's own APK, no switches set.
  - Divinity on the OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, Vulkan 1.3.284), the Razr's route at the game's interval, two runs each way (with, without, without, with).
