Every run reached the fight with no crash and the same red lines, and the screenshots match (the Red Prince below deck in the lamps' light and shadow, the fight). 108,503 passes made with no colour slot in each run with the change: 5 a frame below deck, 16 in the fight. GPU median below deck 15.2 and 13.7 ms with it, 14.1 and 14.5 without (the Pad 3 is at 30 fps there and its clock floats); in the fight 19.5 and 19.6 with it, 19.5 and 19.5 without; 30.0 and 29.9 fps throughout.
  - No pass of the shape (0 in the log's count, so nothing changed): Skyrim, FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE, Animal Crossing and Brilliant Diamond on the Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740); Breath of the Wild, Ocarina of Time, Shield, Super Mario Odyssey, Skyward Sword HD and Link's Awakening on the Pad 3; Link's Awakening and Skyward Sword HD on the Retroid Pocket 5 (Adreno 650); Let's Go and Breath of the Wild on the Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840). Skyrim's depth-only passes bind no colour slot at all; it was also run without the change, with the same picture and numbers.
- **Check, Adreno 830:** Breath of the Wild on the Pad 3, the `outdoors` save. With the fix it runs into the field with no hang report. With `DM_VK_SLOT0_FILL=0` it hangs and loses the GPU as before. Filling every empty slot below a used one also stops it; filling only the slot 7 pass's slot 6, or zeroing the write masks of empty slots, doesn't.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) (Adreno 830), [Mario Kart 8 Deluxe](../../dm/games/0100152000022000/README.md) (Adreno 730), [Divinity: Original Sin 2](../../dm/games/010027400CDC6000/README.md#on-the-moto-razr-plus-2023) (Adreno 730, a pass with no colour target).

### A small layered render pass crashes the Adreno 650's driver (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only, on a Retroid Pocket 5 (Adreno 650, Qualcomm's driver 0x802ea000 built 2023-09-27, Vulkan 1.1.128). OCTOPATH TRAVELER crashes about 3 s into every start, before its first frame is presented: `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x2f100000058 pc=…(vulkan.adreno.so+0x170020)`, thread `NVN queue repla`, right after its first colour targets are made. The address is the same in every run, so the driver reads a value it never set, not a pointer of ours. `DM_VK_UBWC=0`, `DM_VK_SAFE=1` and `DM_VK_RAST_DISCARD=0` change nothing.
- **Cause:** `vkCreateFramebuffer` for a layered framebuffer whose texels fit in the GPU's tile memory. The faulting code is the driver's tiling setup, which `vkCreateFramebuffer` runs. It was found with a local trace of every render pass, framebuffer and pass begin (not in `main`).
  - OCTOPATH TRAVELER draws its 32×32×32 A2B10G10R10 colour-grading LUT as 32 layers in its first frames: one target of 4 bytes a texel, 128 KB in all. The game's layered pass just before it, four RGBA16F volumes of the same size (1 MB), runs.
  - With scratch slots added to the LUT's pass, framebuffers of 4, 8, 12 and 20 bytes a texel (128 to 640 KB) crashed, and of 28 and 36 bytes (896 KB, 1.1 MB) ran, with three attachments or four. So the size decides, not the attachment count or the format.
  - Nor is it the 3D target's 2D-array view (`rp_prepare_`): the LUT drawn into a real 2D array, and into one in RGBA16F, crashed the same.
  - With `DM_VK_LAYERED=0` (targets attached a layer at a time) the game runs, but a LUT drawn that way keeps only one slice ([Layered passes for vertex shaders that write the layer](#layered-passes-for-vertex-shaders-that-write-the-layer)).
- **Fix:** on Qualcomm's own driver on an Adreno 6xx, a layered pass under 1 MB of texels gets scratch colour slots after the game's, RGBA32F 2D arrays, until its texels reach 1 MB (`gfx_vk_layerpad.inc` `layer_pad_on`, `layer_pad`). No fragment shader output reaches them, so their texels are left undefined. The LUT's pass takes two.
  - Each slot keeps one image, made again larger when a pass is larger. The earlier ones are kept, since cached framebuffers name their views (4 sizes a slot).
  - A pass that the 8 slots can't take to 1 MB (under 8,192 texels in all) is attached as it is, with red line 186.
  - `DM_VK_LAYER_PAD=1` turns it on for any GPU, `0` off. The log counts the padded passes (`[vk] layered render passes padded past 1 MB of texels …`).
  - The driver is told by its driver ID (`g_qcom_driver`, as for [the empty colour slot 0](#a-render-pass-with-colour-slot-0-empty-hangs-or-crashes-qualcomms-adreno-driver-android)) and the Adreno 6xx by its device ID (0x06xxxxxx; the Adreno 650's is 0x6050002). The Moto Razr Plus 2023's Adreno 730, also on a Vulkan 1.1 driver (0x80267062), ran about 330,000 of Xenoblade Chronicles 2's layered passes without it, so newer Adrenos are left as they are.
- **Check:** OCTOPATH TRAVELER 1.0.3 on the Retroid Pocket 5, a game app from `main` a50dfcb0 with this change and no switches set.
  - From a new game: the title, H'aanit's flashback, S'warkii, the radar tutorial and the walk toward the headman, at about 49 fps (frame p50 20.4 ms) and 2.16 GB PSS. The grading looks as on the Pixel 9 Pro XL: S'warkii's red-tinted edges and its bluish mist.
  - About 300 passes are padded in each 5-second log window: the game draws its LUT again every frame.
  - Without the change, every start crashed: 4 of 4 in the first test, and 3 of 3 here. The same build with `DM_VK_LAYER_PAD=0` crashes the same way.
  - Not run with it: another game on the Retroid Pocket 5, or another Adreno 6xx.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER](../../dm/games/010057D006492000/README.md).

### A depth image a copy wrote keeps a stale low-resolution depth on Qualcomm's Adreno driver (Android)

- **Symptom:** Skyrim's first-person hands and weapon are drawn in one flat colour, (63, 77, 74), the scene target's clear colour, with no texture or lighting. On an Ayn Odin 2 (Adreno 740, Qualcomm's driver 0x802a4000) it happens every time a save with the axe drawn is loaded and the player looks down at Helgen Keep's first-room gate; an AYN Thor Pro (the same GPU, driver 0x802a4035) showed it in play. Equipping the axe in the session draws it, and so do the Mac and Turnip on the same Odin 2. `DM_VK_ASYNC_PIPE=0` and `DM_VK_POS_ROWS=1` change nothing.
- **Cause:** the model is a depth-only draw into the scene's depth image (s26, `D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT` 1600×900), a copy of its 1280×720 region into another depth image (s35, `vkCmdCopyImage`), and the colour draw (vertex `f7057c92d3b43ddb`, fragment `4cc831b81dfc0b2a`) tested depth-`EQUAL` against s35, without depth writes. s35 gets no depth from any draw, only the copy, every frame.
  - The copy was bit-exact, and the colour draw's inputs matched the depth draw's. Yet `EQUAL` and `LEQUAL` rejected the whole model, while `GEQUAL` and `ALWAYS` drew it, and the same draws against s26 drew it. A per-pixel test cannot do that. Qualcomm's driver keeps a low-resolution depth (LRZ) beside a depth image and carries it from one render pass to the next, and a copy into the image does not make it forget that. The next passes test their less and equal draws against depth the image doesn't hold.
  - What didn't change it, done to s35 in a pass of its own before or after the copy: a clear with loadOp `CLEAR` (to 1 or 0, of the copy's rect or the whole image), a clear with `vkCmdClearAttachments`, loadOp `DONT_CARE`, an empty pass with loadOp `LOAD`, a clear in a pass when the image is made, the copy drawn instead (`gfx_vk_dscopy.inc`, with the pass loading or clearing first), and clear values handed to every pass's begin. Nor did q328's earlier tries: the copy split by aspect or through a buffer, the image's first contents (0, 1, zeros copied in), `MUTABLE_FORMAT`, `NoContraction` on `Fma`, `DM_VK_POS_ROWS=1`, `DM_VK_PREWARM=0`.
  - What did, inside the first pass on s35 after the copy: a depth clear of one pixel with `vkCmdClearAttachments`, at the pass's start or at its end; a draw with depth writes on and compare `ALWAYS`, even one whose fragments a stencil test kills or whose scissor holds no pixel. Turnip's LRZ notes (`tu_lrz.cc`) list both as things LRZ can't be kept through. One such pass is enough: the passes after it draw right. A pipeline with rasterizer discard did nothing.
- **Fix:** the first render pass on a depth image after a copy into it (`copy_t2t`: a copy, a blit, a resolve or a drawn copy; `vtex.ds_copied`) begins with a draw that has depth writes on, compare `ALWAYS`, no colour writes and a scissor of no pixels, so the image keeps its contents (`gfx_vk_dscopy.inc` `copied_depth_pass`, from `rp_start`).
  - Its pipeline is made once for each render pass: the depth copies' vertex shader, no fragment shader. The game's next draw binds its own state again (`bf_reset`).
  - It's on for Qualcomm's own driver, told by its driver ID (`g_qcom_driver`, as for [the empty colour slot 0](#a-render-pass-with-colour-slot-0-empty-hangs-or-crashes-qualcomms-adreno-driver-android)), so not on Turnip. `DM_VK_COPIED_DEPTH=1` turns it on for any driver, `0` off. The log says when it first applies (`[vk] the first render pass on a depth image after a copy into it begins with …`).
  - Uploads into a depth image (`vkCmdCopyBufferToImage`) are left as they are: no game has been seen to need them.
- **Check:** Skyrim 1.1.404 on the Odin 2, from `main` a3bbaa9a with this change and no switches set. The loaded save draws the axe at the gate. With `DM_VK_COPIED_DEPTH=0` it's flat again, and the two pictures are the same away from the axe. From the tester's earlier autosave, the keep's entrance and the room with the axe taken from Gunjar and readied in the session look right, the axe drawn. Final Fantasy Resonance boots to its first screen with the change (no depth copy there). Not run: other games that copy depth (Link's Awakening's half-size copy), Adreno 6xx or 8xx, the Thor Pro's driver.
- **Found on:** [The Elder Scrolls V: Skyrim](../../dm/games/01000A10041EA000/README.md#the-first-person-model-drawn-in-one-flat-colour-resolved) (droidplay-core#444).

### One image in two colour slots of a pass is drawn wrong on the Adreno 830 (Android)

- **Symptom:** Breath of the Wild's Shrine of Resurrection is drawn dark and blurred on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830): Link unlit, the walls a blur. The Odin 2's Adreno 740 draws the same frames right.
- **Cause:** the game's 1280×720 G-buffer passes bind one image in colour slots 1 and 2 and another in slots 3 and 4, and write only the first slot of each pair (channel mask 0xf0f0ff).
  - Each slot was its own attachment of the same image view. Vulkan leaves attachments that alias in one subpass undefined.
  - Every attachment loads and stores, so the unwritten copy is stored too. The Adreno 830 apparently stores it after the written one, so the pass's writes to that image are lost.
- **Fix:** a pass attaches each image once (`gfx_vk_draw.inc` `dup_slots`), on every GPU.
  - It is kept at the first of its slots that the draw beginning the pass writes, else at the first. Its other slots are `VK_ATTACHMENT_UNUSED`.
  - A clear of a slot left unused clears the kept slot (`dup_slot`).
  - A later draw of the pass that writes a slot left unused and not the kept one loses that write. It is counted in the stats line, with a red line.
  - `DM_VK_DUP_SLOTS=0` attaches the image at every slot, as before.
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild's `outdoors` save on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Samsung's driver 0x80320001): the Shrine is drawn as on the Odin 2, with no write lost, and the GPU time drops from 21.4 to 20.5 ms. With `DM_VK_DUP_SLOTS=0` it is dark as before. On the Pad 3 a local test leaving the later slot of each pair unused drew it right too. A Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840) draws it right either way, and with the fix its GPU time drops from 30.3 to 29.6 ms.

### An sRGB window texture shown through a UNORM swapchain is a gamma too dark (Android)

- **Symptom:** Link's Awakening on Android is drawn far too dark on screen: Mabe Village outside Marin's house looks like night (the screenshot's mean colour about 0.04–0.06 of full scale), the title and the menus duller than on the Mac. Frame dumps look right: the dump's `present.png` of the same frame is bright, as on the Mac.
- **Cause:** the game presents a 1920×1080 `RGBA8_SRGB` window texture (vk43), and the front end's swapchain was `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` (format 37, chosen whenever the surface lists it). The front end puts the frame on the screen with `vkCmdBlitImage`, and a blit converts as sampling and rendering do: it decodes an sRGB source to linear and stores into a UNORM destination without encoding. So the swapchain got linear values, which the display shows as sRGB: every channel raised to about the power 2.2. The texture's bytes were right, which is why the dump, a copy of them, showed no fault (its mean 0.355 against the screen's 0.10: 0.355^2.2 = 0.10).
- **Fix:** the swapchain is made in the presented frame's encoding (`front_android.c` `sc_encoding`): `R8G8B8A8_SRGB` for an sRGB frame, where the surface lists it, so the blit decodes and encodes again and carries the frame's bytes; `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` for the rest, as before. When the encoding changes, the swapchain is made again before the next frame, with a `[front]` line. `DM_VK_SC_SRGB=0` keeps the UNORM swapchain.
- **Check:** Link's Awakening on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, Android 16): the swapchain is made again in format 43 at the first frame. Mabe Village's mean colour goes from about (0.04, 0.06, 0.02) to (0.16, 0.24, 0.13), and the title's from (0.37, 0.48, 0.85) to (0.54, 0.67, 0.92). Frames in UNORM, such as other games' windows, see no change.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md#android).

### Clears in a layered pass clear every layer (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, a 3D or array render target drawn a slice at a time by draws that write the layer (a layered pass) and cleared each frame keeps, in every slice but the first, what earlier frames drew there. With additive blending it grows or flickers from frame to frame.
- **Cause:** `clear_color_pass` and `clear_ds_pass` cleared through `vkCmdClearAttachments` with a rect of layer 0 alone. NVN clears the whole bound target. A full clear of a 3D target takes that path whenever its size matches the pass's, since a 3D image has one array layer (`clear_color`, `clear_ds`).
- **Cause, the other order:** a clear before the first layered draw opened a pass of one layer (`clear_in_pass`, `rp_prepare_(0)`), so a 3D target was cleared at slice 0 alone, and that draw then reopened the pass layered and loaded the other slices as they were.
- **Fix:**
  - The rect covers every layer of the pass's framebuffer (`P.layers`, `clear_rect_layers`).
  - A clear whose target's view covers more than one layer or slice asks for a layered pass (`clear_rp_prepare`: `rp_prepare_(1)`, the layers as `rt_layers` gives a layered draw), so it reaches all of them whether it comes before the layered draws or after, as the Mac does in a pass of its own ([Clears of layered render targets in a pass that is not layered](#clears-of-layered-render-targets-in-a-pass-that-is-not-layered)).
  - A full clear whose pass still has fewer layers than the view (`DM_VK_LAYERED=0`, or another target of the pass with fewer) is made outside the pass instead (`vkCmdClearColorImage` / `vkCmdClearDepthStencilImage` on the view's range; a 3D level is cleared whole). A clear limited by the scissor has no such way out: red line 172 names it.
  - A clear with a channel mask or a partial stencil mask is a draw of layer 0, which this does not widen: red line 171 says when one meets a layered pass.
- **Found on:** code reading, from shirai91/nx2android 37c76f613 (carried by DeathKhan/nx2android), where the same rect in that fork's Vulkan backend left Final Fantasy Resonance's 32x32x32 light volumes uncleared in slices 1-31: the fog boxes that read them changed every frame on its first road (pass dumps over 4 frames at the road's centre: 29.6, 40.7, 31.7, 39.7; with every layer cleared, 30.4 in each). Not reproduced on this replay yet.

### Depth and stencil read back by the CPU in NVN's layouts (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, a game reading a depth or stencil texture on the CPU (`nvnTextureReadTexels`) or copying one into a buffer (`nvnCommandBufferCopyTextureToBuffer`) gets the wrong bytes: for `DEPTH32F_STENCIL8` every row after the first holds another row's depths and the rest stays zero, the stencil always reads 0, and `DEPTH24_STENCIL8` and `DEPTH24` come back as float bits instead of 24-bit unorm. No red line.
- **Cause:** `copy_t2b` and `mtl_read_texels` copied a combined image's depth aspect alone into a slice sized and pitched by the guest's texel. Vulkan writes an aspect's buffer copy tightly packed at its own size (depth 2 or 4 bytes a texel, stencil 1), whatever the image's combined format, so an 8-byte `DEPTH32F_STENCIL8` row was read where 4-byte texels were written, and NVN's own layouts were never built: `ZF32_X24S8` (the float, the stencil byte, 3 zero bytes) and `S8Z24` (24-bit unorm depth, the stencil in the top byte). `nvn_fmt_mtl.c` keeps `DEPTH24` and `DEPTH24_STENCIL8` as `D32_SFLOAT` (`_S8_UINT`), so their copies are floats too.
- **Fix:** `ds_wb` (`gfx_vk.c`) copies each aspect the image and the guest's format have into a plane of its own, and the completion thread rebuilds NVN's texels from the planes into guest memory (`wb_apply`, `dm/android/ds_texels.h`), for `STENCIL8`, `DEPTH16`, `DEPTH24`, `DEPTH32F`, `DEPTH24_STENCIL8` and `DEPTH32F_STENCIL8`, from `D16_UNORM`, `D32_SFLOAT`, `D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT` and the `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT` fallback. Colour images are copied as before. A buffer → texture copy into such a texture (`copy_b2t`) goes the other way: `ds_up` splits NVN's texels into the same planes and copies each aspect in, the stencil too, so texture → buffer → texture comes back as it was (it copied the guest's bytes into the depth aspect as they were; red line 41 stays for a format `ds_texels.h` does not know). `ds_unorm24` and `ds_float24` are each other's inverse over all 2^24 depths. A texture's first upload from its pool memory is unchanged.
- **Found on:** code reading, while comparing the replay with DeathKhan/nx2android, whose Vulkan backend had the same flaw (fixed there in 2883cef1a). `dm/tools/tests/test_ds_texels.py` checks the layouts; with `DM_TEST_SERIAL` it runs `dm/android/tests/t_ds_readback.c` on a device (each format cleared, copied out aspect by aspect, rebuilt; then texels uploaded as `ds_up` does and read back). No game is known to show it yet.

### RGB565-family and RGBA4 textures in Vulkan's channel order (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only, on every GPU: RGBA4, RGB5, RGB5A1 or RGB565 textures sample with red and blue exchanged (RGBA4: its four channels reversed). Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu!'s party, bag and Catching Pocket sprites: a cyan Pikachu, a blue Pidgey, a brown Oddish, a blue Poké Ball and blue pocket frames, while the menus' own art is right. No red line.
- **Cause:** `nvn_fmt_to_mtl` maps NVN's RGB-order 16-bit formats (0x39–0x3C, red at bit 0) by name to the Metal format with red and blue exchanged and says so in its swizzle. `vk_of_mtl` takes that Metal format's Vulkan twin (`R5G6B5_UNORM_PACK16`, `A1R5G5B5_UNORM_PACK16`, `R4G4B4A4_UNORM_PACK16`: red at the top), and `vt_create` dropped the swizzle. The guest's bits went into the image as they were, so the GPU read red where blue is. The same mapping holds on the Mac, which converts ([RGB565-family textures in Metal's channel order](#rgb565-family-textures-in-metals-channel-order)).
- **Fix:** the Mac's model. Every image of these formats holds the Vulkan format's order, as the GPU renders, clears, blits and copies into it; only guest memory holds the guest's. The bits are exchanged where they cross (`dm/android/rb16.h`; `vtex.rbx` from the format table's swizzle, red's bit position or 4 for RGBA4):
  - on the CPU into the staging data of uploads (`vt_upload_`, linear textures included) and buffer → texture copies (`copy_b2t`);
  - in the GPU's copy as the completion thread lands it (`chunk_done`, before the snapshot compare), for texture → buffer copies (`copy_t2b`), `ReadTexels` (`mtl_read_texels`) and linear render targets' write-backs (`lin_writeback`).

  The BGR family (0x95–0x98) keeps its bits, and so does every other format. The first texture of each of the four formats logs `[vk] first NVN <format> texture`.
- **Note:**
  - **Not a view swizzle alone:** that would read uploads right, and swap whatever the GPU draws into such a target.
  - **Not the guest's own Vulkan formats:** `B5G6R5_UNORM_PACK16` is optional, `A1B5G5R5_UNORM_PACK16` needs `VK_KHR_maintenance5` and `A4B4G4R4_UNORM_PACK16` needs `VK_EXT_4444_formats`, and the device is made at Vulkan 1.1 with neither extension.
  - **As on the Mac:** a view of such a texture in another format of the same size (R16, RG8) sees Vulkan's order, and a raw copy between such a texture and a BGR-family one moves the converted bits. No game is known to do either.
  - **Not covered:** buffer textures of these formats (texel buffer views read guest memory in place and take no swizzle) still read red and blue exchanged. RGB5's alpha (0x3A, no alpha) is not forced to 1, as no "X" format's is on Android.
- **Check:**
  - `dm/tools/tests/test_rb16.py`: the formats that take the exchange, from the real table; known texels each way (Pikachu's yellow RGB5A1 texel reads as yellow); every 16-bit value comes back for each shift.
  - Let's Go 1.0.0 from `saves/viridian_forest_flip` on the OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830), the Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715) and the Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950): the party, bag and Catching Pocket in their own colours (yellow Pikachu, red and white Poké Ball). Up to those menus the game makes only RGB5A1 textures of the four formats (the log's `first NVN` line).
  - A control host from `main` without the change on the same Pixel and save: the sprites swapped as before, and the title, the X menu and the field otherwise the same.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu!](../../dm/games/010003F003A34000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (enjoyerofslop/droidplay-core#417, reported on an Adreno 825; reproduced on the Adreno 830 and the Mali-G715).

### Pipeline keys built from the state objects' fields

- **Symptom:** new graphics pipelines made all through play, also in a level seen before (a few per frame, about 1 to 2 ms each on the Adreno); the start-up list of pipelines grew past 186,000 keys over a few sessions, and making it again took the fast cores for most of an hour.
- **Cause:** `bind_state` copies the game's state objects whole, and the key took the colour, polygon, blend and depth / stencil states as copied: the bits their fields leave over (16 of colour's 32, 24 of polygon's, 18 of each blend's 64, 25 of depth / stencil's) hold whatever the game's object held, since the game fills objects on its stack without `SetDefaults`. Also in the key: blend enables of targets the pass does not have, and which of the three polygon offsets is on.
- **Fix:** the key takes each state field by field over zeroed bits, masks the blend enables to the pass's targets and keeps one depth-bias switch. The start-up list makes at most its first 3,000 pipelines (`DM_VK_PREWARM`), compiles them without the replay's lock and keeps no repeats.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): 418 pipelines in a session after the fix, none made while playing a level.

### Long passes sent to the GPU in pieces

- **Symptom:** the game waits for the GPU (SyncWait about 12 ms a wait) after the replay has recorded the frame, though the GPU's own work is shorter than a frame.
- **Cause:** a chunk went to the GPU only at a change of render targets, and most of a frame is two passes of about 140 draws each: the GPU started each one only when the CPU had recorded all of it.
- **Fix:** a chunk of 64 draws is submitted before the next draw, in the middle of a pass; the pass is begun again with its targets loaded (passes load and store their attachments).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): SyncWait 12.5 → 5 ms a wait, a level from about 25 to 30 fps at render scale 0.8.

### Constant buffers copied only as far as the shader reads

- **Symptom:** frames with many effects at 18 fps; memcpy and memcmp at the top of the render thread's profile.
- **Cause:** every draw copied each bound constant buffer whole (up to 64 KB, six or seven a shader): the translator declares every buffer as 4096 vec4, so the size bound was all the replay knew.
- **Fix:** at shader load, `spv_cb_ext2` takes the largest constant element index of the access chains on each buffer's variable, by operand (`spv_cb_ext` matched words, so a literal equal to the variable's id, such as an `OpConstant`'s value, kept the whole buffer); a computed index or any other use keeps the whole buffer, and a draw copies that much; the copies are shared within a chunk when the bytes are the same (`ucopy`). When the shader is translated, `tools/spv_android.py` `cb_extents` writes each buffer's extent into res.json (`ext`), bounding computed indices as well: the integer registers run along every path with small sets of known values, so a loop with a known trip count is unrolled wherever it tests its exit; the host takes it where it is not below its own scan's (`cb_ext_pick`). `DM_VK_CB_EXT2=0` and `DM_VK_RES_EXT=0` restore the old scan; `DM_VK_EXT_CHECK=1` binds each buffer whole with NaN past its extent, so an extent too small shows.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): its scene shaders read 64 bytes to 2.3 KB of each buffer; levels with many effects held 30 fps at render scale 0.8. [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) (Android, Ayn Odin 2): its dumped frame read 26.2 MB of constant buffers, 21.0 MB by operand and 6.6 MB with res.json's extents; vertex shader `5ee9a682fff60855` copied a 64 KB c3 for each of 222 draws and reads 400 bytes of it (a do-while counter, 2 then 1, tested in the loop's latch). The device timing is open.

### Read-only storage resources in the vertex pipeline

- **Symptom:** on a Mali-G76 (POCO M5s, Helio G95), menus drawn and gameplay black; Hades II (the game's own Vulkan shaders) drawn on the same phone. Open: not yet tested on the phone with the fix.
- **Cause:** without the `vertexPipelineStoresAndAtomics` feature, Vulkan wants every storage buffer and storage image of a vertex, tessellation or geometry shader decorated `NonWritable` (VUID-RuntimeSpirv-NonWritable-06340). The translator never adds it; the Adreno has the feature, the Mali-G76 does not ([its report](http://vulkan.gpuinfo.org/displayreport.php?id=13693)), so the shader breaks the rule whether or not it writes.
- **Fix:** on such a GPU, `sh_module` passes each module through `spv_readonly` (`dm/android/spv_readonly.h`): `NonWritable` on each such variable the shader never writes through. A shader that does write one gets a `[RED]` line naming it: that needs the feature. `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_readonly.py` builds it for the PC with the NDK's clang and checks it with `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`; with `DM_SPV_DIR` it runs over a game's packed shaders.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): 88 of its 4,857 vertex shaders read storage buffers (154 modules with the depth variants decorated, all valid); 11 vertex shaders in `agl_common` write one (`12112abcfbe273fa` and others) and stay as they are.

### Transform feedback captured in the fragment stage (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only, on Arm's Mali (the Pixel 9 Pro XL's Mali-G715): characters skinned through transform feedback aren't drawn, only their effects; the log has `[RED] [vk] transform feedback: the GPU has no vertex-stage storage writes`.
- **Cause:** the replay captures a vertex shader's feedback outputs with its `.tf.spv` (`spv_android.py` `tf_variant`): before each return, the outputs are stored as bits into storage buffers (set 0, binding 100 + buffer), vertex v at v × stride, drawn as points with rasterization off (`tf_capture`). Vulkan allows stores from the vertex stage only with `vertexPipelineStoresAndAtomics`, which Mali's driver lacks. So the capture was refused, and the feedback buffers the character draws read as vertex streams were never written. The Pixel's driver (0xd803000) does list `VK_EXT_transform_feedback`, which the replay doesn't use: it captures whole output variables, where NVN's layouts take the first components of each output (Arceus stores 3 of output 0's 4), and the capture below needs only fragment-stage stores.
- **Fix:** where the GPU has no vertex-stage stores but has `fragmentStoresAndAtomics` (`tf_frag`), the capture runs in the fragment stage (`dm/android/spv_tffrag.h`), with no game built again:
  - `spv_tff_vs` wraps the vertex shader's entry point (the way `spv_alpha.h` does, so the shader's own returns stay as they are). After the shader runs, vertex v's position is the centre of pixel (v & 1023, v >> 10) of a 1024 × 1024 grid and its point size 1. The clip and cull distances the shader writes become 1, and the layer and viewport it writes 0; those it doesn't write stay unwritten. The translator declares 8 clip distances and 1 cull distance in every vertex shader, past the 8 the phones take in all (`maxCombinedClipAndCullDistances`), and a first version that wrote all 9 made Qualcomm's V@0615 driver (the Adreno 730) abort in `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` (`stack corruption detected`) on Arceus's capture. The wrapper is a lazy module like the others (`lm_new_t`).
  - `spv_tff_fs` makes a fragment shader from `res.json`'s feedback layout at run time (`lm_gen`). Its inputs are the feedback outputs, flat, and it stores their bits where `tf_variant` would have, with v read back from `gl_FragCoord`. An output the vertex shader lacks stores 0, as `tf_variant` does.
  - `tf_capture` draws the points into an attachment-less 1024-wide framebuffer, as many rows as the draw needs. A one-pixel point covers just the pixel it sits on, so each record is written once, however often the GPU shades a vertex (Mali shades positions first). The capture's polygon offset is cleared, since there's no depth to bias. A draw of more than 1,048,576 vertices isn't captured (red line 180).
  - `DM_VK_TF_FRAG=1` takes this path on any GPU with fragment stores (to compare it on an Adreno), `0` never. GPUs with vertex-stage stores (Adreno, Xclipse) keep the `.tf.spv` capture by default.
- **Check (2026-11-11):** `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_tffrag.py` (`spirv-val` on the wrapper and the fragment shaders), and over Arceus's packed modules (`DM_SPV_DIR`): all 6,608 vertex shaders wrapped and its 5 capture fragment shaders pass. Arceus's opening, from a new game to the Prelude Beach dialogue, the same adb route each run:
  - Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715, Android 17), one build: with `DM_VK_TF_FRAG=0` the player falling from the sky, the player holding Arceus's light, the three starters and Laventon on the beach are all missing (red line 115); by default all are drawn and animated. 30 fps both (frame p50 33.4 ms); GPU p50 at the beach 17.5–18.5 ms against 16.5–17.4 ms, with the characters drawn. No new red line. The Pixel's driver has no clip or cull distances at all (`shaderClipDistance` 0), so writing the declared ones would have broken the wrapper there too.
  - Moto Razr Plus 2023 (Adreno 730, V@0615.98): main and this change draw the same frames, the capture in the vertex stage (frame p50 42–46 ms, GPU p50 25.4–27.7 ms in both). With `DM_VK_TF_FRAG=1` the fragment-stage capture draws the same characters at the same speed.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Arceus](../../dm/games/01001F5010DFA000/README.md) on the Pixel 9 Pro XL: its 5 capture shaders (`a3fe02b2f67c772e` and others) write skinned positions (output 0, xyz) and the normal's and tangent's packed bits (outputs 1 and 2, x only) into three buffers.

### Draws under rasterizer discard that write memory (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: characters skinned on the GPU are not drawn while the world, the cutscene cameras and the HUD around them are; no `[RED]` line. On an Adreno 830 logcat may also have Adreno's `Failed to link shaders` / `Pipeline create failed` and `Shader compilation failed for shaderType: 4`, but those are the shader module identifiers' cache misses (`VK_PIPELINE_COMPILE_REQUIRED`, as many as the `[vk] occlusion queries …` line's "needed the modules after all"; see [Shader modules made only for pipelines the driver's cache lacks](#shader-modules-made-only-for-pipelines-the-drivers-cache-lacks)), seen in games that draw right too.
- **Cause:** a draw under `SetRasterizerDiscard` still runs its vertex and geometry shaders, with their storage buffer and image writes and atomics. The replay made such a draw only as a transform feedback capture (`tf_capture`), so one without feedback did nothing: the same bug [Rasterizer discard preserves vertex storage writes](#rasterizer-discard-preserves-vertex-storage-writes) fixed for Metal. Xenoblade Chronicles 2 skins each character that way (no transform feedback in the game): a vertex-only draw writes the blended bone matrices, 48 bytes at `gl_VertexIndex`, into slot 0 of one 384,000-byte storage buffer, which the character's draws read, and a variant updates vertex buffers that later draws read as streams.
- **Fix:**
  - `rd_draw` runs a discarded draw whose vertex or geometry stage writes memory (a storage buffer with `BufferUsageFlags.Write`, an image with `TextureUsageFlags.ImageStore`, raw global memory's write log) as a dispatch runs (`vk_dispatch`): the targets' pass ended before its bindings, its vertex (and geometry) stage alone in a pipeline with rasterization off in the pass without attachments (`rd_key`, key `tf` 2, never in the start-up list), a full barrier before and after it. Its storage buffer writes are forwardable write-backs (`g_vdisp`, `disp_like`), so later shaders read them on the GPU as they read a dispatch's (`buf_fwd`, `fwd_find`). A discarded draw that writes nothing still draws nothing; a GPU without `vertexPipelineStoresAndAtomics` leaves it out with `[RED] … rasterizer discard writes memory from its vertex stage` once.
  - Forwarding needs one write-back that covers every byte a shader binds, and XC2 binds the bone buffer differently on the two sides: each skinning draw from the character's slot to the buffer's end, the character's draws the whole buffer. Run as plain dispatches, the skinning draws drew the characters but about 11 reads a frame met a write-back that didn't cover them and waited for the GPU: 24 fps on a Moto Razr Plus 2023 where the game holds 30. A read that meets a discarded draw's write-back it overlaps but isn't inside now learns the union as a home range (`dm/android/rd_home.h`, at most 16 ranges of up to 4 MB). Such a draw's write then goes on the chunk's copy of the whole range (`rd_bind_w`): made once per chunk (`rd_home_copy`), from the newest copy of a chunk in flight on the GPU (that chunk held, as `fwd_find` holds one) or else from guest memory, in place when its offset there keeps the storage buffer alignment, else through a scratch copy copied in before the draw and back after it (`rd_close`; XC2's 48-byte slots against the Adreno's 64-byte alignment).
  - A draw that isn't indexed now reads a vertex stream's bytes from its first vertex to the end of its last vertex's attributes (`stream_rng`), not the stream's whole bound range: XC2 binds each attribute of a buffer as a stream of its own, 32 bytes further on per attribute and as long as the buffer, so the bound ranges ran past the write-back and into the next buffer. Learned from those, a home range grew 32 bytes at a time, swallowed the neighbouring vertex buffers and a uniform buffer, and every draw reading that uniform buffer waited.
  - `DM_VK_RAST_DISCARD=0` leaves every discarded draw without transform feedback out, as before; `DM_VK_RD_HOME=0` gives each write a copy of its own range (the plain-dispatch form). The `[vk] draws not made` line counts the discarded draws made and left out, where their writes went and the home ranges learned.
- **Check (2026-11-11):** `dm/tools/tests/test_rd_home.py` (the range table: learning, chained merges, the size cap, the least recently used range given up, lookups). Moto Razr Plus 2023 (Adreno 730, V@0615.98, Android 16), XC2 2.1.0's game app, the same route each time (New Game on Easy, the opening, King Crustip's tutorial battle), one host with the switches:
  - `DM_VK_RAST_DISCARD=0`: Rex, Gramps and King Crustip missing in the opening and the battle, as on main; battle frame p50 33.3 ms, NVN submit+present 17.3 ms, GPU 23.5 ms.
  - Discarded draws as plain dispatches (`DM_VK_RD_HOME=0`, without the stream ranges): all three drawn, 42 ms frames (submit+present 41.5 ms), 154,000 waits in 9 minutes.
  - This change: all three drawn in the opening (Rex diving in the salvage suit, on deck with Gramps) and the battle (Rex auto-attacking King Crustip), a steady 30 fps (frame p50 33.3, p95 33.9 ms), submit+present 22.5 ms, GPU 27.1 ms; 2 waits on the current submission and about one on a chunk in flight a frame; 343,000 discarded draws made in 12 minutes (about 50 a game frame), no `[RED]` line, no crash.
  - Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Android 16), with this change: all three drawn on the same route, a steady 30 fps (battle frame p50 33.2, p95 34.1 ms), submit+present 22.6 ms, GPU 22.1 ms, about one wait a frame, no `[RED]` line.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#android): every character in the opening and the tutorial battle on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830) and the Razr (Adreno 730). Skinning shaders `fa456dcb6b05c2ce`, `0cf988e8a8ea2aee`, `bce3419b07cb2f3d`, `3fb8ebc13eb37ad7` (`shd_post.wishp` var122, the bone buffer) and `bd4b7f58c16f180f` (it updates a vertex buffer whose bytes it also reads as vertex streams).

### An indexed draw's vertex stream read only as far as the write-back it starts in (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game whose characters are skinned by draws under rasterizer discard (run as dispatches since [Draws under rasterizer discard that write memory](#draws-under-rasterizer-discard-that-write-memory-android)) plays at a fraction of its speed while its cutscenes run at full speed. The GPU sits at its lowest clock and is busy a third of each frame. The `[vk] guest reads of draws` line counts dozens of waits for the current submission a frame.
- **Cause:** an indexed draw's vertex stream is looked up among the forwardable write-backs as bound (`stream_rng`), since which vertices it fetches isn't known. Games bind a stream at its first vertex or attribute with the whole buffer's size, so the range runs past the buffer.
  - Xenoblade Chronicles 3 skins each character into a 576,000-byte buffer, two of them 0x30 bytes apart, and its draws bind the vertices up to 1,120 bytes in, each binding as long as the buffer.
  - Such a range met the next buffer's write too, so `fwd_hit` learned the two buffers as one home range of 1,152,048 bytes (`rd_home.h`). Every skinning write then took a copy of both, 54 copies of 1.15 MB a frame, each with its snapshot.
  - In that copy the second buffer starts 48 bytes off the 64-byte storage buffer alignment. So the character draws that read it as a storage buffer (each inside the write-back) could not bind it there (`buf_fwd`), and waited for the GPU: 53 waits a frame.
- **Fix:** an indexed draw's stream that starts in a forwardable write-back, runs past its end and is no longer than it (a binding into the written buffer, with that buffer's size) is looked up only as far as that end (`stream_clip`, the rule in `dm/android/stream_clip.h`).
  - Only the newest write-back holding the stream's first byte counts, in the current chunk (from before the draw) or the chunks in flight, as `fwd_find` looks.
  - It is clipped before `fwd_find`, so the overrun no longer teaches a home range. Each buffer keeps a write-back of its own, which starts aligned.
  - An index past the vertices the write-back holds would read bytes nothing wrote, on the Switch as here.
  - `DM_VK_STREAM_CLIP=0` looks such streams up as bound, as before. The `[vk] read on the GPU from a dispatch's pending write` line counts the streams clipped.
- **Check (2026-11-12):** `dm/tools/tests/test_stream_clip.py` (XC3's offsets, streams inside, ranges longer than the write-back, the write-back's kind and place). Xenoblade Chronicles 3 1.0.0 on a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Android 16), its game app with only the host swapped, from a fresh start through the opening to the first battle and the field after it:
  - Without this change (q258, and the same diagnostic build without it): the first battle at 5.5–5.8 fps and the field at 6.4–7.1, the GPU at 160 MHz most of the time and busy about a third of each frame. 55 waits for the current submission a frame (53 of them these reads), 54 home copies of 1.15 MB, and the replay thread's CPU 77.7 ms a frame, 29 ms of it in `memcpy`.
  - With it: the battle and the field at 12.0 fps, the median of 127 two-second windows, all at 160 MHz. Each skinned buffer has a write-back of its own. 2 waits for the current submission and 6 for submissions in flight a frame remain. The replay thread's CPU is 37.6 ms a frame (3.5 ms in `memcpy`), the GPU 48.8 ms.
  - With the two fixes for those waits as well (a draw's write read after a barrier, and the first draw of a submission reading on the GPU; their own pull requests): 30 fps, the Switch's rate, with the GPU at 342–443 MHz.
  - The picture is unchanged: the party, the soldiers and the enemies are drawn, the world textured.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md#android) (q258's 5.6–7 fps gameplay).

### Stencil-mode texture slots read the stencil (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only: a dark stipple and stripes over lit surfaces that follow their depth. Xenoblade Chronicles 2 shows it on the characters' skin, hair and clothes in close-ups and over much of the world (crates, walls, the rock spires on the deck), on Adreno and Mali alike; Xenoblade Chronicles 3 draws its titans and terrain as black-and-white zebra stripes. The Mac's frames are clean. No red line.
- **Cause:** a texture slot that reads a depth-stencil texture in stencil mode got a view of the image's depth: `stage_set` gave every depth-stencil slot the depth aspect. Stencil mode is set by a view (`nvnTextureViewSetDepthStencilMode(STENCIL)`) or by the texture's builder (`nvnTextureBuilderSetDepthStencilMode`). The shader tests stencil bits in the raw bits it reads, so it tested bits of the depth's float. The Mac reads the stencil ([Stencil-mode texture views read through `X32_Stencil8`](#stencil-mode-texture-views-read-through-x32_stencil8), [Stencil-mode textures from the texture builder](#stencil-mode-textures-from-the-texture-builder)); the Vulkan replay never did.
  - XC2 classifies its deferred lighting with the stencil. Full-screen passes at half resolution (pass 110 `3a65a75970c5ac32`, then `68bd4ad78d01642b`) read the G-buffer's `D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT` depth-stencil in stencil mode and test bits 3 and 4 (`floatBitsToInt(texel) & 24`) with another target's alpha. They write a material class into a 16-bit depth buffer (`gl_FragDepth` = class / 65535). Each lighting variant then draws at its class's depth with an `EQUAL` test (pass 144, `373c40cf955281dc`).
  - With depth read, bits 3 and 4 followed the depth's mantissa, about two thirds of a lit surface's pixels took a wrong class, and no variant lit them.
- **Fix:** a slot in stencil mode gets a view of the stencil aspect (`gfx_vk_draw.inc` `tex_aspect`, the rule in `dm/android/tex_aspect.h`): the view's mode where the view sets one, else the texture's.
  - The shader declares a float texture (the translator has no integer texture type) and finds the stencil value in the bits it reads, as on the Mac. Vulkan's rules want an integer sampled type for an integer view; Qualcomm's and Arm's drivers hand the bits over as they are.
  - A depth texture without stencil read in stencil mode still reads its depth, with red line 185.
  - The first stencil-mode slot is logged (`[vk] a texture slot in stencil mode reads the stencil of its depth-stencil image`).
- **Check (2026-11-12):** `dm/tools/tests/test_tex_aspect.py` (the rule: colour, depth, stencil mode from the view and from the texture, a view overriding the texture, stencil-only images, stencil mode with no stencil). Xenoblade Chronicles 2 2.1.0, its game app with only the host swapped, from a new game to King Crustip's battle:
  - Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, Android 16): the stipple and stripes are gone from Rex, King Crustip, the deck and the rock spires. A steady 30 fps (battle frame p50 33.2 ms), no red line. Without the fix the same frames have them.
  - Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715, driver 0xd803000, Android 17): the rock spires carry the same stipple without the fix and are clean with it. (Its characters aren't drawn either way: the Mali has no `vertexPipelineStoresAndAtomics`, so the skinning draws are left out.)
  - Xenoblade Chronicles 3 1.0.0 on the S25 Edge, its game app with only the host swapped: the black-and-white zebra stripes over the title's titans and the Everblight Plain's ground and rocks are gone (seen on the Adreno 830 and the Xclipse 950 before).
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#android) on a Galaxy S25 Edge, at v0.8.2 and before; [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md#android), whose zebra stripes are the same bug.

### Subgroup operations rewritten for stages the GPU runs none in

- **Symptom:** Android only: a crash in the driver's shader compiler on every launch, as the first pipelines are built (`CRASH sig=11 addr=0x48 pc=…(libllvm-qgl.so+0x2dcef0)`, thread `NVN queue repla`); the pipeline being built names a fragment shader whose module uses `OpGroupNonUniformShuffle`, and the log's `subgroup operations in stages 0x20` leaves fragment shaders out.
- **Cause:** the translator turns Maxwell's warp instructions into Vulkan subgroup operations in every stage: SHFL into `OpGroupNonUniformShuffle` (or Up, Down, Xor), VOTE into ballot, all, any and all-equal, `S2R SR_LANEID` and the lane masks into the subgroup built-ins, and FSWZADD reads the lane ID. Maxwell computes a fragment shader's derivatives with them (SHFL.IDX within the 2×2 quad, then FSWZADD): the 23 of Brilliant Diamond's 7,173 fragment shaders that shuffle compute `fwidth` that way. Vulkan allows subgroup operations only in `subgroupSupportedStages` (VUID-RuntimeSpirv-None-06343): compute alone on the Adreno 650 (driver 0x802ea000), fragment and compute on the 740 and 830. The Adreno 650's compiler crashes on Brilliant Diamond's shuffles; a small shader's it compiles, and reads lanes of the whole 64-wide wave rather than of the quad (`dm/android/tests/t_quad_shuffle.c`: `Shuffle(v, 0)` wrong in 3,840 of 4,096 pixels).
- **Fix:** `sh_module_code` passes each module through `spv_limits` (`dm/android/spv_limits.h`) on a GPU that runs subgroup operations in some graphics stages only; a module of a stage that runs them, or one that uses none, is made as translated, so the Ayn Odin 2's, OnePlus Pad 3's and Galaxy S25 Edge's fragment shaders are as before. In a fragment shader the subgroup is the 2×2 quad: the lane ID is `(x & 1) | (y & 1) << 1` from `gl_FragCoord`, as Maxwell and Vulkan's quad operations number a quad's lanes, and a shuffle reads the named lane through fine derivatives, which every quad computes: the value's two 16-bit halves as floats (exact integers), the other column's by `DPdxFine` and the other row's by `DPdyFine`, then the lane's of the four picked, so any 32 bits cross exactly. Ballot, vote and elect see the invocation alone (its own bit, the predicate, true): exact in non-uniform control flow too, where derivatives are not, and for Maxwell's uses of them (an atomic per lane, each lane its own leader); the masks follow the lane ID. Other stages get one lane, as the Apple chain's `spv_single_lane` (`dmsh_build.py`). The subgroup built-ins and capabilities go. A module with operations it does not cover (bit counts, arithmetic, quad and KHR operations) is made as it is, with a `[RED]` line. `DM_SPV_LIMITS=0` (env.txt) makes every module as translated. `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_limits.py` builds the header for the PC and checks its results with `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`; with `DM_SPV_DIR` it runs over a game's packed shaders, and with `DM_TEST_SERIAL=<adb serial>` it runs `t_quad_shuffle` on that phone.
- **Check:** on the Retroid Pocket 5, the crashing pipeline (`f8d70797a143b99f`, `b2f8ca02b9c1a06c`) with the fragment shader's shuffle made a copy of its own value built, and the crash moved to the next of the 23 (`74f249b01166d890`); with the lane ID taken out and the shuffle kept, it still crashed; with all 23 shuffles taken out the game booted. With the fix, `t_quad_shuffle`'s four shuffles are right in all 4,096 pixels, NaN patterns included. Every module rewritten from the packed shaders passes spirv-val: Brilliant Diamond 23 of 21,688, Animal Crossing 500 (a ballot each), Link's Awakening 5, Final Fantasy Resonance 548 fragment shaders (its 1,199 compute ones are left), Mario Kart World 120. With the Adreno 740 and 830's stages (`0x30`), not one module of Brilliant Diamond, Animal Crossing, Link's Awakening, Super Mario Odyssey, Skyward Sword HD or Smash is touched (none uses subgroup operations outside fragment and compute shaders).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) on a Retroid Pocket 5 ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md), problem 2): it boots on the phone's own driver and reaches Eterna City.

### Draws of billions of instances skipped

- **Symptom:** Android only: the game stops drawing, its sound and vsync going on, and the log has `submission N took 2034–10634 ms on the GPU, its end timestamp never written (the driver's hang recovery cut it short)` with a draw of `… x 1366987687 instances` in the submission's list. It happens in every run, at the same scene.
- **Cause:** the game's own instance count. Final Fantasy Resonance's first field on the Adreno 730 (a Moto Razr Plus 2023) asks for 1 to 3.8 billion instances (0x7993a7d2, 0x7a93a3c2, 0xe0ad0a27, …) of one indexed quad (vertex shader `fb570ef861debd17`, 6 indices) every frame. The GPU works through them until the driver's hang recovery.
  - It's an indirect draw: its arguments are written by a compute shader (`544e80ec57b3a10b`, Unreal's Niagara argument generation), and the replay reads them on the CPU once they land.
  - The same field plays on the Adreno 740 and 830.
  - Not the cause: 32-lane warps in compute shaders (`spv_wide` over compute kept it), the write-back read path (`DM_VK_WB_READBACK=0` kept it), compute shared memory over the GPU's 32 KB (none asks for more), failed pipelines or dropped draws (none).
  - Where the count comes from: the particle simulation's warp reduction, through a shuffle this driver gets wrong: [Maxwell's butterfly shuffle on the Adreno 730](#maxwells-butterfly-shuffle-on-the-adreno-730), fixed by `spv_xor`.
- **Fix, a mitigation:** `vk_draw__` skips a draw of more than 2^24 instances, more than any Switch game draws, with a once-only red line naming its shaders and count. The GPU doesn't hang, and what the draw would have shown is missing. It stays for any other runaway count; this one's cause is fixed by `spv_xor`.
- **Check:** Final Fantasy Resonance's `field` route on the Razr.
  - Without the skip, the GPU hangs as the field appears in every run (5 of 5), with no frame after.
  - With it (the same condition in a diagnostic build), the field plays at 15–17 fps through the route's walk north, with no hang.
- **Found on:** [Final Fantasy Resonance](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) on a Moto Razr Plus 2023 (Adreno 730, Qualcomm's driver V@0615.98).

### Maxwell's 32-lane warps in a wider subgroup

- **Symptom:** Android only, on a GPU whose subgroups have more than 32 lanes (the Adreno 830: 64 in fragment and compute shaders, its only size): in a fragment shader that exchanges values within its 2×2 quad (Maxwell's `fwidth` and fine derivatives through SHFL), the pixels of lanes 32 and up read another quad's value, or keep their own: half of each wave's pixels get a wrong derivative. In a compute shader, lanes 32 and up drop out of a warp's ballot, rank and leader, so a warp-aggregated count or a whole-warp reduction misses the second warp. No `[RED]` line; the log's `subgroup operations in stages 0x30, 64 lanes` names such a GPU.
- **Cause:** the translator (Ryujinx's, in `dmsh_xlate`) runs before the GPU is known, with Ryujinx's default host subgroup size, 32 (`IGpuAccessor.QueryHostSubgroupSize`, which `dmsh_xlate` leaves as it is: Apple's SIMD-groups have 32 lanes). For 32, Ryujinx's SHFL helpers (`HelperFunctionManager.GenerateShuffle*`) take the lane ID as it is, keep its low five bits through the segment mask, call a result valid only up to lane 31 and shuffle to that lane; `S2R SR_LANEID` and the lane masks read the built-ins as they are, BALLOT takes the ballot's first word and VOTE is the whole subgroup's. For a host subgroup over 32 it makes each 32 lanes one warp instead (lane & 31, the shuffle's lane ORed with the lane's own `& 0x60`, the warp's ballot and mask word, VOTE from the warp's ballot), which a 32-lane module never has. So on a 64-lane subgroup a quad's SHFL.IDX in lanes 32-63 reads lanes 0-31, and its SHFL.BFLY is never valid there and keeps the lane's own value. FSWZADD reads the lane ID's low two bits only and is right on any size.
- **Fix:** `sh_module_code` passes each fragment or compute shader that uses subgroup operations through `spv_wide` (`dm/android/spv_wide.h`) when the GPU's `subgroupSize` is over 32 and the shader's stage runs them, and rewrites it into what Ryujinx makes for such a host: each lane ID read is `lane & 31`, each `OpGroupNonUniformShuffle` index is ORed with `lane & ~31`, a ballot's first word and a mask's component 0 are the warp's (`lane >> 5`), all / any / all-equal of a predicate are made from the warp's ballot, and the subgroup size reads 32. That is exact on any subgroup of 32 lanes or more. A module with other subgroup operations, or other uses of the built-ins, is made as it is, with a `[RED]` line. Mesh and task modules are made as translated, and a `[RED]` line names each such stage once: only fragment and compute shaders have a device test so far. `DM_SPV_WIDE_CS=0` makes compute modules as translated too. Modules of stages that run none are `spv_limits`' (above), and a GPU with 32-lane subgroups gets every module as translated. `DM_SPV_WIDE=0` (env.txt) makes every module as translated. The chain is not changed: the Mac and the iPhone keep the translator's 32-lane modules. `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_wide.py` builds the header for the PC and checks its results with `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`; with `DM_SPV_DIR` it runs over a game's packed shaders, and with `DM_TEST_SERIAL=<adb serial>` it runs `t_quad_shuffle` on that phone with a fragment shader whose four quad shuffles are the translator's SHFL helpers, and `t_warp_reduce` with `test_spv_narrow.py`'s compute shader of whole-warp reductions, each rewritten and as translated.
- **Check:** `t_quad_shuffle` on the Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830, subgroup size 64), its 64×64 target: the four quad shuffles as the translator writes them (SHFL.IDX of quad lanes 0 and 3, SHFL.BFLY by 1 and 2, mask `0x1c03`) give 2,048 wrong pixels of 4,096 in each (every pixel of lanes 32-63); rewritten by `spv_wide`, none. `t_warp_reduce` on the Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840, subgroup size 64), one workgroup of 64: as translated, the sum 0x10eaa0 for 0x231e1d and the maximum 0xff33 for 0xff49 (the second warp's butterfly steps keep its own values, and its lane 32 never adds); rewritten, the sum, minimum, maximum and OR all right. `test_spv_narrow.py`'s device test, which expects the translated reductions to pass from 32 lanes up, fails there the same way and now leaves subgroups over 32 to this one.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md): its 23 fragment shaders with Maxwell's `fwidth` shuffles (21,688 modules, all 23 rewritten and valid). [Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md): 5 fragment shaders with a quad SHFL.BFLY (`11fa2d7fcf05704f` and others). Super Mario Odyssey and Skyward Sword HD have none. Across the packed shaders of 10 games the fragment shaders rewritten are also Animal Crossing's 500, Final Fantasy Resonance's 548, Mario Kart World's 120 and Ocarina of Time's 2,797. Compute modules rewritten across the packed shaders on hand: Ocarina of Time 301 and Frostpunk 56, none kept as translated, all valid under spirv-val; Final Fantasy Resonance (1,199) and Mario Kart World (184) also have compute modules with warp instructions. Open: the difference not yet compared in a game frame, and Ocarina of Time's 1,000 mesh and 19 task modules on a GPU that runs subgroup operations in those stages (the Z Fold 8: stages `0xf0`).

### Maxwell's 32-lane warps in a narrower subgroup

- **Symptom:** Android only, on a GPU whose subgroups have fewer than 32 lanes (the Pixel 9 Pro XL's Mali-G715: 16 in fragment and compute shaders, its only size). No red line. A compute shader's whole-warp sum counts each subgroup twice: `t_warp_reduce` as translated gives 0x463c3a for a workgroup whose sum is 0x231e1d, while its minimum, maximum and OR come out right. Final Fantasy Resonance has 309 compute shaders with such reductions (UMax, UMin, Or and IAdd, then lane 0 of each warp combines its result into workgroup memory with an atomic).
- **Cause:** the translator writes SHFL for a 32-lane host ([Maxwell's 32-lane warps in a wider subgroup](#maxwells-32-lane-warps-in-a-wider-subgroup)): the SHFL.BFLY helper's source lane is `lane ^ b`, valid up to the clamp. A whole-warp reduction's last step, `x = op(x, shfl.bfly(x, 16))`, then names lanes 16 to 31, which a 16-lane subgroup does not have. Vulkan leaves that value undefined; the Mali-G715 gives the lane's own, so `op(x, x)`: right for the minimum, maximum and OR, twice the sum for an add. `VK_EXT_subgroup_size_control` cannot ask this GPU for 32 lanes (its minimum and maximum are 16).
- **Fix:** `spv_narrow` (`dm/android/spv_narrow.h`, from `spv_fit` in `sh_module_code` when `subgroupSize` is under 32) makes each subgroup a warp of its own. Every lane ID, ballot and mask the game reads is already the subgroup's, and the game combines its warps' results itself, so what is left is the steps no lane of the subgroup can take: a call of the SHFL.BFLY or SHFL.DOWN helper with a constant offset of the subgroup size or more and mask 31, whose result the instructions after it combine with the value it shuffled, becomes that value (the combine an `OpCopyObject`, whatever its op). Every other shuffle reads the lane's own value where its source lane is past the subgroup, as SHFL does for a lane outside its segment, instead of an undefined one. A step whose result is used otherwise is left as translated, with a red line. `DM_SPV_NARROW=0` makes modules as translated. 32 lanes or more are unchanged.
- **Note:** this is right where a warp's leader is chosen within the subgroup (a lane ID of 0, or a ballot's lowest or highest lane), so that each subgroup's leader adds its own result. Final Fantasy Resonance's leaders are `SubgroupLocalInvocationId == 0`. A game that chose its leader by local invocation index would lose the second subgroup's result.
- **Check:** `t_warp_reduce` on the Pixel 9 Pro XL (`test_spv_narrow.py` with `DM_TEST_SERIAL`): as translated, the sum wrong and the rest right; rewritten, all four right. Run over the packed modules (`DM_SPV_DIR`): Final Fantasy Resonance 1,725 rewritten, all 309 with reduction steps made the value; Mario Kart World 284 (3); Brilliant Diamond 23; Animal Crossing 1; Link's Awakening 5. Every result passes spirv-val wherever the module itself does. Final Fantasy Resonance's opening on the Pixel: 29.8 and 30.2 fps, GPU 23.3 and 23.0 ms with it, 30.2 fps and 23.1 ms without; no difference was found in its screenshots.
- **Found on:** [Final Fantasy Resonance](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) on the Pixel 9 Pro XL ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md#mali-super-mario-odyssey-on-a-pixel-9-pro-xl)).

### Maxwell's butterfly shuffle on the Adreno 730

- **Symptom:** Android only, on the Adreno 730 (Qualcomm's driver V@0615.98, Vulkan 1.1): a compute shader's warp reduction through SHFL.BFLY gives a garbage value, with no red line of its own. In Final Fantasy Resonance's first field, a particle emitter with about 20 live particles added 0x21d1f071 or 0xf6b4131c to its count, so the indirect draw of its sprites asked for billions of instances ([Draws of billions of instances skipped](#draws-of-billions-of-instances-skipped)).
- **Cause:** the translator writes SHFL.BFLY as `OpGroupNonUniformShuffle` to the lane ID ^ the mask (its helper keeps both in Function variables). This driver returns garbage from that shuffle.
  - The emitters with one live particle, whose reduction takes nothing from other lanes, came out right.
  - `OpGroupNonUniformShuffleXor` by the same mask names the same lane and is right on this driver.
  - Not the cause: the 32-lane warps on 64-lane subgroups (`spv_wide` over compute kept it), the views' numeric types, uninitialized variables, and the host's write-backs (`DM_VK_SAFE=1` kept it).
- **Fix:** `spv_fit_xor` (`dm/android/spv_xor.h`, the first step of `spv_fit`) rewrites each shuffle whose index is the lane ID ^ b into `ShuffleXor` by b.
  - The index is followed through bitcasts, copies and Function variables stored once earlier in the same block.
  - It runs on Qualcomm's own driver below Vulkan 1.3, in a stage that runs subgroup operations, with 32 lanes or more. The Adreno 740's and 830's drivers (Vulkan 1.3) run the shuffle as translated.
  - The rewrite is exact on any GPU. `spv_wide` keeps a `ShuffleXor` by a mask under 32 as it is, within its 32-lane warp.
  - Other shuffles (SHFL.IDX, as Brilliant Diamond's quad derivatives use; UP; DOWN) are left as translated.
  - `DM_SPV_XOR=0`: as translated; `=1`: on any GPU of 32 lanes or more.
- **Check:** the `field` route on the Razr, two runs.
  - With the rewrite: 19.9–20.0 fps, GPU 30.5 ms, and the skip never fires.
  - With `DM_SPV_XOR=0`, the same build: 15.9 fps, GPU 38.5 ms, and the skip drops a draw of 427,590,567 instances (0x197c83a7).
  - Over the packed modules (`test_spv_xor.py` with `DM_SPV_DIR`), the rewritten counts: Final Fantasy Resonance 733 of 82,749, Mario Kart World 114, Link's Awakening 5, Animal Crossing 1. Brilliant Diamond, Super Mario Odyssey, Smash, Breath of the Wild and Skyward Sword HD have none. All are valid, and `spv_wide` leaves none of them that it rewrites as translated.
- **Found on:** [Final Fantasy Resonance](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) on a Moto Razr Plus 2023 ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md#adreno-730-a-moto-razr-plus-2023)).

### Layer output from vertex shaders on a GPU that has none

- **Symptom:** Android only: a crash in the driver's shader compiler at every boot, before the first frame, as the first pipelines are built (`CRASH sig=11 addr=0x48 pc=…(libllvm-qgl.so+0xd8a064)`, thread `NVN queue repla`). The pipeline being built names a vertex shader whose module declares `ShaderViewportIndexLayerEXT` and decorates a variable `BuiltIn Layer`; its fragment shader has no subgroup operation and no derivative, unlike [Subgroup operations rewritten for stages the GPU runs none in](#subgroup-operations-rewritten-for-stages-the-gpu-runs-none-in).
- **Cause:** Maxwell's vertex shaders may write the render target layer and the viewport index, and the translator declares them as `BuiltIn Layer` and `ViewportIndex` with the `ShaderViewportIndexLayerEXT` capability and `SPV_EXT_shader_viewport_index_layer`. Vulkan 1.1 allows that only with `VK_EXT_shader_viewport_index_layer` (1.2: `shaderOutputLayer` and `shaderOutputViewportIndex`). The Adreno 730's driver V@0615.98 (Vulkan 1.1.128) has neither, and its compiler dereferences null on such a module. The host never asked for the extension; the newer drivers here (the 650's V@0746, the 740's, the 830's) took the modules anyway.
- **Fix:** on a GPU without the extension that is not Vulkan 1.2 or later, `lm_code` passes each module through `spv_layer` (`dm/android/spv_layer.h`, after `spv_fit`). It makes each of those built-ins an ordinary output at a location after the module's highest, which no fragment shader reads, and drops the capability and extension. Those draws then go to layer 0 and viewport 0 (such a GPU has no `multiViewport` either). `DM_SPV_LAYER=0` (env.txt) makes the modules as translated. A built-in that is a block member is left as it is, with a red line (the translator writes none).
- **Check:**
  - On the Moto Razr Plus 2023, the crashing pipeline (vertex shader `472d0b6794a8a777`, fragment shader `5ec702f7caaf2ff6`) built with its vertex shader's layer output stripped by hand, and the crash moved to the next layer-writing vertex shader (`5f7f16a6d1c85b71`).
  - With all 12 of Brilliant Diamond's layer-writing vertex shaders (and their `.z` variants) stripped, and then with this change and the modules as packed, the game boots: the language menu at 60 fps, Eterna City at 59.9 fps.
  - With `DM_SPV_LAYER=0`, the same crash at the same address.
  - The log counted no layered render pass in those scenes, so nothing drawn changed.
  - `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_layer.py` checks the rewrite against the module the translator would write without the built-ins, and runs it over a game's packed shaders (`DM_SPV_DIR`; each result must pass `spirv-val`).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) on a Moto Razr Plus 2023 (Adreno 730): it boots on the phone's own driver. Mesa's Turnip (`DM_VK_DRIVER`) compiled the same modules as they were, past the crashing pipeline.


### Vertex shaders' FMUL and FADD kept unfused (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only, on the Pixel 9 Pro XL (Mali-G715). No red line. Super Mario Odyssey's Cap Kingdom ground showed hard-edged square blocks, bright and dark, with hatched borders, seen from above at the level's start; where the blocks lie changes from run to run and with the camera.
- **Cause:** the blocks are where the G-buffer pass's ground draws (vertex shader `1ed83b718ff1b4e0`, material `b6384a85afd3ef94`) fail their less-or-equal depth test and leave the previous frame's picture. The depth comes from a depth pre-pass that draws the same four meshes with another vertex shader (`410fba6259254fc0`, colour mask 0) just before.
  - Both compute the same position with the same constants, but add the matrix products in another order (`(r1·c8.y + r0·c8.x) + r2·c8.z` against `(r0·c8.x + r1·c8.y) + r2·c8.z`).
  - The translator writes Maxwell's FMUL and FADD as `OpFMul` and `OpFAdd` without `NoContraction`, so the driver may fuse a multiply into the following add. Which product it fuses differs between the two shaders, and the depths differ in their last bits. `invariant gl_Position` is on both; it doesn't hold when the expressions differ.
  - The Mac had the same blocks until every Metal compile preserved invariance ([Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md)).
  - Not the cause: 16-lane subgroups (the ground's shaders use none), the fog's FFT passes (rounding their butterfly index changed the fog texture but not the blocks), texture formats.
- **Fix:** `spv_fit_precise` (`dm/android/spv_precise.h`, after `spv_fit_layer` in `lm_code`) decorates every `OpFAdd`, `OpFSub`, `OpFMul`, `OpFNegate` and `OpVectorTimesScalar` result `NoContraction` in a module whose entry point is a vertex, tessellation, geometry or mesh stage.
  - That is what Maxwell does: it rounds each FMUL and FADD apart and fuses only where the program says FFMA (kept as `Fma`).
  - On every GPU; fragment and compute shaders are left as translated. `DM_SPV_PRECISE=0`: as translated.
  - Changed modules make new pipelines: the first launch after the change builds them again (Brilliant Diamond about 90 s on the Pixel).
- **Check:** on the Pixel, Odyssey's Cap Kingdom start from `saves/bench_ship`: with the rewrite no block in a warm-up and two runs; with `DM_SPV_PRECISE=0` on the same build the blocks are back. The final picture's hard-edged 8×8 cells: 77 before, 11 after.
  - Frame rate unchanged: Odyssey 60 fps (GPU p50 9.4–9.6 ms against 8.9–9.1), Brilliant Diamond's Eterna City 59.7–59.9 fps (GPU 14.9 ms, as before, the same brightness), Final Fantasy Resonance's field 31.5–32.1 fps (GPU 20.8–21.2 ms).
  - `test_spv_precise.py`, and over Odyssey's packed modules (`DM_SPV_DIR`): 14,819 rewritten, all pass `spirv-val`.
  - Not run on an Adreno or the Xclipse yet.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) on the Pixel 9 Pro XL.
### 16-bit normalized textures a GPU cannot filter

- **Symptom:** Android only, on Samsung's Xclipse 950 (Galaxy Z Flip 7): Brilliant Diamond's pond in Twinleaf Town is drawn as a regular grid of crisp white dashes on pale lavender, where the Pixel 9 Pro XL, the Moto Razr Plus 2023 and the Mac draw calm water with the bank reflected in it. No `[RED]` line. A frame dump shows the water's own pass is not at fault: its 512×512 R16G16_UNORM height field (two textures the game ping-pongs every frame) holds values pinned at 0 and 1 in a checkerboard, where a calm pond sits near 0.5.
- **Cause:** the Xclipse 950 samples R16_UNORM, R16G16_UNORM, R16G16B16A16_UNORM and their SNORM forms but does not filter them linearly (no `VK_FORMAT_FEATURE_SAMPLED_IMAGE_FILTER_LINEAR_BIT`); the Adreno 830 and the Mali-G715 do, and so does Maxwell. Brilliant Diamond's water step (fragment shader `364ea85abf9710bf`) reads each texel's four neighbours 1.5 texels away (its constants `1.5` and the 1/512 texel size), between two texels on purpose, so a linear sampler blends the texels one and two away: the filter is part of the simulation's stencil. Point sampled, which is what a linear sampler gives on the Xclipse for such a format and what Vulkan's NEAREST gives, each read lands on one side of a texel boundary, the stencil is lopsided, and the field diverges into a checkerboard, which the water's normals turn into white highlights. Making those samplers NEAREST, as Vulkan requires for a format without linear filtering, changed nothing on the device.
- **Fix:** `vt_create` makes a sampled 16-bit normalized colour texture (not a storage image, not a linear one, not multisample) in the 16-bit float format of the same channels (`n16_half`, `gfx_vk.c`) where the GPU cannot filter the normalized format linearly but can filter the float one, and can draw to and blend it wherever it could the normalized one: R16_SFLOAT, R16G16_SFLOAT, R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT. Its texels are converted on upload (`bc_decode`'s `CONV_U16H` / `CONV_S16H`, through the path of a BC texture decoded on the CPU) and back on CPU reads (`mtl_read_texels`, `n16_back`); a view in the normalized format is made in the float one (`view_format`). Halves hold the texels to 11 significant bits instead of 16 (round trip within 16 steps of 65,535, exhaustively checked on the Mac). A texture-to-buffer copy of such an image is not made, as one of a BC image decoded on the CPU is not (red line 42). The log says `VkFormat 77 is not filtered linearly on this GPU: its sampled images are made as 83` once per format. A GPU that filters the normalized formats (every Adreno and Mali we have) is unchanged. `DM_VK_N16_HALF=0` (env.txt) makes them in their own formats, as before.
- **Check:** Brilliant Diamond 1.3.0 on the Galaxy Z Flip 7 from a save in Twinleaf (`DM_FIXED_TIME` at 15:30), its host from `main` at `351400c1`: the pond is calm water with the bank's reflection, standing by it and on every return; with `DM_VK_N16_HALF=0` the same build draws the grid of dashes again. The town runs at 60 fps (frame p50 16.7 ms, GPU p50 6–7 ms), no red line. Persona 3 Portable makes none of these formats in its opening, so the fine dot grid once seen in one frame of its movie on the same phone is not this.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md#xclipse-brilliant-diamond-on-a-galaxy-z-flip-7)).

### Geometry kept on the GPU between frames

- **Symptom:** the world map of Super Mario Bros. Wonder at 6 to 15 fps with the camera far out (better close up); the render thread's profile led by `vk_draw`, `bind_vbufs` and `memcpy`.
- **Cause:** every draw copied its indices (widened to 32 bits, their vertex range scanned) and its vertex streams into the arena, and converted attributes the GPU cannot fetch as they are, at every frame, though nearly all of it is the same from frame to frame. With the camera far out the map draws about 1,190 draws a frame reading 2.8 million indices and about 9 MB of vertices: 84 to 118 ms of CPU a frame on an Adreno 619.
- **Fix:** `gc_get` keeps a range of guest memory (an index buffer, a whole vertex buffer, or a vertex buffer's attribute as converted) in a GPU buffer of its own once it was seen unchanged in a later frame, and binds it at the draw's offset while the dirty-page tracker shows no write since its stamp (the stamp is taken before the pages are protected). A range written twice goes back to the per-draw copy and is no longer protected, so a buffer rewritten every frame costs no faults; it is looked at again after 600 presents. Kept 16- and 32-bit indices stay in their own size (Vulkan's restart index is the type's all-ones, as NVN's), halving a 16-bit draw's index reads on the GPU. A kept copy's memory is reused once the chunks that read it have completed. `DM_VK_GEOCACHE=0` turns it off; the `[vk] geometry kept on the GPU` line counts it.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): far map view CPU 84–118 → 40 ms a frame and frame time p50 64–176 → 48 ms; the normal map view about 35 ms (POCO X5, 95 % of the index and vertex bindings from kept copies, 29 MB kept). With 16-bit indices the far view's GPU time went from about 39 to 34–41 ms a frame (it is bound by geometry: a lower render scale barely changed it). Keeping the game's uniform buffers the same way did not help: they change every frame.


### The geometry cache's table walked at every request that found no room

- **Symptom:** Android only, in a game that fills the geometry cache's budget (256 MB): `gc_evict` in the replay thread's profile, under `gc_alloc`, `gc_get` and
  `bind_vbufs` or `sb_keep`. In Mario Kart World's race on a Galaxy S25 Edge it took 1.8 to 8.9 ms of the replay's 34 to 54 ms a frame, from one window of the race to
  the next.
- **Cause:** a copy that finds no room asks `gc_evict` to let go of the copies no draw used in the last 120 presents, and the walk covers the whole table: 35,000 to
  60,000 entries in that race. With the cache full and nothing stale, every one of the 16 to 43 such requests a frame walked the table and freed nothing.
- **Fix:** the walk is made at most once a present, and after one that freed nothing only every 60th present (`gc_evict` returns whether it walked; `gc_alloc` gives up
  at once when it didn't). A request that finds no walk due is copied per draw, as one that found no room after a walk was. `DM_VK_GC_EVICT_PACE=0` (env.txt): a walk
  at every request, as before. The `[vk] geometry kept on the GPU` line counts the walks, the copies they let go and the requests that found none due.
- **Check:** Mario Kart World's race on the S25 Edge, the replay thread profiled: `gc_evict` at most 0.2 ms a frame in two races with the pacing (31 walks let 9,387 copies go,
  29,886 requests found none due), against 1.8 to 8.9 ms a frame in four races without.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md).

### Read-only storage buffers copied into every chunk

- **Symptom:** Android only: a game whose shaders read large storage buffers spends a share of the replay thread in `memcmp` and `memcpy` under `stage_set`
  (`ucopy`), and the `constant and storage buffer copies` line counts megabytes copied to the arena (and as many again to the shadows) every frame.
- **Cause:** a storage buffer a shader only reads was handled as a uniform buffer is (`ucopy`): copied into the arena once in every chunk that read it, with a host
  shadow of it, and compared with that shadow at every other draw of the chunk that bound it. A chunk is 48 to 64 draws, so a buffer read across the frame was
  copied several times a frame, though its bytes rarely change: Super Smash Bros. Ultimate's battles read about 44 storage buffers a frame (2.4 to 2.7 MB, tens to
  hundreds of KB each), and every one seen in an earlier frame was the same, byte for byte.
- **Fix:** `sb_keep` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`) keeps such a buffer as the [geometry above](#geometry-kept-on-the-gpu-between-frames) is (`gc_get`, its raw bytes, so a
  buffer also read as a vertex stream has one copy; the kept blocks carry storage usage too): once seen unchanged in a later frame it is bound from its kept copy
  while the dirty-page tracker shows no write since its stamp, and a write sends it back to the copy. Its pending writes were dealt with before it is looked up:
  `draw_view` lands them, and `sb_fwd` binds a dispatch's pending write on the GPU. The `[vk] geometry kept on the GPU` line counts its bindings apart.
  `DM_VK_SB_KEEP=0` (env.txt): copied in every chunk, as before.
- **Check:** Super Smash Bros. Ultimate's `battle` route on a Retroid Pocket 5 (Adreno 650, V@0746.0). A test build comparing every kept copy it bound again with
  guest memory found no difference in 55,000 bindings (23 GB). The arena copies fell from 2.1–2.2 MB a frame to 0.22–0.23 MB (26 to 34 storage bindings a frame
  from kept copies). A test build that turned the keeping on and off every 300 presents, in two battles (each on one stage): the replay thread took 21.2 to 23.4 µs a
  draw with it against 24.9 to 31.7 without (means 22.2 and 29.4), about 1.2 ms a frame at 200 draws; both stages held 60 fps either way. On a Galaxy S25 Edge
  (Adreno 830), the same on-and-off build in one battle: 27.8 to 31.3 µs a draw with it against 32.0 to 41.1 without (means 29.6 and 36.2); battles at 59.8 and
  60.0 fps with it and 59.9 and 59.9 without, no fault, the picture the same.
- **Found on:** [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) ([Android baselines](../../dm/android/baselines.md)).

### Read-only storage buffers compared at every bind

- **Symptom:** Android only: the replay thread spends most of its time in `memcmp` under `ucopy`, called from `stage_set`'s read-only storage buffer line, and the
  `constant and storage buffer copies` line counts hundreds of megabytes a frame copied to the arena and as many to the shadows.
- **Cause:** a read-only storage buffer that [`sb_keep`](#read-only-storage-buffers-copied-into-every-chunk) does not keep (past 8 MB, or written every frame by the game
