  or a write-back) and that no placed pool holds went to `ucopy`. It was copied into the arena once per chunk, with a host shadow, and compared whole with that shadow
  at every later bind in the chunk. Mario Kart World binds one 9.56 MB buffer about 370 times a frame in a race: on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830) those compares were
  228 ms of the replay thread's 280 ms a frame (81%), about 3.5 to 4.5 GB a frame, and the two copies 29 ms more.
- **Fix:** `sb_copy` (`gfx_vk_sbcopy.inc`, its reuse table in `sb_reuse.h`) takes the dirty-page tracker's word instead. The copy is stamped and its pages protected
  before it is made, and a later bind in the same chunk takes it while no page of it has a generation past the stamp:
  - the game's writes fault and move a page's generation;
  - so do the replay's own writes while it records (uniform buffer updates, small copies and fills), which fault the same way;
  - write-backs and linear render targets landing on the completion thread go through `dm_guest_write_prepare`, which moves it too;
  - a dispatch's write still on the GPU is bound there first (`sb_fwd`), as before.

  Memory no tracked pool holds whole is copied and compared as before. No shadow is kept. The `[vk] read-only storage buffers outside placed pools` line counts
  binds that took their chunk's copy, copies made and those made after a write. `DM_VK_SB_DIRTY=0` (env.txt): compared at every bind, as before.
  `DM_VK_SB_CHECK=1` keeps the shadow as well and compares every bind the tracker let through, logging any difference.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_sb_reuse.py` drives the reuse table with a fake tracker that stamps a page only on a write while it is protected, as `nvn_dirty.c`
  does. It checks that a second bind in the chunk takes the copy, that the copy protects every page it read, that a write since the copy makes a new one (and the one
  after it reuses that), that a write elsewhere, a new chunk, another size and untracked memory each do what they should.
  - Mario Kart World on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830), one build, `DM_VK_SB_DIRTY=0` against the default:
    - the race went from frames of 216–312 ms (the replay's CPU 200–294 ms at 1,625–2,443 draws) to 47–65 ms (36–52 ms at 1,017–1,889 draws);
    - the attract drive and title from 181–220 ms to 41–47 ms;
    - `DM_VK_SB_CHECK=1` over the attract drive, the menus and a race compared 1,840,757 binds with the shadow and found none different.
  - Super Mario Odyssey's opening (from the library, on a host with the change) ran 16.6–16.7 ms a frame either way, the picture the same.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md).

### Texture and sampler banks packed in one `cbuf_slot`

- **Symptom:** Android only: a black picture while the game runs at full rate with no draw dropped; every render target of a pass dump is all zero, and the dump's binding lines show `tid 0 sid 0 -> none` for every texture.
- **Cause:** the translator writes Ryujinx's `TextureHandle.PackSlots` as a texture's `cbuf_slot`: the texture handle's bank in the low 16 bits, the sampler handle's bank + 1 in the high ones. A separate texture with a separate sampler (handle type 1, Unity's) carries `0x30002`, both in c2. The Android replay passed the packed value to `bank_word` as one bank number, which names no bank, so every handle read 0. A plain bank (no high bits) read as before, so games without separate samplers were unaffected.
- **Fix:** `rb_tex_read` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`) unpacks it as `UnpackSlots` does: `cbuf_slot` the texture's bank, `samp_slot` the sampler's, which the sampler handle of a type 1 texture is read from.
- **Found on:** Danganronpa V3: Killing Harmony Anniversary Edition (010063F014176000, provisional profile, Unity, SDK 10), on a POCO X5 5G (Adreno 619).

### A 3D render target drawn one slice at a time

- **Symptom:** Android only: the 3D scene turns blue (red and green almost gone) after the tone-mapping pass, while the G-buffer and the HDR lighting in a pass dump look right; the pass reads a small 3D texture (an 8×8×8 R11G11B10F colour-grading LUT).
- **Cause:** the game renders the LUT a slice at a time, each slice a render target (the view's layer is the depth slice). `view_get` made any view of a 3D image a 3D view of the whole volume, and reset the slice to 0: Vulkan takes only 2D views as attachments, and every slice was drawn over the first.
- **Fix:** 3D images that can be colour attachments are created `VK_IMAGE_CREATE_2D_ARRAY_COMPATIBLE_BIT` (`gfx_vk.c`), and `rp_start` asks `view_get` (`g_view_slice`) for a 2D view of the target's slice (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`). The pass dump copies such a slice by its depth offset. Open: a clear outside a render pass (`vkCmdClearColorImage`) still clears every slice of a 3D image.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md) (Bowser's Fury), on a POCO X5 5G (Adreno 619).

### Layered draws get every layer of their targets

- **Symptom:** Android only: the 3D scene comes out as white specks and edges on black after the tone-mapping pass, while the G-buffer and the HDR scene colour in a pass dump look right. The 3D LUT the tone mapper reads holds values that follow no colour ramp.
- **Cause:** the game draws a volume texture in one pass: one instance per slice, and a geometry shader (or a vertex shader) writes the layer. `rp_prepare` attached every target a layer at a time: a 2D view of the view's first slice and a framebuffer of one layer. Every write to another layer was lost, and the LUT's other slices kept whatever their memory held.
- **Fix:**
  - `sh_get` marks a vertex, tessellation or geometry shader whose SPIR-V decorates an output `BuiltIn Layer` (`spv_layer_out`, `vshader.wlayer`).
  - A draw with such a stage asks `rp_prepare_` for a layered pass. It attaches each 3D or array target whole, as a 2D array view of the slices or layers its view covers (all of them from its first, without a layer range), and the framebuffer gets that many layers.
  - A layered pass serves draws that write no layer too (they write layer 0, as before), so only a layered draw that meets a pass of one layer ends it and starts it again.
  - A view of one layer stays that layer, as in [A 3D render target drawn one slice at a time](#a-3d-render-target-drawn-one-slice-at-a-time).
  - The `[vk] render passes with every layer of their targets attached` line counts them. `DM_VK_LAYERED=0` attaches one layer, as before.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md)'s first field, on an Ayn Odin 2 and a OnePlus Pad 3.
  - Unreal's slice geometry shader (`e2f17523c090feaf`, `gl_Layer` from the vertex shader's instance-based slice index) writes the 32×32×32 A2B10G10R10 colour-grading LUT. Two pairs of 32×32 RGBA16F volume targets in each frame (32 instances per draw) are drawn the same way.
  - With the fix the field shows its lit, burning road, as the game's own tutorial picture of it does.

### A vertex shader's layer from 8 on, on Arm's Mali (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android only, on Arm's Mali (the Pixel 9 Pro XL's Mali-G715): the 3D scene comes out nearly black after the tone-mapping pass, with only dim outlines of the characters, while the G-buffer and the lit HDR scene in a pass dump look right. The 3D LUT the tone mapper reads has some slices written and the rest zero. No red line.
- **Cause:** Arm's driver drops every primitive whose layer comes from a vertex shader and is 8 or more, whatever the framebuffer's layer count (`maxFramebufferLayers` is 256) and whether the image is 3D or an array; a negative layer is dropped too. A geometry shader's layer reaches every layer. `dm/android/tests/t_vs_layers.c` shows it: one draw of 32 instances, each writing `gl_Layer` = instance into 32 layers, fills layers 0-7 on the Pixel's Mali-G715 (driver 0xd803000, with or without `shaderOutputLayer` enabled on a Vulkan 1.2 instance, `ShaderViewportIndexLayerEXT` or SPIR-V 1.5's `ShaderLayer`) and all 32 on an Ayn Odin 2's Adreno 740. So of a LUT drawn one instance per slice ([Layered draws get every layer of their targets](#layered-draws-get-every-layer-of-their-targets)), only the slices below 8 were drawn.
- **Fix:** on a GPU whose vertex shaders reach only the first `g_vs_layers` layers (8 on Arm's proprietary driver, by its driver ID; 0 elsewhere), a draw whose vertex shader writes the layer, into a layered pass of more layers, is made once per 8 layers (`gfx_vk_draw.inc` `vs_layer_groups`):
  - `lm_code` passes each module through `spv_layer_base` (`dm/android/spv_layer.h`): a vertex shader stores its layer less the int at byte 16 of the push constant block, and a fragment shader that reads the layer adds it back (the game's LUT shader computes its slice's colour from it). `sh_get` marks such shaders (`vshader.lbpc`, `lbase`), their pipelines' layouts take a push constant range at byte 16 for those stages, and every draw with such a pipeline sets the base, 0 by default.
  - After the draw as made (layers 0-7), the draw is made again for each later group of 8, each in a pass of its own whose views start at the group's first layer (`rp_prepare_` `g_lbase`), with that base. A primitive then lands only in its own group's pass, and the next draw begins its pass from layer 0 again. The pass's bindings stay bound across those passes (one command buffer).
  - `DM_VK_VS_LAYERS=<n>` (env.txt) sets the limit on any GPU, `0` makes every draw once as before; `DM_SPV_LBASE=0` makes the modules as translated. The first such draw is logged (`made again for each later 8`).
  - A fragment shader's alpha-test variant (`spv_alpha.h`, which has its own push constant) keeps its layer read as it is.
- **Check (2026-11-13):** `dm/tools/tests/test_spv_layer.py` (`spirv-val` on the rewritten modules; with `DM_SPV_DIR`, over a game's packed shaders: Z-A's 25 modules that write or read the layer all pass). `t_vs_layers` on the Pixel: one draw reaches 8 of 32 layers, the per-group way 32; on the Odin 2 both reach 32 (and there too a layer past the last or below 0 is dropped).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md#android) on the Pixel 9 Pro XL: its 32×32×32 A2B10G10R10 colour-grading LUT (`b29f08619f379a83`, one instance per slice, the layer = instance + 1; fragment shader `5b931243ad28aeeb`, which reads `gl_Layer`) had slices 1-7 drawn, and the Gare de Lumiose platform was nearly black at 20 fps. With the fix it is lit as on the Odin 2, at the same frame rate.

### A shader's own constants (c1) read after the control section is gone

- **Symptom:** Android only: a movie plays black (its sound runs) and the game's picture is fine before and after it; in a pass dump the decoded luma and chroma planes hold the picture, but the YUV-to-RGB pass that reads them writes all zero.
- **Cause:** the conversion shader takes its colour matrix from c1, the constant segment in its own code area, whose bounds are in the control section (+0x700 start, +0x704 end). The control section is only guaranteed valid during `nvnProgramSetShaders`, which is why the keys are computed there; the Android replay read the bounds again at each draw, when the game had already reused that memory (0..0), so c1 was not bound and read 0. The Mac takes c1 from the shader's reflection instead.
- **Fix:** `nvnProgramSetShaders` keeps each stage's c1 bounds beside its keys, and `stage_set` (`gfx_vk_draw.inc`) takes them with `prog_c1` (`hle_nvn.c`).
- **Found on:** Danganronpa V3: Killing Harmony Anniversary Edition (010063F014176000, provisional profile, Unity, SDK 10), its H.264 movies, on a POCO X5 5G (Adreno 619).

### A texture's first content from storage the CPU flushed

- **Symptom:** Android only: a texture the game fills on the CPU reads as zeros on the GPU; Breath of the Wild's sky brownish or murky olive and its distant haze dark instead of blue.
- **Cause:** a texture whose storage the CPU writes (not packaged, not linear) was made as an image of zeros. Until the GPU first uses it the GPU has not written it, so what its storage holds is its content when the CPU cannot write that pool (CPU_NO_ACCESS: filled from files), cannot cache it (CPU_UNCACHED), or flushed part of it before that first use (`nvnMemoryPoolFlushMappedRange`, recorded by `nvn_flushed` in `hle_nvn.c`).
- **Fix:** `tex_init_fill` (`gfx_vk.c`, as `nvn_mtl.m` does on Metal) uploads a new image's storage once in those cases, with no write tracking (`vt_upload(…, 0)`); `DM_NVN_FLUSH_FILL=0` turns the flushed case off. `DM_VK_FILL_CHECK=1` (`env.txt`) reads each filled image back at once and logs how many bytes landed.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): `System/KSys/sky.skybin`'s 256×32×16 RGBA16F in-scattering table, copied into its own CPU_CACHED pool with `memcpy` and flushed (781,567 of 1,048,576 bytes non-zero), on an Ayn Odin 2. The sky turned blue only together with the fix for compressed render targets on Vulkan (a separate change): until then, the submission holding this upload was one the GPU faulted on and the kernel driver dropped.

### Image stores into an attachment of their own pass

- **Symptom:** an image-only fragment draw's writes disappear from the subresource also bound as its color attachment when the pass ends; other image layers retain their writes.
- **Cause:** a writable image or storage alias marked every attachment written, which retained the tile store. For a loaded color attachment with no raster output or clear, that store writes the old tile contents over the shader's image writes to the same texture. Disabling the unwritten-store optimization did not avoid this correctness fault.
- **Fix (resolved in synthetic GPU tests; game validation open):** track possible shader writes separately from color-tile writes. A loaded color attachment with possible image/storage writes and no tile writes uses `DontCare` at pass end, independently of the optimization switches. Record physical color writes before resetting a merged pipeline's logical-output map; specialized fragments have conservative unknown outputs. Clear load actions and explicit clears executed in the current pass count as tile writes. A clear already landed in a standalone pass does not: marking it as a tile write retained a stale store over the following image-only draw (found while landing #808). Depth/stencil and non-loaded attachments retain their existing decisions. Mixed raster/clear and image writes to the same subresource are outside this proof and still retain the tile store.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_image_attachment_store` executes literal shaders through production draw replay and reads all four image layers on the GPU, with the unwritten-store optimization on and off. It also checks raster outputs, an earlier raster draw followed by an image-only draw, pending and explicit clears, a standalone clear before an image-only draw, unknown output reflection, a zero channel mask, a native `DontCare`-load control, and physical/logical output mapping. The standalone-clear case failed four checks before tracking the actual clear load action; all 120 checks pass with it. `t_unwritten_store` additionally preserves depth writes with a possible image alias.
- **Found on:** [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md#rendering), reported and diagnosed by Jacob-Tate in [#187](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/187#issuecomment-6519569185), with the original store fix in [34c7453](https://github.com/Jacob-Tate/nxnative/commit/34c7453e081203627752edd877dde40452afe7b2). Independently reproduced with synthetic shaders on an Apple M2 Max, macOS 26.6; no local game or iOS validation.

### Unwritten attachment stores without lookahead

- **Symptom:** read-only depth/stencil attachments are written back after every pass, and the periodic unwritten-store savings counter remains zero with the default configuration.
- **Cause:** `udc_note_draw` and `sd_finish` returned early when `DM_NVN_TBDR` was off. This also disabled the local unwritten-attachment proof, although it does not depend on future sampled-texture readers. The broader lookahead remains disabled because it does not understand bindless readers.
- **Fix:** collect per-pass write facts independently of the lookahead switch and omit only stores of attachments that were loaded and provably unchanged. Clears, depth writes, stencil writes and possible writable image/atomic/storage aliases conservatively retain stores, except for the mandatory [image-only color-attachment rule](#image-stores-into-an-attachment-of-their-own-pass). Lookahead, pass merging and cross-submission invalidation remain disabled. `DM_NVN_UNWRITTEN_DC=0` disables optional unwritten-store savings; the image-write preservation rule stays active.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_unwritten_store` performs real GPU readback against a store-all control and literal color/depth/stencil values, with lookahead disabled. It covers read-only depth/stencil, depth writes, stencil writes, pending clears, and writable images (including native image atomics) and both storage-buffer binding forms. `t_unwritten_store --bench` runs a synthetic 32-pass 1080p workload with balanced on/off order; it is not a game FPS measurement.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata US 1.0.3](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#factory-combat-capture-and-9s-texture-follow-up), during iPhone factory-combat performance investigation. The change passes the GPU proof and rendering regressions on Mac; iPhone gameplay benefit remains to be measured.

### iOS layouts: portrait, a half-folded Duo, iPad windows

- **Symptom:** portrait gameplay overlaps the touch controls, touch coordinates land outside the game picture, or a TV's picture renders at 4K again after a layout pass.
- **Cause:** the front end assumed a landscape screen with the controls over the whole view, so its picture and touch geometry did not follow a portrait screen (an iPad, an iPhone Duo's inner display) or a Duo's fold. On iPadOS the deprecated `UIRequiresFullScreen` kept the game out of resizable windows, and a native rotation A/B on the iPhone Duo iOS 27.1 simulator showed that it also blocked upside-down portrait.
- **Fix:** `dm/ios/Sources/DmLayout.h` computes the picture and controls rectangles from the view's bounds, its safe area and UIKit's division region (`UIHingeInteraction`, iOS 27.1); front_ios.m lays the views out from them and DmGamepad.m its controls. Landscape keeps the overlay unless the hinge is half open over a horizontal division; portrait puts a fitted 16:9 picture above the controls, at the division or the midpoint. On a Duo in portrait only the top safe-area inset applies. Game touches map through the picture rectangle (with [Fill](../settings.md#picture), larger than the view), and a layout change releases held inputs. The phone's orientation list stays landscape (the Duo's inner display follows its bounds), the iPad's has all four and no `UIRequiresFullScreen`. While a TV shows the picture the phone is all controls (`dm_layout_pad_only`). The drawable's scale is set in one place, `DmGameView layoutSubviews`: the screen's pixels, at most `maxPixelWidth` across, which the TV sets to 1920, so a later layout pass cannot undo the TV's 1080p cap.
- **Check:** the [Duo harness](../../dm/ios/tests/duo/README.md) tests layout, hit targets, touches, the in-game menu's live settings and the drawable cap in the Simulator with synthetic host state. A physical Duo was tested by the PR #683 author on their fork; full-game tests on a physical iPad and a Duo with this repository's build are open.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md).

### The picture laid out in the shape the game presents

- **Symptom:** with *Picture shape* at 1.43:1 (`DM_SCREEN_ASPECT`) the game renders 1920x1344, but on an iPhone Duo in Fit (the default) the picture comes out smaller than the 16:9 one, with black bars on all four sides; it fills the screen only in Fill. On the Mac a click lands off the point under the pointer (it was mapped on a 16:9 rectangle of the window).
- **Cause:** the front ends assumed a 16:9 picture. iOS sized the game's view to the largest 16:9 rectangle (`DmLayout.h` `dm_aspect_fit`, `dm_aspect_fill`) and the present then fitted the 1.43:1 frame inside that 16:9 drawable (`present_viewport`), so the picture was fitted twice; the Mac's mouse-to-touch mapping (`front_mac.m`) used the same 16:9 rectangle.
- **Fix:** the present reports the shape of the frame it draws ([the layer's](#a-crop-only-picks-texels-the-picture-has-the-layers-shape)) whenever its width / height changes (`nvn_mtl.m` `present_now` → `front.h` `front_picture_shape`; every graphics API presents there). The iOS front end lays the game's view out in that shape (`DmLayout.h` takes it as a parameter: the overlay, the split, Fill and the TV alike), so a 1.43:1 picture fills a 1.43:1 screen in Fit and touches are normalized on the picture as drawn; the Mac maps the mouse on the picture's own rectangle. A 16:9 game reports 16:9 and keeps its layout, and a size change of the same shape (1280x720 handheld, 1920x1080 docked) is not reported.
- **Check:** the [Duo harness](../../dm/ios/tests/duo/README.md) tests the taller picture in Fit, in Fill and on a wider screen, and the production root view following a reported shape and returning to 16:9. Not yet run on a device.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#screen-shape-dm_screen_aspect-current) and [Animal Crossing: New Horizons](../../dm/games/01006F8002326000/README.md#screen-shape-dm_screen_aspect-current) with `DM_SCREEN_ASPECT=1.43`.

### Task and mesh draws in compute form, on a GPU without mesh shaders (Android)

- **Symptom:** Android, on a GPU whose driver has no `VK_EXT_mesh_shader` (the stock Adreno 830 driver of a OnePlus Pad 3; the Adreno 7xx drivers and Turnip; Samsung's Xclipse 950; Mali), `[RED] [vk] task / mesh shader … on a GPU without VK_EXT_mesh_shader` and every NVN2 `DrawMeshTasks` skipped: Ocarina of Time's depth prepass, main pass and foliage were all missing there (Kokiri Forest without its grass, flowers, ferns and tree canopies).
- **Fix:** such a draw runs in compute form (`gfx_vk_meshemu.inc`), from modules `spv_meshemu.py` makes of the same translated kernels the mesh path wraps (`<key>.cs.spv`, a mesh shader's `<key>.pvs.spv` and `.pvs.z.spv`, `res.json` `mesh_emu`):
  - **Task kernel:** compute, the draw's workgroups from its first; each writes its payload to a buffer.
  - **Launch list:** `shaders/meshlaunch.comp`, one workgroup, sums the payloads' launch counts into a list of {task, number among its launches} and writes the next steps' indirect arguments.
  - **Mesh kernel:** compute, dispatched indirectly; each workgroup writes its records (a vec4 per output) and its 32-bit indices, a primitive past the count the shader set made degenerate.
  - **Draw:** the program's fragment shader under the mesh shader's pass-through vertex shader (record `gl_VertexIndex`), indexed and indirect. Without a task shader the first two steps are left out and both the dispatch and the draw are direct.
  - **Per-primitive outputs:** a mesh shader that writes some (Ocarina of Time's HUD) gets a record per primitive corner instead of per vertex, with its primitive's outputs in it, so its fragment shader reads them as ordinary inputs that are the same at all three corners.
  - **Memory:** one GPU-only arena that every draw uses from its start: each step ends in a full barrier and a draw's pass ends before the next draw's dispatches. A draw larger than it goes in parts. `DM_VK_MESH_EMU=0` skips the draws as before; `DM_VK_MESH_ARENA_MB`, `DM_VK_MESH_MAX`.
  - **A kernel is a compute shader to the replay:** its module is a lazy module that goes through the rewrites a compute shader's does (`lm_code`): the pool set, the subgroup rewrites, shared memory past the GPU's limit moved into a storage buffer (for a kernel a range of the compute form's own arena, scratch for the one dispatch; no kernel of Ocarina of Time or Mario Kart World is past the Adreno 830's 32 KB), and its set's binding numbers made dense with the layout's. The emulation's own buffers are set 1's bindings 100 to 103, below the first dense number. The fragment shader under a pass-through vertex shader is in an ordinary pipeline, so the game's alpha test runs.
  - **Kernel pipelines:** a large kernel (300 to 560 KB of SPIR-V) takes Samsung's Xclipse compiler 2 to 11 s and Qualcomm's 1 to 3 s, and Kokiri Forest's first frame made 31 at once, 50 s on a Galaxy Z Flip 7. So a kernel the driver's cache has is taken from it at once (by its module's identifier); one that needs a compile is built in the background, on the threads that build graphics pipelines met for the first time, and its draws are left out until it exists. On a target a graphics pipeline builds at once for (one not drawn every frame) the replay waits for the kernels, all together at most 0.5 s a present. A compile over 0.25 s asks for the driver's cache file to be written, so a start that is stopped does not make them again. A kernel the driver refuses counts in the start record (`vk_start.txt`), as a compute pipeline does.
  - **With the command recorder** (`DM_VK_RECORDER=1`, Ocarina of Time's default): the indirect dispatch and the indirect draw are records of the recorder's, as every other command of the replay is (`gfx_vk_rec.inc` `R_DISPIND`, `R_DRAWIDXIND`). Made on the replay thread, they went into the command buffer ahead of the binds queued for them, beside the recorder's own calls. `test_rec_desc` checks that every command the loader has is redirected.
- **Cost:** every task / mesh draw ends the render pass for its dispatches, and the pass is begun again with its targets loaded; the kernels' dispatches are most of the GPU time it adds (6.7 of 7.6 ms in a profiled frame on the Adreno 830, the 57 extra passes 0.9 ms). Kokiri Forest, the driver's cache warm, against the draws skipped:
  - **OnePlus Pad 3** (Adreno 830, Qualcomm's driver): 17.5 fps against 19.7 (frame 56 against 50 ms, GPU 38 against 34 ms, replay 37 against 32 ms).
  - **Galaxy Z Flip 7** (Xclipse 950): 11.6 fps against 13.0 (frame 89 against 78 ms, GPU 64 against 51 ms, replay 54 against 49 ms).
  - **A start with an empty driver cache** on the Pad 3 spends 50 s in frames over 0.4 s against 43 s with the draws skipped: the stalls are the game's own pipelines, and the kernels' 22 s of compiles run in the background.
- **Vegetation left out (a game setting, off by default):** the launcher's **Fewer plants (faster)**, offered for a game whose `android_defaults.txt` carries `DM_VK_MESH_LEAVE_OUT` (`=0` there), sets `DM_VK_MESH_LEAVE_OUT=1`: the task / mesh draws whose mesh shader is one of the game profile's `mesh_leave_out` keys are left out (`=<key>[,<key>…]` in env.txt names keys itself), unless a stage of theirs writes guest memory (a storage buffer or image: its compute producers and their readers stay). Kokiri Forest's ground cover and tree canopies are seven mesh pairs (a depth prepass and a colour mesh each: colour `eed1add7c89f5d34`, `73dc0b90c4082e43`, `17c8ab6419068d4b`, `ecead5cd0569091f`, `604134a5262ff670`, `fed302d791a4ba43`, `4c7d6be5a7717c8d`; prepass `0c1ffecd02018fb0`, `25c4a740753e7d7a`, `101ecb327aafe9b1`, `5b737961ae3e1b10`, `eebdb9347d09b344`, `d98076e4148db34f`, `839241636fce850a`), about 14 draws a frame with no task shader. Left out on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830 at 1,100 MHz, natural pacing): 29.1 → 30.0 fps, GPU 27.0 → 24.2 ms a frame (the mesh kernels 5.1 → 3.0 ms, 14 fewer pass pieces). The picture pays: the ferns and flowers around Link go, and the big trees' canopies are bare branches (the game note's pictures), so it stays off until the player turns it on. The list and the idea are nebadasSwifty's (Banana Native #167, its "Full vegetation" setting, MIT), the switch written anew here.
- **Mario Kart World:** its 26 task and mesh shaders get compute forms too. On a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830) 18 of them ran through the title, the menus and a Mushroom Cup race with no failure, 85 draws a frame at the start grid, for a few ms of GPU. Nothing they draw shows in the picture there: the grass draws go to a 128x128 target, the 68 bush draws to a half-resolution screen-space target, and the game's terrain is black on Android with or without them. Read back from the GPU, the bush tasks launch no mesh workgroup at the start grid and 13 to 69 a frame along the course, and a launched workgroup's records are one small cluster near the horizon, none of them NaN.
- **Build:** `spv_build.py` translates again a task or mesh output that has no `mesh_emu` in its `res.json`, alone, so a build made before this gets the compute forms without a chain change. All 520 of Ocarina of Time's (20 task, 500 mesh shaders, 4 of them with per-primitive outputs) are made, and spirv-val passes every module.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on a OnePlus Pad 3 (Adreno 830, Qualcomm's driver); drawn there and on a Galaxy Z Flip 7 (Xclipse 950). The same design as DeathKhan's nx2android fork's mesh emulation (github.com/DeathKhan/nx2android: 1f7e70b, 0e3285b, defd439; `shader_meshwrap.py`, `meshlaunch.comp`), written anew for this replay.

### The compute form's kernels were bound by their shared memory (Android)

- **Symptom:** Ocarina of Time's foliage in compute form costs 5 to 10 ms of GPU a frame in Kokiri Forest (a OnePlus Pad 3 5.1 ms of mesh kernels at 1,100 MHz, a Galaxy S25 Edge or Z Fold 8 up to 10 at the clock their governors reach), two mesh kernels of it a third: the grass `eb021183bf9b0092` (1.7 ms, 2,621 workgroups) and `fed302d791a4ba43` (1.1 ms, 532).
- **Cause:** not the kernels' arithmetic (about 3,000 operations a thread) but their stage memory: 11.4 and 13.1 KB of shared memory a workgroup of 32 threads, so few workgroups fit a shader core at once and the raw global memory reads (61 pool-table lookups a thread in the grass kernel) are not hidden. Declaring the shared array twice as large, and nothing else, took the grass kernel from 1.7 to 3.8 ms; leaving the body out left 0.3 ms. Most of the memory is the payload copied in from the task (3.2 KB) and the attribute blocks (8 KB), which the kernel only wrote so that the wrapper could copy them out.
- **Fix:** `spv_meshemu.py` reads the translator's addresses. It writes every attribute store and every payload read as `(register + C) >> 2` with the area's offset in `C` (`InstEmitAmpereMem.cs` `AStageOut`, `AIsberd`), so the compute form sorts each access by area and rewrites the kernel's functions: an attribute store goes straight to `vtx`, at the same word the stage memory's block layout gave it (vertex `v`'s component `k` at `(v / 32) * nvc * 32 + k * 32 + v % 32` within the workgroup's blocks), a payload read straight to the task's payload in `taskout` (word 0 is the launch number), and the shared array keeps the header and the primitive bytes alone: 29 words for the grass kernel. The pass-through vertex shader reads the blocks the same way, one word a component the SPH enables, and gives the constants for the rest (what the hardware gives, `(0, 0, 0, 1)`); nothing is zeroed or copied after the body. A kernel whose addresses do not all sort keeps the stage memory whole in shared memory and copies its blocks out as before: 14 of Ocarina of Time's 520 (the 4 HUD meshes with per-primitive outputs, whose records are corners; a task shader that reads its payload back; 9 with register-only addresses). `res.json` `mesh_emu` gains `direct`, `recw` and `pack` (1), and its version is 3: `spv_build.py` translates the task and mesh shaders again, alone, so a game's other shaders keep their chain.
  - **Packed workgroups** (`compute(..., pack=2)`, off by default): two of the shader's 32-thread workgroups side by side in one of 64, an Adreno wave, each with its own stage memory, lane, ballots, shuffles and votes; the host dispatches `ceil(count / pack)` and passes the count (`gfx_vk_meshemu.inc`, `meshlaunch.comp` word 3). Measured in the same PR.
- **Cost:** Kokiri Forest, fresh shaders, `prof_now` of one frame (the clock from `power/gpu_frequency`):
  - **OnePlus Pad 3** (Adreno 830, 1,100 MHz): the grass kernel 1.73 to 0.75 ms, `fed302d791a4ba43` 1.09 to 0.56, the grass task kernel 0.35 to 0.2; with every kernel direct the mesh kernels' sum 5.1 to 3.3 ms (the task kernels 0.6 to 0.5), the frame 27.0 to 29.4 fps at GPU 26.8 to 24.6 ms. The picture is the same (the ferns, flowers, grass and canopies in both; the screenshots differ by the scene's own motion).
  - **Galaxy S25 Edge** (Adreno 830, Samsung's driver, its governor at 607 MHz in both runs): the grass kernel 2.98 to 1.31 ms, `fed302d791a4ba43` 1.89 to 0.93, the mesh kernels' sum 8.6 to 5.4 (the tasks 1.0 to 0.7), the frame 17.3 to 19.0 fps at GPU 45.3 to 41.6 ms; still three vsyncs a frame (the frame step's hold). The first launch after the change compiles the 41 kernels in the background for 35 s (Samsung's compiler), with their draws left out meanwhile, as after any shader update.
  - **Galaxy Z Fold 8** (Adreno 840, its clock falling from run to run): the grass kernel 1.31 ms at 646 MHz to 0.94 at 578, `fed302d791a4ba43` 0.85 to 0.71, the mesh kernels' sum 5.5 to 4.5 ms; as a share of the game's own dispatches in the same frame the kernels go from 0.60 to 0.48, about a quarter less, against 38 % less on the two Adreno 830s: the 840 was less bound by its local memory. The frame's gain there is within the clock's noise (20.2 fps at 646 MHz, 19.5 at 578, 18.6 at 500).
  - **Unwritten words:** the stage memory was all zero when the body started, so a task that leaves payload words unwritten gave zeros, and a mesh shader's unwritten vertices (0, 0, 0, 0). A direct task zeroes its payload in `taskout` before the body (25 stores a lane for the grass task's 800 words; `meshlaunch.comp` reads the launch count from it). A direct mesh shader's unwritten vertices keep what the arena held: a vertex no primitive names is never read, and the three Adrenos drew Kokiri Forest the same.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on a OnePlus Pad 3, by ablation of the kernels' parts (the shared array doubled, the zeroing, the output copy, the body and the raw-memory helpers each left out).

### Task and mesh draws in compute form on Qualcomm's driver, which has mesh shaders (Android)

- **Symptom:** on the Adreno 840 with Qualcomm's driver (Galaxy Z Fold 8), Ocarina of Time's distant canopy in Kokiri Forest is torn by white shards with the native `VK_EXT_mesh_shader` path.
- **Cause:** the foliage is drawn twice by different mesh shaders: a depth prepass (depth LESS with writes), then a colour pass (depth EQUAL without writes). Through the 840's mesh pipeline, the colour pass lands behind its own prepass depth, so EQUAL fails and the sky fill shows through.
  - **The gap is real displacement:** thousands of depth steps. LEQUAL with a bias of −4096 still leaves shards, and only ALWAYS hides most of it.
  - **Ruled out as the cause:**
    - the position code (structurally identical in both shaders);
    - the inputs (the same storage buffer, route and data, and the same constant buffers);
    - float contraction (every Fma split into FMul + FAdd with NoContraction);
    - subgroup width (`spv_wide` on task and mesh);
    - raw memory timing (`DM_VK_RAW_GMEM_SYNC=1`, `DM_VK_INPLACE=0`);
    - triangle vertex order;
    - pipeline state (shared with the vertex path).
  - **The same kernels draw clean in compute form**, so the fault is in the native mesh path: Qualcomm's driver, or something only the native wrapper does. Which one is open.
- **Fix:** on Qualcomm's own driver, task and mesh draws run in compute form ([on a GPU without mesh shaders](#task-and-mesh-draws-in-compute-form-on-a-gpu-without-mesh-shaders-android)) even though the driver has `VK_EXT_mesh_shader`.
  - **Scope:** other drivers keep the native path; Turnip and the Adreno 7xx and 830 drivers have none.
  - **Switches:** `DM_VK_MESH=1` takes the native path again (for an A/B); `DM_VK_MESH=0` turns it off on every GPU.
  - **The log** says which path is used: `task / mesh shaders: Qualcomm's driver has VK_EXT_mesh_shader, but its draws run in compute form`.
- **Cost:** none measured. Fold 8, Kokiri at the save's opening view, each run started at thermal status 0, last 60 s:

  | | native | compute form |
  |---|---|---|
  | fps | 17.2 | 17.3 |
  | GPU p50 | 44.7 ms | 43.9 ms |
  | frame p90 | 64.6 ms | 62.0 ms |
  | submit CPU p50 | 30.2 ms | 32.6 ms |

- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on a Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840, Qualcomm's driver).

### The GPU clock held up by a game setting, on Qualcomm's kernel driver (Android)

- **Symptom:** a game the replay keeps CPU-bound between chunks runs below what its GPU could give: the Adreno's clock stays at a third to a half of its highest while the GPU is 75–85 % busy, and the frame step holds a longer frame than the GPU needs at a higher clock. Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest on a Galaxy Z Fold 8 ran at 460–726 of 1,300 MHz, 21 fps at hold 3.
- **Cause:** the governor sizes the clock to the GPU's busy share, and a GPU that waits for the replay between chunks never looks busy enough for the next step. The pipeline fixes behind it (the dirty tracker's walk, the indirect dispatches' drains, the frame step's probes; [A probe to the next shorter hold](frame-rate.md#a-probe-to-the-next-shorter-hold-on-a-gpu-governor-that-follows-the-busy-share-android)) let the Galaxy S25 Edge's governor ramp; the Fold's still did not.
- **Fix:** a game setting, off by default: the launcher's **Keep the GPU clock up**, offered for every game on a phone with `/dev/kgsl-3d0` (Qualcomm's kernel GPU driver, the same test `vk_driver.c` makes before loading Turnip; a Mali, Xclipse or PowerVR phone has no such node, and doesn't show it). On, it writes `DM_VK_GPU_FLOOR=100` to the game's `launcher.txt`, and the host puts a KGSL power constraint (`KGSL_PROP_PWR_CONSTRAINT`, level `KGSL_CONSTRAINT_PWR_MAX`) on the Vulkan device's own contexts: the ones this process's `/dev/kgsl-3d0` descriptors own after `vkCreateDevice` and did not before, found by the same call with `KGSL_CONSTRAINT_NONE` (`gfx_vk_floor.inc`, the rules in `gpu_floor.h`, `dm/tools/tests/test_gpu_floor.py`). It is sent again every 30 presents, since the GMU holds a context's rule only while the context is registered with it. `=2..99` in `env.txt` asks for a floor at that share of the highest clock, which a GMU-decided clock takes (the Fold's 840) and a kernel-decided one answers with EINVAL (the S25's 830: the highest clock is sent instead). Off or unset, no call is made. The log says once whether it was taken: `[vk] GPU clock floor (DM_VK_GPU_FLOOR=100): taken on the device's KGSL context 19 (the GMU decides the clock: …)`, a `[RED]` line when the kernel refuses it, and the stats line counts the constraints sent. No root is needed: the constraint is the process's own contexts'. Neither the S25's nor the Fold's driver lists `VK_QCOM_queue_perf_hint`, the other way Banana Native's `vka_dev.c` (MIT) takes, which this follows.
- **Measured** (Ocarina of Time, Kokiri Forest, main's OoT defaults):
  - **Galaxy Z Fold 8, a cool phone** (battery 31 °C, thermal status 2 from the day's runs; 15 minutes each by hand at the game's own frame step, the floor first): 26–29.5 fps a minute against 21, hold 2 held, the clock pinned at 1,300 MHz; the battery 1.0 °C warmer at the end against 1.2 °C cooler for the control.
  - **The same Fold warm** (battery 33 °C, the bench's `kokiri` route, about 2 minutes a run, adjacent runs in the order off, on, on, off, a floor of 60): off 19.9 and 19.9 fps (GPU p50 39.5 and 39.7 ms, the clock's median 578 MHz, 160–726); on 17.8 and 17.6 fps (GPU 48.6 and 49.1 ms, median 461 MHz); `=60` 20.0 fps (GPU 39.6 ms). On, the GPU ran at 1,300 MHz for 5–15 s and heated to 68–80 °C, and then the thermal engine cut its highest clock to 461 MHz (`thermal_pwrlevel` 11) for 13–40 s at a time, below the 578–646 MHz the governor picks alone; off, the thermal limit came down in steps (1,050 to 646 MHz) and never reached the clock in use. `=60` held 826 MHz until a limit of 578 (level 9) took over. The thermal status reached 2 in each run with the floor, 1 without it.
  - **Galaxy S25 Edge** (15 minutes each, after the pipeline fixes): no gain, 25 fps pinned at 1,200 MHz against 26.5 with its governor at 525–832 MHz, the battery 0.6 °C warmer.
- **So** it stays the player's choice per game, never a default: it can buy a game a shorter frame while the phone has thermal headroom and costs frames once the thermal engine answers the heat with a harder limit. The setting's text says both, and that the phone runs warmer and uses more battery.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on a Galaxy Z Fold 8 (Adreno 840) and a Galaxy S25 Edge (Adreno 830), Qualcomm's driver.
